Counts of the run of seed 1 (M7a step 2e, with the memory change of *Orders a unit did not take*): garrison orders 37 (6 occupants at most inside at once), engineer 6 (4 buildings captured), spy 6 (6 infiltrations), tech building 4 (income cycles 2343), jumpjet 34 (a jumpjet in the air for 8688 ticks; 7 built jumpjet units rose from their barracks), deploy 185 (6 changes of form), radiation exits 16 (radiation on the map for 2445 ticks), erasure 88 (6 begun), prism 8 (28 support beams), charger 15, bomb planter 102 (11 bombs planted), disarm 9, suicide 44, sabotage 14, air strike 9 (10 called), crate 130 (7 picked up). At step 2b, before that change, it gave erasure 280, charger 131, bomb planter 289 and suicide 254 orders. At M6c step 5 the same seed gave engineer 2, spy 2, tech 2, radiation exits 2, 1 erasure begun and disarm 4.
- **Margins (M7a step 2b).** With the M6c step 5 scenario the sweep of seeds 1 to 16 gave these minimum / median per counter: garrison orders 16/23, occupants 2/3, engineer 2/2, captures 2/2, spy 2/2, infiltrations 2/2, tech 2/2, income cycles 1176/1178, jumpjet orders 17/36, flight ticks 10492/11983, built jumpjets 2/3, deploy orders 23/56, changes of form 4/4, radiation exits 1/9, radiation ticks 1800/3833, erasure orders 79/264, erasures begun 0/1, prism 6/6, charger 24/247, support beams 12/25, bomb planter 79/238, bombs planted 0/1, disarm 0/1, suicide 72/185, sabotage 4/15, air-strike orders 0/2, strikes called 3/4, crate orders 61/100, crates picked up 4/8; seeds 3, 4, 7, 9, 11, 12, 13 and 15 each had a counter at zero (they would fail the gate). The setup changes above (more engineers, spies, occupiers, erasers, bomb planters, disarmers, deployers and designators at the start; a second DERR and SPYB per side; the HULKs; no starting jumpjet unit and a second buildable jumpjet type, because the army builder takes the type with fewest on the map and a surviving starting jumpjet kept the barracks from building more) give, over seeds 1 to 16: garrison orders 12/26, occupants 1/4, engineer 6/6, captures 3/4, spy 5/6, infiltrations 5/6, tech 4/4, income cycles 1765/2342, jumpjet orders 22/49, flight ticks 8688/8688, built jumpjets 4/7, deploy orders 30/81, changes of form 3/6, radiation exits 5/23, radiation ticks 2445/4463, erasure orders 22/249, erasures begun 3/7, prism 6/6, charger 3/204, support beams 13/31, bomb planter 32/242, bombs planted 7/32, disarm 6/11, suicide 15/144, sabotage 3/13, air-strike orders 7/9, strikes called 6/9, crate orders 57/121, crates picked up 3/8. Every counter's minimum is at least 3 except the most occupants at once (1, seed 10 only; median 4). Over seeds 1 to 32 no counter is ever zero; four seeds have one counter below 3 (10: occupants 1, 18: crates picked up 1, 26: jumpjet orders and built jumpjets 2, 28: sabotage orders 2). The AI was not changed for this: no choice of the AI is forced or scripted, the map and start units only give each ability an occasion. Further tuning was tried and dropped (six occupiers instead of four moved the low counters to other seeds: crates picked up 0 in seed 2; frail jumpjets and higher start credits did not help). After the memory change of step 2e (fewer repeated orders, so the games go differently), seeds 1 to 16 give: garrison orders 10/24, occupants 1/4, engineer 6/6, captures 3/4, spy 5/6, infiltrations 5/6, tech 4/4, income cycles 1765/2342, jumpjet orders 27/46, flight ticks 8688/8688, built jumpjets 3/7, deploy orders 30/85, changes of form 3/6, radiation exits 0/16, radiation ticks 2445/4471, erasure orders 45/109, erasures begun 3/7, prism 6/6, charger 13/27, support beams 18/30, bomb planter 49/109, bombs planted 7/32, disarm 6/11, suicide 26/66, sabotage 3/11, air-strike orders 7/9, strikes called 6/8, crate orders 56/121, crates picked up 3/7. Seed 1, the gate's, still has smallest counter 4; over seeds 1 to 32 one seed has a counter at zero (13: radiation exits; it would fail the gate) and eight have one below 3 (4: radiation exits 2; 10: occupants 1; 18 and 32: crates picked up 2; 20 and 22: crates picked up 1; 26: jumpjet orders and built jumpjets 2; 28: sabotage orders 2).
- **Save and load, replay.** The run saves and loads the game at the halfway tick and at the first tick each kind of M6c state exists (garrison occupants, a jumpjet in the air, radiation, an erasure, a planted bomb, an air strike, a crate); the loaded game must hash alike and the run goes on from it. The test above also replays the finished game (`Game::run_replay`: setup and the persons' orders, the AI runs again) and requires the same final state hash.
- **Not exercised as an event.** The *orders* for the engineer and spy sidebar rules (a house that has none orders one) are not used: ENGI and SPYU are not buildable here (their counters count the orders to the starting ones); the sidebar orders are covered by `crates/deterministic/ai/tests/special.rs`. A disarm *order* happens but the disarm event itself is not counted separately, nor are suicide and sabotage hits (their orders are).
- **Findings from building the scenario (AI), fixed at the merge.** (1) A crate that landed on a water cell was sent a ground unit all the same (`glasnost_ai::crates::same_region` let a goal outside every reach region pass), which then kept its *move* behaviour for ever without moving; now such a goal rules the unit out (test `a_crate_on_water_sends_no_walker`). (2) The prism order was given at every think until three prisms stood or were queued; it now waits for a free building line (test `no_prism_order_while_the_building_line_is_busy`). Erasure, bomb and charger orders came by the hundred in a long game; most were the same unit re-ordered at the same goal (fixed at M7a step 2e, see *Orders a unit did not take*). The counters count orders given, not units used. (3) Starting units next to a deployed radiation-immune unit leave its field (that is what the radiation counter shows).

The M7a duel (`duel-m7a`, M7a step 4). A fifth duel, `golden/duel-m7a.txt` (`Scenario::DuelM7a`, index 7 of `Scenario::ALL` and of the node list in `xtask/src/gate.rs`; `crates/sim-gate/src/m7a.rs`), plays the own AI against itself on the synthetic ruleset and map of `glasnost_ai::fixture_m7a` (the fixture's ruleset plus invented types; stock values nowhere): 12 000 ticks (`M7A_TICKS`), every 10th tick's hash, seed `M7A_SEED` = 3 (1 until M7a step 3.3, 2 until M7a step 1g, when Alpha won seed 2 at tick 10 314; kept at M7a step 3.6, where seed 3 still plays the full run with the grinder and Alpha's hulks). Alpha plays a Yuri-style side and Beta a fixture side: Alpha has no refinery or harvester of the fixture but a slave miner (SMV, three SLAVE walkers, built at the war factory) whose deployed form SMB is also its sidebar refinery (S-0402 claim 14; the required-country lists of S-0407 claim 5 (b) keep each economy to its side, the owner lists stay shared because the factory test of claim 11 reads them). Both sides have the berserk rifleman MADMAN (weapon MADRAY, warhead MAD with the berserk flag; six per house at the start, two cells apart, and the barracks builds more), the psychic sensor EYE (radius 12, built by the AI) and MINDB, which gives the psychic reveal REVEAL (buildable once a war factory stands; a skirmish drops pre-placed buildings of a player's house, S-0009 claim 27). Since M7a step 3.3 Alpha alone (required-country lists) also has the gattling vehicle GATV (six stage slots holding SPIN0 to SPIN2 of damage 6, 9 and 12; 3 stages, spin-up 4, spin-down 2, thresholds 20, 60, 120; built at the war factory as an army type), the gattling defence GATB (same slots and settings, built by the AI's defence rule) and the mind controller TAKER (a rifleman whose weapon THRALL has the takeover warhead MIND, capacity 4, range 2, cost 200); its victims are Beta's units. The lobby gives 15 000 credits (`fixture_m7a::CREDITS`), which is what lets both houses reach MINDB within the run: with the fixture's credits no seed fired a reveal. `m7a::play` saves and loads at the halfway tick and at the first tick each kind of M7a state appears (a reveal fired, a berserk unit, a hostile-intent line seen through a sensor, a slave-miner building, a slave at work, a gattling at its top stage, a gattling building spun up, a controlled object); the full gate run (native and wasm32) and `every_m7a_ability_is_exercised_and_the_replay_reproduces_the_game` (`crates/sim-gate/tests/m7a.rs`) assert that `Exercise::missing` is empty and replay the game to the same final hash. Counters (`Exercise::counters`) and their minimum / median over seeds 1 to 16 (`GLASNOST_SWEEP=1-16 cargo test --release -p glasnost-sim-gate --test m7a m7a_seed_sweep -- --ignored --nocapture`), measured at M7a step 3.3 after merging the late start of M7a step 1b: berserk weapon orders 3/21, units made berserk 20/34, psychic sensor orders 2/2, ticks with a hostile-intent line seen by a sensor 1982/3517, psychic reveal orders 6/12, reveals fired 6/12, slave-miner buildings placed or deployed 5/8, ticks with a slave at work 6312/8073, gattling stage rises 9/15, ticks with a gattling at its top stage 1396/2954, takeover orders 6/15, takeovers 10/15. Alpha wins 7 of the 16 games before 12 000 ticks (seeds 4, 5, 7, 11, 12, 15 and 16; before step 3.3 every seed ran the full run, with stronger gattling weapons or a larger controller capacity Alpha won all 16, and with a controller range of 3 or a price of 300 some seed gave only 1 or 2 takeover orders), and seed 1 also ended early with an earlier tuning, so the seed moved to 2, which plays the full run in every tuning tried. Seed 2: berserk orders 23 (16 and 7), units made berserk 53, sensor orders 2, intent-line ticks 3 517, reveal orders 12 (6 and 6), reveals fired 12, slave-miner buildings 7, slave ticks 8 513, gattling stage rises 28, top-stage ticks 2 846, takeover orders 11 (all Alpha), takeovers 10, gattling defence orders 2 and kept spins 337 (Alpha). `Exercise::building_spin_ticks` (ticks with a gattling building spun up) is kept but not asserted: Alpha builds its two defences, but in most seeds no enemy reaches them within the run (0 in seed 2). **The sensor orders are 2 in every seed, below the margin of 3:** the AI orders a sensor only while its house has none (`psychic::build_sensor`), so two houses give two unless one is lost and built again. No setup tried moved it (a sensor of strength 1, starts 22 cells apart instead of 32, 16 000 ticks; with the closer starts games ended before the full run, and a house that lost its sensor at tick 10 451 had not ordered another by 12 000 while it held 850 to 1 900 credits; why was not traced). The counter stays, so that a change which drops the sensor from the game fails the gate; the sensor's margin is in the intent-line counter, which only a built sensor can raise. **Adding a type and a counter:** (1) insert the invented type in `fixture_m7a::ruleset` (with `register`; a side's own type gets that side's required-country list); (2) for a start unit, add its name to `fixture_m7a::INFANTRY` or `VEHICLES`; (3) add a field to `m7a::Exercise`; (4) count it in `Exercise::observe` (a new set in `Watch` if it is a beginning, as `berserk`), and call `watch.first(present, "name")` there if its first appearance deserves a save and load; (5) add its line to `Exercise::counters` and raise the array's length; (6) run the sweep above (every minimum at least 3, the median well above) and then regenerate the golden with `GLASNOST_UPDATE_GOLDEN=1 cargo test --release -p glasnost-sim-gate -- --include-ignored`, which must change only `duel-m7a.txt`.

The headless runner: `glasnost-headless synthetic [ticks] [seed]` prints the per-tick hashes of the synthetic replay, `glasnost-headless replay <file>` those of a replay file. The local lane: `GLASNOST_GAME_DIR=<install> cargo run --release -p glasnost-tools --bin glasnost-headless -- smoke [ticks] [seed] [index]` loads every map of the install in the sandbox setup, orders every mobile unit to another unit's cell at tick 4, runs the ticks and writes counts and timings (no names, no values) to `local-lane/sim-smoke.txt` (with each map's shots, detonations, kills and most projectiles in flight). A skirmish pass also sets each map up as a skirmish of the tool's design (`headless::skirmish_setup`: as many players as the lobby allows, all on random positions, the neutral and special houses, unit count 10, bases on), loads it without running it and counts the maps loaded and refused (with the reason) and the starting units created. The scripted skirmish (M5a): `GLASNOST_GAME_DIR=<install> cargo run --release -p glasnost-tools --bin glasnost-headless -- skirmish [ticks] [seed] [index …]` sets up the first two stock maps with two start positions (or the listed map indices) as skirmishes of two AI houses (`econ::setup::skirmish`, default settings, the first two open countries that own a harvester type the scripts can use, else the first two open countries; the scripts use only harvester and army types the simulation moves: a vehicle or infantry unit with the teleporting locomotor jumps by S-0207 claim 20 and S-0208 claim 19; the scripts use no aircraft), lets `glasnost_ai`'s script play both through orders for at most `ticks` ticks (default 120000, a ceiling; with seed 7 the two default stock maps end after some 11000 and 17000 ticks) or until the game is over, and writes to `local-lane/skirmish.txt` the rules' type counts by the script's roles, the players' country indices, the end tick and the winner's house index, the order count, the objects each house had by role, the ticks in which its credits rose, the credits it gained, its harvesters' ticks by current behaviour and harvest phase, its credits at the end and the types it could build then by role, the game's shots and kills, and whether the replay without the scripts and a save in mid-game, loaded and continued, reproduce the final state.

Provisional rules of the simulation, each tied to a request:

| Where | Interim rule | Request |
|---|---|---|
| `load` | not implemented yet: the removal of a removed house's triggers by the unused-house cleanup (S-0009 claim 28; map triggers are not implemented), header pairs as start position marks (claim 29; the importer keeps an unread component of pairs 9 … 16 unread, for the mark in force at each phase to complete), observers (claim 22), the tags of cell tags and of pre-placed objects with their draws (S-0011 claim 34), wall buildings (S-0112 Terms), a departing wall tower's wall hits (S-0112 claim 7, S-0303 F4), terrain object types that only the map names (S-0009 claim 42 creates them with health −1; our engine places nothing there, with a note), and the bridge-body mark that a destroyed high bridge sets (S-0010 claim 25: bridges are not destroyed yet) | — |
| `objects` | pre-placed objects (S-0009 claims 34 to 43) carry no veterancy value or tag (a vehicle with the bridge flag stands on the deck where its cell has one, else on the ground), start switched on, and aircraft stand landed; follower cars carry their leader (claim 41) but are not sent after it (S-0212 claim 26 is a seam) | — |
| `process` (route row, a seam) | routes are not built. When they are, S-0215 revision 39, claim 13 applies: under our owner-routes departure both repair and reload route-point alliance guards and the non-allied fallback test use the actor owner's house; the simulation has no machine-local house | Q-0663 (answered; not built) |

## Simulation (M4: combat)

Combat lives in `glasnost_sim::combat`, driven from the tick pipeline and the behaviours of M3; every phase runs in S-0203's places (no component picks its own order). Its rules are typed in `combat::data` (`CombatRules`, built inside `SimRules` from the schema's combat fields: weapons, projectiles, warheads with their versus values read at double precision, per-type `TypeCombat`, countries' multipliers, the general values and the difficulty rows). The quirk switches the combat specs name are in `rules::Quirks` (236 switches now, each defaulting to the original), plus `clear_weapons_any_turret_count` and `coefficient_zero_replaced` (importer, `convert::sim::SimQuirks`) and `percent_list_integer_prefix` (`ini::Quirks`); the quirk inventory test checks every switch the simulation, movement, infantry, rendering and combat specs propose.

- **Firing (S-0301, S-0310, S-0313).** `check` is the firing check in S-0301 claim 9's rank order, with S-0310's aiming answers (turret and body facing, the building aim point); `fire` makes a shot (burst counter, the delay kinds and their draws, ammunition and reloading, firing-frame sync, the full delay F1 and the reload F3), `position` the fire point (the 3 × 4 single-precision matrices of S-0313 F1, burst alternation, building pixel shifts F3), `behave` the firing parts of the behaviours (vehicles S-0310 claim 9, infantry S-0107 claim 11, buildings S-0215 claim 32 with the turret snap), and `ballistics` the arc speed and reachability.
- **Weapons and acquisition (S-0308, S-0309).** `weapon` chooses the slot by table P (with table N, gattling and switching types, elite slots), the mean damage of a healer for acquisition and the versus lookup (S-0304, S-0305); `acquire` holds the searches (ring order F2, candidates by table C, threat F4 to F9, early stops, the timer draw first), retaliation, anger, expected remaining health and the per-tick search after an actor's processing, area guard and hunt.
- **Projectiles (S-0306, S-0307).** `projectile` launches (straight and arcing pitch, scatter F5), flies (turning F7, steering F10 to F12, the rows of tables T1 and T2), detonates (detonation point, cluster draws, airburst children) and removes shots; projectiles are kept in `World::projectiles` in update order, with IDs from the object counter. `range` is the range test (elevation bonus F2, measured and source points, the high-bridge refusals) and `lof` the line-of-fire walk.
- **Damage (S-0302, S-0303, S-0304, S-0305).** `damage::apply` runs S-0302's stage table (F1 to F3, F8, thresholds, readiness loss, damage effects and building fires, reactions: anger, fear and retaliation, the killing hit with delayed kills and credit); `area::detonate` the area damage (ring metric, radii, wall hits, exploding overlays).
- **Death, veterancy, healing, suppression (S-0311, S-0312, S-0314, S-0315).** `death` removes killed objects (nearby point F2, debris, death weapons, infantry death sequences, crew and survivors, building destruction, terrain objects), `veterancy` ranks and abilities (and the experience a pre-placed object starts with), `heal` self-healing, `fear` suppression and panic.
- **Effects.** `combat::effects` lists the cosmetic events of a tick (animations, detonations, shots, deaths) in `World::effects`, which is not state (it is skipped by saves and hashes); the viewer draws them.
- **Repair steps (S-0314).** `heal::repair_step`, `step_size`, `step_cost` (F2 with our engine's departure) and `building_repair_step` are the step rules; nothing asks for a step yet (depots, hospitals and the repair order wait for the structures and economy topics), and a building's step cost is F2 on S-0408's adjusted cost (`seams::building_adjusted_cost`, which calls `econ::sell::adjusted_cost`).
- **Input rules.** S-0214's armed, healing actor, claim 6 (d) and (e), rows I16, I21, GC4, AC8, ACC, BC3, BC4, k1, table R and the idle order read the weapon choice, the range test, the firing check's legality answer and veterancy abilities. Row I8's *can garrison* (S-0214 rev 29) is S-0508 claim 1's occupation test, `targeting::may_garrison`: garrisonable type, occupier actor, the building's owner the actor's own house or a passive house (alliance plays no part), not built up or sold, centre in the usable area, not being erased, occupant count not equal to capacity, not both positive health and badly damaged, actor not mind-controlled (tests in `claims_s0214_garrison.rs`). Occupants are `structures::garrison` (M6c, section *Simulation (M6c: garrisons)*); erasure and mind control are seams that answer no.
- **Seams.** Not modelled yet, each left where its topic plugs in: bunkers, transports and open-topped passengers, cloaking and disguise, berserk, mind control, spawned aircraft and flight (`weapon::in_air` is false), parasites, crates, bridges (deck damage), smudges, particles, animation playback (a weapon that waits for an animation detonates at once), sides (no survivors are created; the crew type is the technician), the production cost factors (1.0), the map's inert and harvester-immune flags, turret-locked weapons (not imported), the weapon cursor flags of S-0214 rows I17 and I18, and economy, production, power and shroud (S-04xx, M5). Where a behaviour or combat rule asks an M5a topic (slave miners and the harvesters' guard rules of S-0402, available credits and payment of S-0404, short of power of S-0405, adjusted cost and refund of S-0408, the defeated state of S-0414), it calls `glasnost_sim::seams`. The harvesters' guard rule, short of power, available credits, payment, the adjusted cost, the refund and the defeated state are connected to their M5a topics; the slave group's parts answer from `econ::slaves` (S-0402 claims 14 and 19 to 28).
- **Claim ledgers.** `docs/m4-claims/S-03xx.md` lists every claim of each combat spec as tested, seam, partly or not implemented, with the tests that check it (`crates/deterministic/sim/tests/claims_s03*.rs`, each citing `// spec: S-xxxx claim N`). `testkit::combat_ruleset` and `testkit::game_with` give the invented combat ruleset and an empty-map game for such tests.

Q-0236 is answered by S-0312 claim 16: a killed aircraft with an empty explosion list shows no animation and takes no draw (`combat::death::kill`).

Q-0230 and Q-0237 are answered by S-0307 claims 21 and 25: an outside-area alternative fails a non-winged actor's clear-cell region test, a *winged* actor's clear-cell test accepts without the occupancy, region, land and wall tests (`combat::range::approach_search`), and after the close-range shortcut an actor of any locomotor but walking gets only the destination (`approach::shortcut`).

Q-0661 is answered by S-0610 revision 3, claim 1: a psychic-weapon or psionics immunity that no rules layer gives is *yes* for building types and *no* for infantry, vehicle and aircraft types (an initial default, not a reset by a later layer that omits the key). `building_rules` in `glasnost_sim::rules` applies it, so an otherwise damageable building ignores a psychic-damage hit unless its data turns the immunity off (`claims_s0302::s0610_claim_1_psychic_immunity_defaults_by_kind`). The psionics immunity's other consumers (the berserk-weapon firing check of S-0610 claim 3 and the control effects of S-0601) are not built; berserk hits already exclude buildings by S-0302 claim 10.

Q-0662 is answered by S-0613 revision 3, claim 1 and its Edge cases: a rules set without the shared gas system type is not rejected and draws no warning; the type is created at game start and reads no section, as S-0004 claim 3 says, which is what `convert::layers` (`finish`) already did (`refs_tests::game_start_particle_system`). The created type's values (lifetime −1, no behaviour kind, no held particle type) are for the gas clouds of S-0613, which are not built.

Provisional rules of combat, each tied to a request:

| Where | Interim rule | Request |
|---|---|---|
| `combat::range::approach_search` | the two edges S-0307 leaves open: a vehicle alternative off the map's legal cells never passes the clear-cell test; claim 22 (e) withdraws a close-range attacker's own entry on a copy of the cell's record for the clear-cell test, so the order of the cell's occupants never changes | Q-0172 |
| `combat::damage` (berserk hit), `combat::fire`, `combat::acquire::friendly_exception`, `targeting::berserk` | the berserk code predates S-0610 (now revision 3) and follows only S-0302 claim 10 and F3: a berserk hit sets `berserk_until` = now + *T*, read by the halved rearm and the friendly-target exception; `targeting::berserk` is still a seam (always false). Building types now default to psionics immunity (S-0610 claim 1, Q-0661), but buildings were already excluded from berserk hits. Not built yet (S-0610 is wave 2): the firing refusals of berserk weapons (claim 3), dropping the target and queuing *hunt* on going berserk (claim 4), the countdown in the unit's own updates and the *guard* or *hunt* at expiry (claim 5), the order block and other while-berserk rules (claim 6), including the exemption from S-0215 claim 33a's clearing of allied targets by computer-owned units (that rule is not built either); a hit with *T* ≤ 0 has no effect, which is the off state of `quirks.berserk_zero_duration` rather than its default | none (S-0610, wave 2) |
| (garrisons) | retired by M6c: the garrison occupants, the shared index and its resets are built (section *Simulation (M6c: garrisons)*); the open part, a dead occupant among the firing choices, is Q-0970 there | Q-0970 |

S-0301 revision 16, S-0215 revision 44 and S-0406 revision 9 answer Q-0798, and its row is retired. The weapon providers of a building are its upgrade positions 1 through the used count (at most 3), in increasing order, empty positions skipped (`combat::weapon::installed_upgrades`); a count of 0 supplies none, even with types still recorded, and a count above 3 uses the three valid positions. The attack gate applies only with a nonzero count and an occupied position 1, and tests that type only (`combat::weapon::gate_upgrade`, `combat::behave::upgrade_gate`): an empty position 1 bypasses it, and a level-skipped installation in position 2 imposes none. A passing gate makes its primary and secondary attempts at once, also for an animation-delayed building, and starts no delayed shot. A failed gate starts neither an attempt nor a delayed shot; with `quirks.upgrade_attack_weapon_gate` off it takes the ordinary path, delay included. A secondary slot that resolves to no weapon does nothing (`combat::fire::shot` returns before any change or draw). S-0308 claim 12's own text still says "first installed"; S-0406 claim 12's position order is followed, a pending cross-spec change on the spec side. Ledger: `docs/m6-claims/step2-core.md`.

## Simulation (M5a: economy and base building)

- **Power, shroud and radar (S-0405, S-0409, S-0410).** `econ::power` keeps each house's totals and blackout (`HousePower`), starts a pre-placed building switched off for a map flag of 0 only when its type drains or needs power (`map_off_applies`, claim 18), works them out in the house update when an event has made the house due (placement, removal, owner change, switch, health noticed in the building's own turn, blackout start and end), and answers `low_power` and `fraction` (the double that S-0403 claim 5 reads). `in_operation` is the live test of claim 8 (its needs-engineer row reads the saved *manned* state, `BuildingSwitches::manned`, set by `power::owner_changed` at a building's first ownership change and never cleared, S-0515 claim 2; save format version 16) and `firing_answer` is table T1 at S-0301 rank 4 (`combat::check`); the one-off effects (control-centre count, gap field, super-gap end) run in the building's own turn through `econ::events::turn`. `power::switch` applies the power orders. `econ::shroud` keeps per player house the cover count, hostile gap count and marks of table S1 (plus one shared off-map state), applies sights in the objects' turns and at building placement, sets the discovery mark of every house on a placed object's usable cell (S-0409 claim 20), runs the optional regrowth and then the re-cover sweep before the turn pass (regrowth first on a tick where both are due, S-0409 claim 18), gap fields, the spy satellite's reveal and the whole-map re-cover (taken back and re-applied in object order), the reveal on fire (`combat::fire::shot`), area reveals for other specs, `defeat_reveal`, discovery, the point test of S-0110 claim 8 (which the radar map applies at a cell's surface height at its centre, S-0410 claim 6) and the query `explored`. `econ::radar` rechecks claim 1 in the house update after an event (power first, then radar and the spy satellite); `on_defeat` turns radar on for good and reveals the map. `econ::minimap` holds the radar map's pure functions (point grid F1, scale F2, cell at a pixel F3, object pixel F4, a building's pixel set F8 (`building_pixels`), flash F5, terrain mean F6, tile colour F7, overlay table T1, listing, arrivals and `compose`) for the viewer; nothing there is game state. Seams, each a small function in `econ::power` that answers "no" or 0: pulses and locks, being erased or drained, chargers, absorbed passengers, occupants; the superweapon charge hold (claim 15) and the super-gap's deploy behaviour (claim 16); what a disabled vehicle cannot do. `production::power_changed` (S-0405 claim 11) works out every line's step period again; S-0409 claim 11 reads `victory::defeated`. Ledgers: `docs/m5-claims/S-0405.md`, `S-0409.md`, `S-0410.md`.
- **Production, tech tree and the sidebar's rules (S-0403, S-0404, S-0407, S-0411).** `econ::production` holds every house's lines in creation order (`World::econ.lines`), the build time F1 to F7 (`build_time_of`, `period_of`, on the power fraction `power::fraction`, refreshed by `power_changed`, which S-0405's house update calls), the price with cost bonuses, pad term and free unit (the adjusted cost is `econ::sell::adjusted_cost`, S-0408's), available credits and payment (`available_credits`, `pay`, behind `seams::available_credits` and `seams::pay`), pay-as-you-go steps in tick row 6, holds, cancels and refunds, the queue, the choice of the releasing building (primary, else last acquired), a busy or failed release cancelled at once with the refund, and the factory checks of S-0404 claim 8 (called from `econ::events`). An item's object is created at the start (the creation draw of S-0404 claim 11) and stays off the map until it is released or placed. `econ::release` releases a done unit by S-0404 claim 13's rows (ground vehicle factory with the exit displacement and the hand-off to an idle twin, naval factory toward the rally point, infantry factory and cloning building with the door cells and the exit lists of `quirks.foundation_exit_lists`, aircraft factory with the dock or the edge entry and its draw) and makes claim 14's copies at the house's cloning buildings. The way out is S-0417's (ledger `docs/m5-claims/S-0417.md`): a ground factory with the vehicle-factory flag queues *unload* (current at a per-tick start that finds the ready mark set, `quirks.factory_unload_waits_ready_mark`) and gives the released vehicle a contact and tether (`ObjectEcon::exit`) and the factory's rally point as its saved guard spot; `release::process_unload` runs the stages of table S1 (apron clearing at the apron cell `apron`, from the exit list's eleventh pair with `quirks.factory_apron_short_list`, by scatter requests to E's occupants and then its occupied neighbours; the door through `behaviour::Door` on the gate door time; sending out on the 68-point exit track T1, `drive::begin_exit_track`, with tunnelling and teleporting vehicles on a temporary drive, or to F4's point with `quirks.factory_fixed_hover_exit` for the others, which can be a cell of K's own foundation: a vehicle in contact with a building may enter that building's non-blocking cells, S-0209 claim 5 row VP3 in `occupancy::vehicle_outcome`; when F4's cell holds another building, S-0212 claim 24's substitute replaces the vehicle's destination cell in `path::compute` (departure D3's "replaces"), as it does for every unit whose destination cell is substituted, so the move ends there; waiting; closing, which queues *guard*, started by the ready mark as *unload* is). `release::arrival` is the cell-arrival update (from `drive`, `walk`, `hover` and a `teleport` landing): the clear report ends contact and tether, then the run-away reply of claim 8 (harvest, the saved rally as destination, or a scatter) for a vehicle factory. A naval factory takes two processings. An infantry unit is tethered until its first cell arrival. An infantry unit released by a factory with a rally point gets the rally as its guard spot before it is put in play, so the placement fall-back (S-0215 claim 13's guard-spot row) makes the rally its destination; it then walks to its exit cell with *move* queued and the rally kept as its guard spot for the second leg, unless its type is a jumpjet or teleporting one, which heads straight for the rally (claim 10); without a rally point, or with one in its own cell, the release gives it no destination (its later walk-out is S-0417 Open question 3). An infantry factory has max(*d*, 1) contact places, *d* its docking-place count, and its exit search takes a cell only on the free result of the unit's own cell-entry test (S-0417 claim 14). A docked aircraft appears at S-0404 claim 15's pad position (the foundation centre plus the docking displacement of its contact place for a pad or repair building; `DockingOffsetN` imported into `docking_offsets`), and an edge-entering one without a rally point gets *move* forced with the nearby-cell search's result around the factory's reference cell (S-0417 claim 14). A docked aircraft gets contact and an exit tether when its type need not return to a dock (claim 11; the tether ends on take-off, S-0501 claim 8 in `air`). An idle vehicle factory in guard without the stupid-guard-mode flag makes no apron test at the first processing after the behaviour starts and sends claim 4's clearing requests at every later one, before its delay draw (`release::idle_apron`, claims 12 and 13). `release::holds_back` adds S-0215 claim 11's vehicle-factory exceptions to the readiness test, and `orders` ignores stop, scatter and deploy for a tethered unit and keeps the link on behaviour orders (S-0213 claims 8 and 9, T3 b). `econ::tech` gives the tech-tree answer (override, secret type, tech level, country lists with the index wrap, stolen tech, superweapon setting, prerequisites with groups and upgrades, build limits), the present, owned and ever-built counts, the taken-up set (which is also the icon set) and its rechecks at the house update (`tech::house_update`; a recheck returns the types it removed), the queue-time limit and `assign_secret_labs` for S-0412's game start. `econ::sidebar` has the click tables of S-0411 claims 4 and 5 as pure functions (`click`), the icon states, the legality of production orders (`legal`, which `orders::issue` applies to every house), and, at every player house's update, the cancel and cancel-all orders of S-0411 claim 2 and S-0404 claim 9 for the types a recheck removed (`removal_orders`; the start of a sale removes nothing) and the automatic release order of claim 9. Darkening (claim 3) and the at-limit tests use the build-limit answer with the production allowance (`tech::at_limit`). A build order, the queue's forced start and the factory check of S-0404 claim 8 use the limit-only test `tech::could_make_limit_only` (S-0407 claim 13, S-0404 claims 3, 8 and 10), so a prerequisite loss alone neither cancels nor drops an item; a factory building queued to be sold takes no start, queue request, resume or release (S-0411 claim 14). Each player house keeps its icons per tab in S-0004 claim 11's insertion order (`HouseTech::icons`; the new offers of one recheck in S-0407 claim 17's order: kinds by the acquisition of their earliest still-owned qualifying factory, then registration index), and the removal orders follow the tab order and then that icon order (S-0411 claim 13). The superweapon setting (`setup::superweapons_allowed`) and don't-score vehicle entries (`victory::unscored_vehicle_entries`) come from S-0412 and S-0414. A free dock (`production::dock_free`) is a contact place not held by a released or reserving aircraft (S-0501 claim 23), and the dock-bound queue test uses the house's pad total (S-0407 claim 16, S-0501 claim 30). Seams: vehicle-thief take-overs and infiltration (`tech::infiltrated`, for the special-abilities specs). Ledgers: `docs/m5-claims/S-0403.md`, `S-0404.md`, `S-0407.md`, `S-0411.md`, `S-0417.md`.
- **Placement, selling, repair, deploy and pack-up (S-0406, S-0408, S-0415).** `econ::placement` has the cell test (`cell_test` with a `Test`: the placing house, whose walls the exceptions of claim 8 accept, whether place-anywhere counts, and an object to ignore), the foundation cells (the 8-cell 3 × 3 word), adjacency (F1), explored ground, `place_legal` (what `orders::issue` asks of a place order with a cell), the input helpers `click_cell` and `cursor_shroud_result`, and `place_building`, which `production::place` calls: the factory condition of claim 1, then either an upgrade (`upgrade_target`, `add_upgrade`, F5) or the cell test and `put_on_map`. `put_on_map_facing` is the one core for a place order, a deploy and a filling wall: walls underneath removed by the silent wall sale (claim 9), a wall type turned into its overlay (claim 10), otherwise the building put at the cell centre at the surface height in its construction state (`begin_construction`; `objects::place_with` passes `by_order` to the events), then the wall fill for a place order (claim 11). The construction behaviour (`deploy::process_construction`) and the selling behaviour (`sell::process_selling`) are dispatched from `process.rs` and run every tick with no draw. *Construction* is queued and starts at the ready mark: a deployed building has the mark when it enters play, a placed one only once (*n* − 1) × *d_b* ticks (1 for a zero-delay range) have passed since it entered play, so a placed building's build-up runs twice (S-0406 claim 17 and F7, `quirks.placed_buildup_twice`; `placement::construction_turn`, `Construction::entry`). Until then the building's first per-tick start is withheld (`placement::first_start_withheld`), and the tests that ask for its behaviour see the queued *construction* (`placement::seen_behaviour`). Construction records its first processing *C* (`Construction::first`) and hands over at *C* + (*n* − 1) × *d_b*, or *C* + 1 for a zero-delay range (claims 14 and 15, F6; `sell::buildup_range`, S-0108 claim 5, where a gate has *n* = `GateStages` + 1 and *n* is otherwise the frame count of the art's build-up sprite, which the converter imports as `buildup_frames`); a one-stage range with a positive delay never hands over (claim 18, `quirks.single_stage_buildup_never_completes`; the converter warns about such types): the opening effects, then *guard* queued with the ready mark and the target kept. A placed building faces 0 (claim 16), and so does a deployed one (S-0415 claim 6). The sale's build-down ends when (*n* − 1) × *d_b* ticks have passed, with *n* capped at 23 for a building that undeploys into a vehicle and has no guard spot (S-0408 claim 17's early report; `sell::builddown_ticks`); a positive-delay range of 1 stage or fewer never reports under `quirks.single_stage_buildup_never_completes` (the sale stays in build-down, no refund) and reports on its first wrap, *D* + *d_b*, with it off (S-0408 claim 17, which answers Q-0225). `econ::sell` has the refund (F1, on `production::cost_factors`) and main's adjusted cost (F2), sellable and repairable, the sell order (building, upgrade, vehicle on a building cell), the wall sale (`wall_sale`, silent or not), the upgrade sale of S-0408 claim 5 at the upgrade count's position (`BuildingSwitches::upgrades` holds the three positions; a turret upgrade resets the count to 0, any other lowers it by 1; an empty position is the Edge-cases departure), the repair switch and the building's repair step in its own turn (`sell::building_turn`, via `econ::events::turn`, on S-0314's `building_repair_step`). `seams::building_adjusted_cost` and `seams::building_refund` now answer. `econ::deploy` has the vehicle's *unload* by the ranks of S-0415 claim 1 (`process_unload`, ranks 1 and 3 handed back; rank 2, deploy fire, chooses 14 + U(0, 2) ticks its shot and the in-place rank are S-0517, section *Simulation (M6c: deployable units)*), the attempt, refusal, turn and completion (claims 2 to 6, state in `DeployState`, reset by every deploy order), `packs_up` (S-0213 claim 12) and the pack-up's end (`pack_up`, claims 10 to 12: the return cell accepts the new vehicle when its S-0209 entry outcome there is clear, with the packing building gone). Sounds are `Effect::Sound` events (deploy, pack-up, sell, cannot-deploy, unit sold) for the viewer. Seams: the theater-refresh build-up range of S-0108 claim 5 (no theater flag imported yet), the to-tile tileset test (no morphable flag in the catalogue), docked units, absorbed passengers, garrisons and sale survivors (no sides), mind control, deploy-readiness states other than health, map-placed upgrades (still separate objects). A direct call of `place_building` with no done item of the type creates the object (engine callers and tests; orders always come through a done item). Ledgers: `docs/m5-claims/S-0406.md`, `S-0408.md`, `S-0415.md`.
- **Slave miners (S-0402 claims 14 and 19 to 28, F7; S-0312 claims 30 and 31; S-0408 claims 6 and 10a; S-0415 claims 10 and 11; S-0203 claim 12; S-0213 claims 5 and 8, T3 h; S-0209 WP2).** `econ::slaves` keeps a master's group (`ObjectEcon::group`: the slave places in initial creation order, each slave inside or out and ready or not with its delay's start tick; the group state (at rest, field seeking, travelling, retrying, working, checking the field, packing), last-rest tick and next update; the remembered destination of claim 28 is the building's guard spot, which an automatic pack-up sets to the old top-left cell) and each slave's own state (`ObjectEcon::slave`: master, phase, load per tiberium, remembered cell, recalled). `objects::create` makes the initial slaves inside and off the map (one creation draw each). A successful conversion moves the group (`slaves::detach` and `slaves::attach`, from `deploy::pack_up` and the deploy); the conversion does not change the group state. A pack-up first creates the return vehicle with its own hidden group (claim 19a, one creation draw each), then on success ends that temporary group as a removal (`slaves::end_removed`, S-0312 claim 31) before the old group is attached; on refusal the vehicle keeps it (`quirks.failed_packup_keeps_vehicle`), or with the quirk off it ends and the vehicle is removed. The group update (`slaves::group_turn`, every 10 ticks) runs for a building master in `econ::events::turn` after its power step and before its paid repair, and for a vehicle master in `tick::object_turn` before its movement (S-0203 claim 12). It first runs each slave place's effects in place order (claim 26): a replacement, a reload completion, or an outside slave's arrival, full or exhausted load, search and delivery. Then the master's decision: a vehicle's packing (recall, then field seeking), checking (to at rest), field seeking, travelling and deploy attempts (`deploy::attempt`); a building's busy-state table of claim 27 (`building_update`): at rest and working go to checking the field outside construction and selling, checking the field runs the short scan and the long scan with claim 25's (0, 0) for a failed search (`quirks.slave_relocation_failed_search_origin`), F7 and an automatic pack-up through `sell::start_pack_up` that sets the guard spot to the old top-left cell; field seeking runs the long scan and site search and offers the site (to travelling) or rests, and travelling or retrying rests, both recording the last-rest tick. Last, when the decision allows it, emergence at the unloading cell in reverse place order, facing 0 (S-0208 claim 3's centre row, one draw). A deployed master's scans centre on its reference-point cell and need only tiberium land. A slave's *harvest* (`slaves::process_slave`, from `process.rs`) only loads one bale every *h* ticks; a non-resource cell, a full load and a capacity of 0 (or a negative one with `quirks.slave_negative_capacity_stalls` off) queue *guard* with a 1-tick delay. The slave miner vehicle's *harvest* (`slaves::process_harvest`) searches for a site near its destination at every processing, or queues *guard* and rests without one, with the standard delay. The first processing of selling or packing runs claim 28's directed site rule (`slaves::first_sale_processing`, from `sell::process_selling`). `death::kill` hands the slaves of a destroyed master to S-0312 claim 30 (`slaves::master_killed`); a true sale takes S-0408 claim 10a's path (`slaves::master_sold`, from `sell::finish`); a refused pack-up (`slaves::end_removed`) and any other removal (`slaves::removing`) take S-0312 claim 31's; a lost slave starts its place's regeneration (`slaves::lost`). Stop and behaviour orders to a vehicle master recall its slaves (`slaves::recall`). The wake-up rule is `harvest::wake_rule` with its inputs from the world (`harvest::slave_wake_rule_holds`). Tests: `crates/deterministic/sim/tests/claims_s0402_slaves.rs`.
- **Skirmish setup, country bonuses, victory and defeat (S-0412, S-0413, S-0414).** A skirmish is started in three steps: `econ::setup::skirmish(rules, players, settings, unit_count, seed)` builds the `GameSetup` (the players in house order, each a `Player` with name, country, start, AI flag and lobby `HouseValues` (team, handicap, colour, observer), then the neutral and special houses; `Settings` holds credits, short game, superweapons, crates, build off ally, redeploy and the all-base-units mode, each `None` for the rules' default); `setup::resolve_lobby` (optional) settles random countries and colours on its own lobby stream, so the game's stream is untouched; then `load::new_game` (or `Replay::new`) refuses a setup S-0412 forbids (`LoadError::Setup`), creates the houses with their credits, difficulty index and row (`house_values`) and, for every non-sandbox game, runs `setup::start_game` after the starting units: crate count, extra credits, start alliances, wall-owner rewrite, colour fields and secret labs. The accessors `credits_setting`, `short_game`, `superweapons_allowed`, `build_off_ally`, `redeploy`, `observer`, `person_played`, `wall_owner` and `colour_field` answer other topics. Country factors go through their owning specs' seams (`house_combat` with the row, `CountryRules.speed_aircraft` through `setup::aircraft_speed`). `econ::victory` keeps each house's on-map counts (hooks in `econ::events`), runs a pending surrender's destruction and the defeat test in the house update after power and the ordinary radar recheck and before the tech-tree recheck (S-0414 claim 16; tick 1 on, contenders only), defeats a house (every object of the four kinds hit in creation order, off the map and in production included, with the cancellation skip of `quirks.defeat_cancellation_skip`; radar on, map revealed), runs the end test, the results and the end delay, commits the pending result at the start of the houses' part of the last tick (`victory::commit`), and applies the surrender and alliance orders. The outcome is `victory::outcome(&World) -> Option<Outcome>` (`Some` once the game is over, with each player house's `Verdict`); `victory::verdict`, `victory::defeated` and `victory::ended` answer per house and game. Seams: crate placement, superweapon granting, the all-base-units mode (refused), observer positions, aircraft flight, mind control, erasure, passengers and the nominal-survivor condition. Ledgers: `docs/m5-claims/S-0412.md`, `S-0413.md`, `S-0414.md`.
- **Viewer live sandbox (M5a).** The *game on load* option starts either the sandbox game (every owner's objects, as in M3) or a skirmish (`live::skirmish_setup`: one human house and one computer house of the rules' first open countries, no starting units beyond the base units, the fixed sandbox seed), in which `glasnost_ai`'s script plays the computer house through orders every `THINK_PERIOD` ticks. A skirmish load also reads the art of every building, vehicle and infantry type (`build::game_files`), since a game creates types the map does not hold; the game's own buildings are drawn after the cell pass from `Painter::buildings` (`build::LiveBuilding`, damage by S-0108 claim 3). The sidebar is a placeholder layout of our own (`app::mapview::sidebar`, the original-looking one is M5b): credits through S-0411's counter, power output and drain, the radar map (`app::mapview::radar`: S-0410's grid, image, arrivals and `compose` for the controlled house in 5/6/5 bits, black with a "no radar" note while the house has no radar (claim 1), a click jumps the view to the cell of F3; point colours come from the cells' tile radar colours only (claim 9's tile case: the first tile radar colour of the tile cell the shown image variant holds, S-0010 claim 20, also used by the static minimap), and each house's objects take its house display RGB, S-0103 claim 15), tabs by production line kind, cameos with their state (darkened, build stage of 55, ready, on hold, queue count) where a left click and a right click go through `econ::sidebar::click`, and placement: a ready building's cameo enters placement mode, the cursor names the foundation's top-left cell by S-0406 F2 (`placement::click_cell`), the foundation is previewed green or red from `place_legal` and the cell test (or the upgrade target), a left click issues the place order and a right click leaves the mode. The shroud is S-0110's (`app::mapview::cover`, `render::shroud`): in every drawn frame each legal cell whose 60 × 30 canvas meets the view gets its shroud piece from the controlled house's explored states (`econ::shroud::explored`, which answers S-0409 claim 14's shared state for non-legal neighbours), read afresh each frame, so a cell, or the shared state of non-legal neighbours, that a later gap change covers again is shrouded again from the next frame (S-0110 claim 1 and the re-covered-cells and non-legal-neighbour edge cases): 15 when it is not explored, else claim 2's piece for its reduced set of unexplored neighbours (table T1). The pieces are image frames of `SHROUD.SHP` (a missing or unreadable sprite draws no cover, and the load lists a problem; so does a missing `FOG.SHP`), applied by cell in increasing *x* + *y* on the height-0 grid (claim 7, `quirks.shroud_on_flat_grid`; off, each canvas sits at its cell's terrain height) to the view's light-alpha values (127 at the start of each drawn frame, F1: a shroud pixel sets the value unless its index is 254). The pieces are not painted: every drawing shaded through S-0104 claim 15 (`compose::Paint::Lit`, a shade table and ρ; tiles, overlays, smudges, terrain objects, buildings, infantry and vehicles) picks its row from its pixel's value, on the CPU and the GPU (a per-frame `R8Uint` light-alpha texture). Status extras and our placeholders keep their fixed rows and are not darkened (S-0111 claim 2 for health segments and pips). Fog of war is always off in skirmish, so no fog piece is drawn; the fog rules of claims 3, 4 and 10 are in `render::shroud` with tests only. No light-glow images are drawn yet (claim 6). The point test of claim 8 is the simulation's `econ::shroud::point_shrouded`, which keeps the switch's on state; its candidate cells (`point_candidates`) take *u* = trunc(*Z* / 104) for heights below 0 too, with the odd-negative pair of `quirks.shroud_negative_odd_pair` (a simulation switch, default on), and the radar's gap test shares them. The sandbox game and the *shroud* checkbox off draw no cover. `GLASNOST_GAME_DIR=<install> cargo test --release -p glasnost-app --lib snapshot_skirmish_view -- --ignored` writes `local-lane/skirmish-view/start.png` and `after.png` (after `GLASNOST_SNAPSHOT_TICKS` ticks, default 600).

- **Building body range (S-0108 claim 6, S-0215 claims 1 and 11).** `econ::body` follows only the *idle* body range of a building (`AnimIdle` and `Normalized`, imported as `anim_idle` and `normalized`): a building placed at game set-up enters it at once (`set_up`, which also queues *guard* with no current behaviour), a construction handover enters it deferred, and entering construction or a sale leaves it. On each building turn, before the first per-tick start, the stage of a building without a turret advances and wraps, and the ready mark is set when it lands on the last stage, or on every turn of a zero-delay range (`quirks.building_wrap_normalisation`). The other body states (*active*, *aux-1*, *aux-2*, *full*) and what enters them are a seam: the range runs on as *idle* through them.

Q-0500 is answered by S-0402 revision 18, claim 27a: a field-seeking building's successful search offers the shifted site cell (`econ::slaves::offer_site`). A building that is not currently *selling* and is a factory of infantry or vehicles, a cloning building, a repair depot or a construction yard takes it as its guard spot (`takes_site_offer`), the one remembered destination a later pack-up uses; any other building keeps its earlier guard spot or its absence, and no building is given a movement destination. *Move* is requested without an immediate start in every case (current *selling* refuses it under S-0215 claim 7), and a building's *move* processing does nothing for 450 ticks (`process`, S-0215 claim 4 and T1), so the group's return to rest leaves it current. Revision 18's other changes need no code change and are now tested: the exact non-wrapping refinery distance with `quirks.refinery_distance_wraps` off keeps the primary and tie rules, a dump rate of 0 or below pays one tiberium per processing, and refinery selection comes no earlier than the processing after the change to returning.

Provisional rules of M5a, each tied to a request:

| Where | Interim rule | Request |
|---|---|---|
| `combat::death::kill`, `econ::production::release`, `econ::production::place` | a production item killed by a destruction hit (S-0414 claim 15) is only marked killed (no other death effect, no draws) and stays on its line with health 0 until a cancellation deletes it; a release order for it fails and cancels it with the refund, a place order does nothing | Q-0193 |

S-0215 revision 42 answers Q-0241 (claim 1): set-up placement runs the building's grand opening after *guard* is queued, and the opening sets the ready mark (`econ::body::set_up`), so *guard* is current from the building's first per-tick start, armed or not, turreted or not, with or without a map-installed upgrade. The opening's free unit stays suppressed at set-up (S-0402 claim 18). Its row is retired; map-placed defences now guard and fire from their first turn.

S-0215 revision 43, S-0308 revision 11 and S-0301 revision 15: upgrade weapons and the upgrade weapon gate. `combat::weapon::slot_weapon` now takes slot *i* of a building from its first installed upgrade (S-0406's upgrade positions) whose type has an ordinary weapon there, never an upgrade's elite slot, and only then the building's own slot (S-0308 claim 12). A non-SAM building with an installed upgrade fires on *can fire* only when its first installed upgrade passes the gate (`combat::behave::upgrade_gate`: a primary, and both slots the same weapon, a primary burst above 1, or a secondary burst above 1). A passing gate makes the primary attempt and then the secondary attempt with no firing check of its own (S-0301 claim 9's exception). A failed gate keeps the target and *attack*, sets the ready mark and chooses 1 tick, with no attempt (`quirks.upgrade_attack_weapon_gate`, on; off, the ordinary selected-weapon attempt). Upgrades the map places are still separate objects, not installed upgrades (S-0009 claim 40 seam), so neither rule applies to them yet. Tests: `s0308_claim_12_upgrades_supply_weapons` and `s0215_claim_32_*upgrade*`, `s0215_claim_32_*gate*` in `claims_s0301.rs`.

## Simulation (M6a: missiles)

S-0506 (missiles and the spawn controller) is built in `glasnost_sim::spawn` (the controller and its slots), `glasnost_sim::rocket` (rocket flight) and `glasnost_sim::combat::flying` (the flying-object registry of S-0303 claim 6 and F7). Its rules are `SimRules::missiles` (`spawn::MissileRules`: each spawner type's slots, count, regeneration and reload, and the three rocket parameter sets A, B and C from the general section), imported by `glasnost_convert` (spawn keys, rocket sets, the aircraft trailer). The ledger is [docs/m6-claims/S-0506.md](m6-claims/S-0506.md); the tests are in `crates/deterministic/sim/tests/claims_s0506.rs`.

- **Spawn controller (claims 1 to 13).** `objects::create` calls `spawn::created`, which makes the full count of docked slot objects off the map and sets the first pass at creation + 20. `spawn::turn`, from the tick pipeline after the behaviour step, runs a pass every 10 ticks: the slot steps in index order, then the controller step (`spawn::pass`). A spawner weapon's shot (`combat::fire`) launches nothing and calls `spawn::shot`, which sets the pending target. The pass launches docked slots by claim 6's spacing (9 or 20 ticks), places them at the fire point raised by 10 with F2's facing, and releases them onto the rail (`OnRail`, with the rail time of claim 8) once every slot is preparing or empty; the controller then resets and cools down. Death, removal and owner change go through `spawn::killed`, `spawn::removing` and `spawn::owner_changed` (claim 13).
- **Rocket flight (claims 14 to 21, F1 to F9).** A rocket-type object runs `rocket::tick` in place of a locomotor. Its phases are fixing the aim, pause, tilt or raise, ascent, cruise (plain or lazy) and descent. It moves by F1 only into legal cells and stalls at the map edge under the quirk. It detonates at its nose point F9 through `combat::area::detonate`, with the set's damage and warhead (elite ones for an elite launch), and then leaves play. Trailers appear every 3 ticks on the shared timer. A rocket destroyed above the terrain keeps flying (`rocket::keeps_flying_when_destroyed`, called from `combat::death`).
- **Hooks into other specs.**
  - S-0301 claim 9: rank 2 *busy* while a vehicle launches, rank 4 *rearming* via `spawn::any_alive`; `spawn::firing_answer` from `combat::behave`.
  - S-0309 claim 4: `spawn::impossible` keeps the target (`combat::acquire`).
  - S-0303: flying candidates and the half distance in `combat::area`; S-0312: interception through `combat::death`.
  - S-0311 claim 8: an untrainable missile-spawn object's experience goes to its launcher (`combat::veterancy`, `spawn::launcher_of`).
  - S-0214: `targeting::movable` is false while 0 < launching count < *N* (`spawn::partly_launched`).
  - S-0504 claim 3 / S-0207 claim 6: `drive::begin_track` waits while `spawn::track_held`; `spawn::launching_count(w, spawner) -> i32` is public.
  - In the air: `combat::weapon::in_air` answers by flight phase for the set-A and set-B types and by the 208-lepton test for a distinct set-C type (claim 22).
- **Revision 3 (answers Q-0610 to Q-0616).** The raw arctangent of F6 and F7 is S-0204 F7's exact table `glasnost_num::tables::RAW_ATAN`, generated from the tangent recurrence (`tablegen::raw_atan_table`). The phase timer starts in the aim-fixing tick with zero elapsed ticks and runs that tick's update. Raise adds its rate only before expiry, and the update that finds cruise altitude still accelerates (claim 24). A rocket missile is in no cell list (claim 23). A destroyed missile keeps its phase timers (claim 20). A crossing flying object follows the objects already in its new sector (S-0303 claim 11). Pending targets follow claim 25 under `quirks.spawner_pending_target_survives_idle_clear`. No pass drops pending for a targetless spawner, and the pending target's own loss clears it.
- **Revision 6 (claim 25 with S-0309 rev 14 claims 4, 23 and 24 and S-0215 rev 29 claim 12).** Every place that clears an object's own target goes through `spawn::clear_own_target` or `spawn::set_own_target` (an actual change of a nonempty own target to empty requests the pending clear; a nonempty replacement does not). Orders use `spawn::explicit_clear_own_target`, which also records a clear of an already empty target, so that with the switch off it empties pending. A nonempty assignment that S-0309 claim 24 redirects to none uses `spawn::redirected_to_none`, which requests the clear even when the old own target was empty. Dropping an unusable opportunity target requests it even when the same search picks a new target. The attack-move searches that run as for an object with no target (S-0309 claim 23) discard and restore the own target without any request, under either setting. A request is recorded on the controller (`SpawnController::clear_request`) and settled by `spawn::settle_clear` at the start of the spawner's turn, of every pass, of `spawn::shot` and of `spawn::removing`, before anything reads the pending or controller target, so the outcome equals an immediate clear; a clear and restore before a pass still clears. This replaces the earlier per-turn inference (`watch_own_target`). The save format version is 15.
- **Revision 5 (claim 26, Q-0645 answered): the shared kamikaze re-order cycle.** `spawn::kamikaze_orders` runs once per tick from `tick::step`, after resource growth and the planted-bomb seam (row 2) and before the diving-aircraft seam (row 10) and the turn pass (S-0203 claim 1 row 17). The first cycle is due at tick 1 (`SpawnedObjects::default`), and each cycle schedules the next at its own tick + 30, even with no kamikaze. A normal salvo release, or a reset that actually releases a preparing flagged object, replaces the deadline with the release tick + 2; slot clearing enrolls in descending slot index and keeps the deadline. `SpawnedObjects::kamikaze` is a `Vec` in enrollment order (removal keeps the others' order). At each due cycle every enrolled object, rocket missiles included, gets ammunition 1, its release cell as target and *attack* queued without an immediate start, with no draws. A rocket's flight ignores them (its aim is fixed by its first move order and it has no behaviour processing).
- **Claim 24 and S-0614 claim 18 (local turn order).** `tick::object_turn` already runs behaviour processing (with aircraft attack-run shots) before the spawner's launch pass and the parasite event, and the separate infantry and vehicle firing after both; `claim_24_vehicle_shot_waits_for_next_pass` tests the vehicle case. No test drives an aircraft spawner yet.
- **Seams for other lanes.**
  - S-0501 claims 35 and 36: `spawn::aircraft_launched`, `spawn::aircraft_attack` and `spawn::aircraft_return` now route to `air::carrier_move`, `air::carrier_attack` and `air::carrier_return`, so slot aircraft fly, attack and dock end to end (M6a: aircraft, below).
  - `spawn::owner_changed` has no caller until owner change exists.
  - `spawn::magnet_held` answers false (no magnetic beam).
  - Other seams: EMP and locomotor-warhead resets, building docking points and tunnel heading points, and the set-C take-off animation (no import key names it).
- **Naval stand-in (retired).** `set_b_launcher_attacks_in_play` in `claims_s0506.rs` still uses a two-slot launcher on the driving locomotor. Since S-0504 the ship locomotor moves, and `ship_borne_set_b_launcher_attacks` in `claims_s0504.rs` runs set B from a launcher on the ship locomotor on water, so the stand-in no longer replaces a vessel.

## Simulation (M6a: parasites)

- **Parasites (S-0614).** `glasnost_sim::parasite` holds a unit's parasite record (`ParasiteState` in `Object::parasite`). Only a latching weapon creates one, so saves without parasites are unchanged. `combat::fire::shot` calls `parasite::fired`: the firer leaves the map inside the projectile and the latch block is set. `combat::projectile` calls `parasite::carrier_ended` when the projectile ends, which either latches or fails to latch (claims 3 and 4). A parasite-flag detonation makes no area hit but keeps its animation and debris (S-0303 claim 2, S-0306 claim 21). An attached parasite acts late in its host's turn (`parasite::host_turn`, called from `tick::object_turn` after self-healing, behaviour processing and movement, before firing; claim 18):
  - drone deliveries and the hold (claims 6 to 9, F1);
  - the grappling cycle of table G, with strikes, splashes, wakes and the cull (claims 10 and 11, F3, F5, F6). A culled host starts sinking through `naval::start_sinking`.
- **Leaving.** A parasite leaves its host through these hooks:
  - `parasite::host_hit`, from `combat::damage::apply` (sonic hits, healing and suppression, S-0315 claims 6 and 7);
  - `parasite::repair_step`, from `combat::heal::repair_step`;
  - `parasite::host_jumps`, from `teleport`;
  - `parasite::leaving`, from `objects::take_inside` and `objects::remove` (host loss, claim 15).
- **Held units.** A held unit:
  - does not drive or fly (`tick::object_turn`);
  - is not movable (`targeting`);
  - gets rank 4 *impossible* when it is a mobile spawner, and rank 9 *illegal* when it is an organic firer (`combat::check`);
  - cannot deploy (`econ::deploy::deploy_ready`);
  - drops its destination when it is a teleporter.

  A host never retaliates against its own parasite (`combat::acquire`). Returning placement (claims 4, 13, 15, 20 and 23) uses S-0208 claim 3's position choice and S-0209's entry outcomes. A projectile removed without detonating leaves its parasite off the map (claim 21). On host loss a vehicle parasite whose fallback choice fails is put at world position (128, 128, 0) (claim 23). The seven quirk switches from the spec's Edge cases are in `quirks`.
- **Orders a human gives.** Select a drone and click an enemy vehicle. The attack order fires the latching weapon (`targeting::click`, then `orders::issue`).
- **Claim ledger.** `docs/m6-claims/S-0614.md`.

Provisional rules of M6a for missiles and parasites: none at present (the naval ones are listed in the naval units section). Q-0610 to Q-0616 are answered by S-0506 revision 3, S-0204 revision 8 and S-0303 revision 11; Q-0620 to Q-0623 by S-0614 revisions 4 and 5.

Known limits of M6a:

- A culled host is set sinking (`naval::start_sinking`) and goes down and leaves play by S-0504 claim 4 (`naval::sinking_turn`). The tick of the cull within the host's turn is still open (S-0504 claim 4).
- Some hooks have no callers yet because their topics are not built: `parasite::host_unloading`, `parasite::host_consumed`, `parasite::host_curtain_started` (curtain protection, S-0614 rev 6: 50 ticks of suppression just before release), `parasite::superweapon_teleport` and `parasite::locomotor_replaced`.
- A teleporting superweapon's departure (S-0614 rev 6 claim 12): a drone parasite is not released at launch and its fate at departure is unresolved (S-0614 Open questions, P7). `parasite::host_jumps` is only the host's own teleport jump; no departure hook exists, so the superweapon topic must not call it for that path until the spec settles it.
- Not modelled: spark particles, the tank bunker, uncloaking, F2 rocking (presentation) and claim 19's re-selection (presentation).

## Simulation (M6a: naval units)

S-0504 (naval units) is built in `glasnost_sim::naval`, with small hooks in `drive`, `crush`, `objects`, `tick` and `combat`. The ledger is [docs/m6-claims/S-0504.md](m6-claims/S-0504.md); the tests are in `crates/deterministic/sim/tests/claims_s0504.rs` and `crates/convert/src/sim_naval_tests.rs`.

- **Ship locomotor (claims 1 and 3).** A vehicle with the ship locomotor carries `DriveState` (`objects::create`) and moves by `drive::tick`, called from `naval::tick` in the tick pipeline. Its heading point, scatter centre, arrival stop (S-0207 claim 19 a), launching hold (`spawn::track_held` in `drive::begin_track`) and destination clearing after a failed search (S-0212 claim 23) are the driving ones. Its crush mode is S-0210 claim 9's ship case (`drive::crush_mode`: a crushable overlay, or a wall for crusher-all, in the first or second cell, never a vehicle), and its crush slowdown is entered by a crusher on a crush-mode track entering a cell with the overlay of registration index 0 (`crush::entered_cell`, `quirks.crush_slowdown_first_overlay`).
- **Wakes (claim 2; S-0207 claim 23).** `drive::wake_check(w, id, period)` is shared: *moving now* is a turning body, or an active track with `drive::progress` above 0 at the check, after the tick's movement. A ship uses period 8 and makes no wake when its type is underwater (`naval::ship_wake`). A drive vehicle uses period 10 (`drive::driving_wake`). This answers Q-0600: the old `DriveState::step` test is gone from the wake, and `DriveState::step` stays for S-0402 claim 5a.
- **Sinking (claims 4 and 5; S-0312 claim 10).** `combat::death::kill` sinks a killed vehicle when `naval::sinks_at_kill` holds (naval, not underwater, not organic, weight at least `combat.general.sinking_weight`, on a water cell). It then skips the explosion and destruction list picks and `leave_play`, keeps 1 health point and calls `naval::start_sinking`. Debris, the death weapon and the crew still run. `naval::sinking_turn` runs at the start of the vessel's own turn (`tick::object_turn`; for a vessel destroyed while sinking it is the whole turn, S-0312 claim 32). It lowers z by 5. Below −400 leptons above the terrain it leaves play with no draws. Otherwise, on ticks divisible by 4, it takes two ranged draws and makes a wake at (x + r₂, y + r₁, terrain height). Its locomotor does not run while it sinks. A sinking firer gets *illegal* at rank 4 and a sinking target *illegal* at rank 9 (`combat::check`), and `combat::acquire::redirect` gives no target for it. The state is `Object::naval` (`naval::NavalState`).
- **Underwater vessels (claim 6).** `naval::submerged` needs an underwater type that can cloak (`cloakable`, imported) and a cloak state. Cloaking (S-0516) is not built, so no vessel is submerged yet and the submerge and surface test waits for S-0516.
- **Docking (claims 7, 8, 12 and 14).** `naval::naval_match_refuses`, `naval::starting_distance`, `naval::depot_distance` (F3, exact integer root) and `naval::waiting_gate`, with `naval::SETTLING_DELAY`, are called by the depot cycle of M6b (below): a damaged ship clicked onto its repairing yard docks at the yard's fixed point and is repaired in play (`claim_14_ship_repaired_at_yard`).
- **Revision 5 (answers Q-0640, Q-0642, Q-0643, Q-0644).** A vehicle type without a weight weighs exactly 1.0 in the sinking test (claim 11, `naval::sinks_at_kill`). Sinking is no immunity: damage reaches a sinking vessel by the ordinary rules and it keeps self-healing (claim 10, S-0314 claim 19; `combat::damage::apply`, `tick::object_turn`); a further killing hit is answered by revision 6. A body turn under way continues while it sinks (claim 10; the locomotor is suppressed, the body turn is not). The naval match's only exception is the plain *amphibious* zone (claim 7, as built). Ships share the driving locomotor's path refresh, retry delay and first-step blocker rules (claim 9, as built through `drive`). The chase cut of S-0212 claim 20 is not built for any locomotor yet. `naval::yard_docking_point` gives a naval vehicle-factory yard's docking point (claim 12); the depot cycle uses the same point (`depot::docking_point`).
- **Revision 6 (answers Q-0646).** A further killing hit on a sinking vessel takes the ordinary kill path: credit and death effects again, and S-0312 claim 10 leaves an eligible vessel at 1 health, still sinking, its descent going on from its current height (claim 13; `combat::damage`, `naval::start_sinking`). A type that fails the sinking condition follows S-0312 claim 32 (next bullet). Claim 14 (yard interruptions and queued entry) rests on S-0314 claims 20 to 23's depot cycle, built in M6b.
- **S-0312 revision 22 (claims 15 and 32, with S-0504 claim 13).** A killing hit on a vessel already sinking (after a killing hit or a cull) that fails claim 10's conditions, for example a culled host that is underwater or below the sinking weight, now ends at 0 health with claim 9's explosion and destruction animations and their two list picks, and the vessel stays in play, still sinking, when its type cannot crash (`combat::death::kill`). A dead sinking vessel takes only its descent in its own turn (`tick::object_turn` calls `naval::sinking_turn` and nothing else: no healing, no behaviour processing), so it leaves play below −400 leptons at the end of the same descent. At 0 health it is out of ordinary damage (S-0302 claim 8), so no later hit kills it again. A crashable type that was sinking cannot start a crash below the surface and is removed at the end of the hit's effects, in either row of claim 32. Destruction is registered again and the raises, debris, death weapon and crew repeat through the ordinary kill path; passengers, the attached bomb, the bomb mark and death image frames are not built.
- **Revision 7 (editorial).** Other sinking triggers belong to their movement specs (the jumpjet drop of S-0502 claim 19, not built), and claim 9's chase cut and last-step refresh are S-0212 claim 20's, which includes ships. No code change.
- **S-0312 revisions 23 and 24 (answer Q-0652).**
  - A crashable vessel that meets claim 10's conditions at its first killing hit cannot start a crash. It gets 1 health and is removed at the end of the hit's effects (`combat::death::kill`).
  - It takes neither of claim 9's list picks and no crash-start draws. Its debris, crew and death weapon still apply.
  - It makes no descent and no sinking wakes (S-0504 claim 4).
  - Revision 23's bomb and crate wording needs no code: bombs and crates are not built.
  - An absent `Crashable` reads as no (`combat::data`). The import note that a later absence keeps the value in force is left to the ordinary rules resolution and is not tested here.
- **Revisions 8 to 10.**
  - Claim 9's ship refresh distance is the table-root distance under `quirks.table_root_distance`, which `drive` already uses.
  - F3's double round-toward-zero root equals the exact integer root that `naval::depot_distance` already takes.
  - Claim 4 removes a crashable vessel at its first killing hit (the S-0312 change above).
  - Claim 13's death-image-frames qualifier is not modelled, because death images are not built.
  - The only code change is the S-0312 one.
- **Import.** `Cloakable` (`combat.cloakable`) is new. `[General] Wake` absent gives an importer warning. `Naval`, `Underwater`, `Organic`, `Weight`, `ShipSinkingWeight`, `UnitRepair`, building `Naval`, `MovementZone` and `Locomotor` were already imported.
- **What play needs.** A naval yard is a building with `Naval=yes` and a vehicle factory. A player places it on flat cells whose tiles are in the tile catalogue's water role range (S-0406 claim 3 d, `econ::placement`), and it needs an empty water cell at its exit (`econ::release::naval_row`); the tests spawn the yard directly and skip placement. A naval unit is a vehicle with `Naval=yes` and the ship locomotor; the tests give it a float locomotion class and the water movement zone. A map needs water cells (land type water) next to the yard, and land rows whose multipliers let the ship's class enter water but not land. Orders are the ordinary ones: build the type on the naval line (`Order::Build`), then move and attack by `targeting::click` and `orders::issue`.

Provisional rules of M6a naval units: none at present. The Q-0641 row (depot cycle not built) is retired: M6b builds it (below).

## Simulation (M6b: depot cycle)

The depot cycle of S-0314 claims 6 and 16 to 24, S-0504 claims 7, 8, 12 and 14 and S-0215 claim 29 is built in `glasnost_sim::depot`. A repair depot (a building with `UnitRepair=yes`) and a naval yard with the repair flag accept an allied vehicle, which drives to the docking point, docks, and is repaired in three phases until the depot sends it away. The ledger is [docs/m6-claims/depot-cycle.md](m6-claims/depot-cycle.md); the tests are in `crates/deterministic/sim/tests/claims_s0314_depot.rs` and `claim_14_*` in `claims_s0504.rs`.

- **State.** `Object::depot` (`depot::DepotState`): a vehicle's `link` (depot, place, tethered; valid only while its contact is that depot), `queued` (claim 23's queued entry), `entering` (the depot claim 23's order makes the vehicle remember, step 1j; the step 1h `walked` mark is gone) and `loco_off` (claims 21 and 22); a depot's `serviced` vehicle. The phase is the depot's *repair* progress count. This state came with save format 18.
- **Permission and entry (claims 16 and 17; S-0214 table V value *n*).** `depot::accepts` is claim 16's permission. `targeting` gives *enter* over an accepting allied depot (row n3) and *no-enter* for a vehicle of zone *fly* (row n1); a held one-place depot keeps *select* (row n4). The enter order takes contact at once in the first free place (`depot::order_entered`). The vehicle's *enter* processing (`depot::process_enter`, from `process`) repeats the request; only a depot with the pad flag tethers it there (`depot::pad_exchange`: the foundation centre's cell, and no moving destination or place 0 more than 128 leptons from the centre), and a tethered vehicle turns to 16384 and, once still, queues *repair* with the ready mark on the depot and *sleep* on the vehicle. Reaching the docking-point cell during movement (`depot::movement_arrival`, from the driving and hovering locomotors' arrivals) queues *repair* and *sleep* without a tether, so an ordinary depot repairs its vehicle untethered. An unarmed depot's guard processing makes the early start of claim 17 (`depot::early_start`, from `combat::behave`).
- **Docking point (claim 18; S-0504 claim 12).** `depot::docking_point`: the foundation centre plus the place's displacement (`econ::release::pad_position_at`, S-0404 claim 15's rules), or the cell (2, 1) for a vehicle factory. `path::heading_point` heads a vehicle in depot contact for it, and `hover::destination_point` makes it a hovering vehicle's destination *D*, so a hover stops in the docking cell of a depot larger than one cell.
- **Repair (claim 6; S-0504 claim 8).** `depot::process_repair`: waiting (the naval waiting gate, 3 ticks to settling), settling (one step; locomotor off only within 200 leptons; moving farther away, no step, the locomotor switched on and *D* ticks; not needed sends it away unannounced), working (*D* + *K*<sub>u</sub> − 1, then *K*<sub>u</sub>; done or not needed announces and sends it away; cannot pay returns to settling and announces low funds at exactly 0 credits). Release goes to the depot's rally point, else claim 25's departure cell (`depot::exit_cell`: door kinds, the naval rows, S-0404's exit list or the perimeter, the first giving free); a neutral or special house's vehicle with a guard spot goes there. With no cell the vehicle keeps contact: from working it returns to settling with a 1-tick retry, in settling it retries after *D*. The announcements are `Sound::UnitRepaired` (audio: F7 kind *unit repaired*, 2 cells, 400 ticks) and `Sound::LowFunds`, the depot owner's only.
- **Orders, sale, destruction, power (claims 20 to 23).** `depot::order_applied` (from `orders::apply_behaviour`) and `depot::stop_order` give claim 20's and claim 23's order rules; a docked vehicle is tethered (`econ::tethered`) and keeps its contact, while an untethered one, at an ordinary depot in any repair phase, is released by another order. `depot::vehicle_turn` (from `tick::object_turn`, after the locomotor movement) retries a queued entry every tick; permission queues *enter* with an immediate start only when ready.
