- **Demolition blast and flash (claims 5 and 6).** An undefined marker acts at the stand-in point (128, 128, 0) and makes no bridge attempt. `demolition_blast` makes five area detonations with the demolition warhead and no source or house, then calls `bridges::cell_attempts`. `light_flash` pushes an `Effect::Trigger` with code 66, the waypoint point and the flash-size level as its number; the presentation does not draw it yet.
- **Ambient (claim 7).** `TriggerState::mp.ambient` holds the base and target values code 73 set; the renderer does not read them yet.
- **Local area (claim 8).** Shrinking touches no object (it does not remove, move, stop or switch off buildings or units; an excluded power source and an excluded building still count; an ordered attack on an object outside is still an attack). `resize_local_area` sets the footprint's local area, recalculates every legal cell, recomputes the passability categories and rebuilds the reach regions.
- **Restoration (claims 9 and 13).** The record is in creation order with top-left cells; a building of the type and house that covers the recorded cell with any foundation cell is healed; otherwise a fresh building is placed by ordinary during-play placement (construction queued; claim 15, Q-0840 answered: the ready mark is clear at entry, the placed case of S-0406 claim 17, even without a build-up sprite) and the ground-layer occupants of its foundation are re-placed in reverse lifting order by the 1 x 1 nearby search from their old cells, facing 0, removed when no cell is found. `record_starting_buildings` runs in `load::new_game` after the game-start terrain pass. `restore_starting_buildings` heals a recorded building that is still there, and otherwise replaces it, displacing mobile ground units.
- **Entries (claims 10 to 14).** An infantry unit arriving at a transport raises its tag once before the acceptance test (`transport::arrival`); vehicle boarding, depot and refinery docking and pad landing raise nothing (tested in the owning claim files); capture makes no ownership-change raise; `vehicle_like_entry_seam` is new. `carry_over` moves a flagged tag to the captured or hijacked object. `capture_seam`, `hijack_seam`, `consuming_entry_seam` and `retaining_entry_seam` hold the raise order for S-0509, S-0312 claim 13's hijack, S-0510 and S-0508 when those are built.
- **Quirks.** `restoration_failed_return_removes_unit` (on), `repeated_lightning_strike_suppressed`, `trigger_crate_respects_setting` and `trigger_crate_expiry_while_disabled` (off: off is the original) are new (443 with the upgrade switches of S-0215). The two crate switches are not consulted, because crates (S-0520) are not built.
- **Save format 28.** The trigger definitions' new fields, the warhead's flash fields and `TriggerState::mp` are new state, and they are hashed.
- **Local lane.** `GLASNOST_GAME_DIR=<install> GLASNOST_LANE_SCENE=trigger-coverage cargo test --release -p glasnost-app --test m6b_lane -- --ignored --nocapture` starts every offered map and counts its trigger events and actions as implemented, seamed or outside the subset (`mp_triggers::event_coverage`, `action_coverage`).
- **Seams.**
  - Code 108 (crates) places a crate through `crates::place_trigger` (S-0520).
  - Engineer capture (S-0509), hijacking (S-0312 claim 13), garrisons (S-0508), infiltration (S-0510) and sabotage call nothing yet; their seams are in `mp_triggers`.
  - Teams, computer targeting, change owner, destroy attached objects, explosions, cheering and the waypoint animations stay as S-0011 left them.

Provisional rules of M6b multiplayer triggers, each tied to a request:

| Where | Interim rule | Request |
|---|---|---|
| `mp_triggers::resolve_house` | position values A to H take the house at that start position; S-0009 claim 29's rule for header counts of 9 or more is not applied | — |

Aircraft shadow anchor (S-0105 claim 40, Q-0799 answered): `mapview::build::vehicle_voxels` draws an aircraft shadow at the projection of its horizontal position at the terrain height there (`Mobile::shadow`, from `live::shadow_anchor`; a map-only scene reads the terrain from the map), 60 pixels higher over a qualifying high bridge, whatever its flight height. Q-0841 is answered by S-0501 revision 15, claim 43: an aircraft counts as marked on a deck only by its own deck mark (`AirState::deck`, read by `live::shadow_anchor`), never by its layer or its listing. The mark is set by a descent that reaches height 0 above a high-bridge deck or a landing that finishes at deck height, cleared by an ordinary climbing tick or a refused touchdown, and left alone by horizontal flight (`air::tick`, `air::touchdown`). Altitude calculations use the deck as their reference for a marked aircraft, and for an unmarked one at least 416 leptons above a high-bridge cell (`air::height`); `air::listing_height` and `air::sync_listing` list a flying aircraft in the ground layer, the deck layer or neither (S-0209 claim 13). Vehicle shadow anchors stay as the body point (open in S-0105). The paradrop plane's flight height (1500 leptons in the lane) is drawn. The pitch of a rocket body and the pitch and bank of a flying aircraft are drawn since M6c step 1, section *Rendering of aircraft attitude (M6c)* below.

Save format 29 to 31 (M6b2): one bump for the step-2 to step-4 state changes: the silo's busy-until tick (`ObjSw::silo_busy_until`), `FallState::chute_once`, `Cloud::delay` meaning the effective stage delay, and the cloak generator's `was_on` phase mark. Format 31 adds the warp member's `entry_ok` (the claim 50 placement check). Format 30 adds `AirState::deck` (the aircraft deck mark, S-0501 claim 43) and the warp member's `fell_back` and `refused` marks (S-0513 claims 50 and 51). Format 28 is the multiplayer trigger and driving-destination state.

## Simulation (M6c: garrisons)

S-0508 (revision 7) is built in `glasnost_sim::structures` (`garrison`, `entry`, `ownership`); the ledger is [docs/m6-claims/S-0508.md](m6-claims/S-0508.md), the tests are `claims_s0508.rs` and `claims_s0508_fire.rs` (fixtures in `structures_common`). New state is `Object::structure` (`StructState`: a building's occupants in order, the shared occupant/muzzle index, an occupant's building); save format version 32.

- **Entry (claims 1 to 6).** `walk::tick` calls `structures::entry::arrival` at the arrival update, after the boarding test and before the arrival raise of S-0208 claim 22: an infantry unit in *capture* whose destination is a building that stands in its cell. An occupier or assaulter type makes the garrison attempt (`garrison::attempt`: the occupation test of S-0508 claim 1, `targeting::may_garrison`, shared with the cursor of S-0214 row I8; one entry raise; the unit leaves the map through `objects::take_inside` and is appended to the occupants; or the refusal of claim 4, `entry::refuse`). The announcement is `Effect::Sound::Garrisoned`, which the audio crate plays for the entering unit's house (`BuildingGarrisonedSound`, `EVA_StructureGarrisoned`).
- **The building's turn (claims 7 to 9).** `econ::events::turn` calls `garrison::turn` for a civilian garrison building (garrisonable, tech level exactly −1): a badly damaged one evicts, the owner follows the first occupant while it belongs to the civilian house, an empty one returns to the civilian house (`ownership::change`, which runs `econ::events::owner_changed`) with `Effect::Sound::Abandoned` for the owner it lost it from (`BuildingAbandonedSound`; the announcement only when the *garrison abandoned* radar event is created, F7).
- **Eviction (claims 10 to 13).** `garrison::evict` with the options of `Evict` (`PLAIN`: deploy order, forced evacuation, destruction, the map's action 111; `SALE`: the inside fallback; `HUNT`: the house-wide hunt action 6). The exit cell is the highest-ranked cell of `garrison::exit_ring` that qualifies for the first occupant (`garrison::qualifies`, revision 7 claim 10): there is no independent legal-cell or usable-area gate; a candidate with no map cell reads as the stand-in blank cell of S-0009 claim 44; terrain level −6 or lower is clear before the other tests (`quirks.garrison_negative_level_exit`, S-0208 claim 19's bypass); an ordinary occupant, which went in from inside the usable area and keeps that status (its retained position), is refused a cell outside the usable area (S-0209 claim 4), a legal one included; otherwise the *clear* entry outcome at reference level −1; the occupants are put at its centre from the last to the first, each with its scatter request (claims 11 and 22: the placement has no second entry test and no terrain, capacity or usable-area refusal; an occupant that is already on the map, an inconsistent state, is removed, not destroyed). A building's *unload* is `garrison::process_unload`; a sale calls `garrison::sold` (S-0408 claim 9, after absorbed passengers, before the survivors); a destroyed building calls `garrison::destroyed` (S-0312 claim 17); the trigger actions 111 and 6 are in `triggers::action`. The quirk switches `garrison_capacity_equality`, `garrison_exit_skips_corner`, `garrison_negative_level_exit`, `garrison_no_exit_removes` and `assaulter_reversed_kill_credit` are consulted.
- **Assault, erasure, defeat (claims 14, 15, 19).** `garrison::attempt` for an assaulter type runs `assault` (`assault_eligible`; the occupants removed with the weapon's occupant animation, `OccupantAnim`; reversed or ordinary kill credit through `damage::credit` and `death::destruction_raises`). `garrison::erased` is the call the erasure topic (S-0514, not built) will make; `objects::remove` also removes the occupants of a building that leaves play. The assault removes the occupants from the last list position to position 0, the occupant at position *i* showing its animation at muzzle point *i* (`garrison::assault_shift`: no wrap, an unwritten point is (0, 0); a position of 10 or later uses point *i* mod 10, S-0508 claim 25, below). An occupant killed inside (defeat's single hit on its owner's objects) goes through `death::passenger_death`: credited, destruction raises, removed from the world; claim 23 (an engine design rule, no switch) removes its place in the same update (`garrison::removing`, called from `objects::remove`): the others keep their order, the index is kept when below the new count and is 0 otherwise (a range check, no modulo), and the count, the capacity test, the delay quotient, the muzzle modulus and the experience recipient use the count after removal. With no civilian house the owner of a civilian garrison building is kept at every turn with no abandoned sound (claim 24, a design rule); the forced evacuation still empties it.
- **Firing from inside (S-0308 claim 13, S-0313 claim 6, S-0301 claim 7, S-0302 claim 2, S-0307 claim 4, S-0311 claim 8, S-0215 claim 32, S-0405 T1).** `combat::weapon::slot_weapon` takes the weapon of the current occupant (`occupant_weapon`) for a garrisoned building whose type lets occupants fire, `choose_slot` answers slot 0, `fire::full_delay` divides by the occupant count and the occupy factor, `damage::shot_damage` applies the garrison multiplier, `range::in_range` the garrison range, `position::fire_point` the muzzle point of the index, `fire::shot` advances the index (not for a disc laser), `veterancy::destruction_credited` gives an untrainable garrisoned building's experience to its occupant.
- **Importer.** `OccupantAnim` (weapon) and `MuzzleFlash`*n* (building art, one pixel pair per place of the occupant capacity, an unwritten point (0, 0), `garrison_muzzles`) are fields now; the audio names `BuildingGarrisonedSound` and `BuildingAbandonedSound` and the announcements `EVA_StructureGarrisoned` and `EVA_StructureAbandoned` are read by the audio crate.
- **Client.** The click is S-0214 row I8 (already in `targeting::click`, with the capture cursor). The live view draws the map's occupiable buildings with the game's own buildings (`mapview::live`, `live_gates`), and the body frame of an occupiable building is 2·[occupied] + [hurt] (S-0108 claim 2, `quirks::occupiable_frame`). The lane scene `garrison` (`crates/app/tests/m6c_lane.rs`) sends three occupiers into a map-placed building, puts them out with a deploy order and writes `local-lane/m6c/garrison-empty.png`, `garrison-occupied.png` and `garrison-evicted.png`.
- **Own AI.** See *Own AI (M6c: special abilities)* below.

There are no provisional rules of the garrisons left: Q-0970 to Q-0973 are answered by S-0508 revision 7, and Q-0990 (assault animation at list position 10 or later) by revision 8's claim 25, engine design rule with no quirk switch: `garrison::assault` shows the animation of list position *i* at muzzle point *i* mod 10, whatever the number of configured points, the occupant count or the firing index (S-0313 claim 6 points to it).

## Simulation (M6c: engineers and capture)

S-0509 is built in `glasnost_sim::structures` (`entry`, `engineer`, `ownership`); the ledger is [docs/m6-claims/S-0509.md](m6-claims/S-0509.md), the tests are `claims_s0509.rs`. New state: `House::lost_to_capture` (the mark of claim 11); save format version 33; the presentation event `Effect::Captured`.

- **The entry (claims 1 and 2).** `structures::entry::arrival` is the one dispatcher of an infantry unit's arrival at a building (called from `walk::tick`, after the boarding test): the behaviour is *capture*, *area guard* or *patrol* and the building, in a cell the unit stands in, is its destination or its target. An occupier or assaulter in *capture* with a building destination makes the garrison attempt (S-0508); a vehicle-like destination building is taken over (claim 17, `engineer::takeover`); an engineer at a bridge repair hut is S-0511's (`bridges::hut::engineer_entered_hut`, whose entry raise is the general raise); otherwise the general entry raise (`mp_triggers::entry_raise`) comes first and then an engineer follows `engineer::entry`, a spy S-0510 (`structures::spy`) and any other unit is refused and scattered (`entry::refuse`).
- **Engineer outcomes (claims 3 to 7).** `engineer::entry` judges the row after the general raise: arrival-friendly buildings (own house, allied by the engineer's house, or garrisonable and passive) are refused at full health and repaired below it (`quirks.engineer_repairs_during_sale` for a sale in progress); then the multi-engineer row (never reached in a skirmish, `engineer::multi_engineer` is false; the hit, F1 and F2, is `engineer::damaging_hit`), a non-capturable building (the engineer is used up, `quirks.engineer_lost_on_uncapturable`), a building being sold or erased (refused), and the capture. A capture is a second entry raise, the lost-to-capture mark on the house that owns the building then, the tag carry-over, `ownership::change` (cause `Capture`), the scatter of the infantry on the building (claim 9) and the used-up engineer (destroyed raise, removal).
- **Ownership change (claims 12 to 16).** `ownership::change` handles the contacts of the building (claim 14, F3), runs `econ::events::owner_changed` (power, shroud, factories, production, counts, the captured mark, cloak, superweapons through the tech recheck), clears a silo's slots (S-0506 claim 13) and pushes `Effect::Captured` for the audio crate (the capture and loss announcements of S-0701 claim 39, the *captured* radar event) and the client (claim 15: `Play::captured_yard_cancels_placement` cancels the placement in progress when the local house gains a construction yard). The planted-bomb disarming (claim 13) is a seam until S-0519; claim 16 (the computer-only sale) is the original's and never applies to our AI houses. The map's change-owner action (S-0011 claim 24, code 14) is built (`triggers::action`, cause `Trigger`, no announcement).
- **Importer and audio.** `BuildingRepairedSound` and `CaptureEvaEvent` are read by `glasnost_audio::names` from the imported text (a game-wide sound at the repaired building's centre; the tech building's capture announcement). The other keys were read by earlier specs.
- **Own AI.** See *Own AI (M6c: special abilities)*.
- **Client.** The click is S-0214 rows I4 to I7 (already in `targeting::click`: capture, damaging entry and repair building all give the *capture* behaviour order for an engineer, S-0214 table M), with the capture cursor frames. The lane scene `capture` of `crates/app/tests/m6c_lane.rs` sends an engineer into an enemy building and writes `local-lane/m6c/capture-before.png` and `capture-after.png` (the building drawn in the local house's colour).

## Simulation (M6c: spies and infiltration)

S-0510 is built in `glasnost_sim::structures::spy` (the entry dispatcher `structures::entry` hands a spy there after the general entry raise); the ledger is [docs/m6-claims/S-0510.md](m6-claims/S-0510.md), the tests are `claims_s0510.rs`. New state: `House::infiltrated_vehicle_factory` and `infiltrated_infantry_factory` (claim 5); save format version 34; the presentation event `Effect::Infiltrated`.

- **Rows (claims 1 to 4).** `spy::rows` lists the effects E1 to E7 of the building (radar recover, power blackout, stolen tech, superweapon reset, cash theft, vehicle and infantry factory flags); with `quirks.infiltration_single_effect` only the first applies, otherwise all do. The spy is used up (destroyed raise, removal, no kill or score). An allied or own-house building gives no effect.
- **Theft (claims 6 and 7).** `spy::theft_amount` reads `SpyMoneyStealPercent` through `Rules::spy_steal` in single precision (`Decimal::single_value`, absent 0.5); `quirks.spy_theft_unbacked_gain` decides what an out-of-range fraction does. The importer warns for a fraction outside 0 to 1.
- **Factory flags (claim 5).** `objects::veteran_at_creation` reads the flags: infantry after a barracks, trainable non-naval vehicles and undeploying buildings after a war factory. `spy::alternate_icon` gives the sidebar the alternate icon (S-0007 claim 25); the client draws it (`game/session.rs`).
- **Audio.** The infiltration lines and the *infiltrated* radar event are in `glasnost_audio::events` (`infiltrated`, `announce_entry`).
- **Own AI.** See *Own AI (M6c: special abilities)*.
- **Client.** The click is S-0214's (the infiltrate cursor, already in `targeting`). The lane scene `infiltrate` of `crates/app/tests/m6c_lane.rs` sends a spy into an enemy radar building and writes `local-lane/m6c/infiltrate-before.png` and `infiltrate-after.png`.

## Simulation (M6c: tech buildings)

S-0515 is built in `glasnost_sim::structures::tech`; the ledger is [docs/m6-claims/S-0515.md](m6-claims/S-0515.md), the tests are `claims_s0515.rs`. New state: `BuildingSwitches::income_restart` (the tick of the last restart of a building's income countdown); save format version 35. The manned state of claim 2 was built with S-0405 (`BuildingSwitches::manned`, set by `power::owner_changed`).

- **Bonus and restart (claims 3, 4, 7).** `econ::events::owner_changed` calls `tech::owner_changed` for a building whose owner really changes: the acquisition bonus (`ProduceCashStartup`) is added to the new owner's balance (signed 32-bit wrapping, no factor) when the old owner is a passive house (`setup::passive`), then the countdown restarts. `econ::events::entered_map` restarts it at a building's first entry to the map (claim 4 a); a countdown never restarted reads 0 and has no cycle.
- **The cycle (claims 5, 6, F1, F2).** `econ::events::turn` calls `tech::turn` in a building's own turn, after the power topic: with `D = ProduceCashDelay` at least 1, the cycle falls at the restart tick + *D* - 1 (`quirks.cash_cycle_one_short`; off: + *D*) and later ticks have none (`quirks.cash_cycle_lapse`; off: the first turn at or after the due tick has it). A building being erased (`power::being_erased`) has none; otherwise the cycle restarts the countdown and pays the current owner `ProduceCashAmount` by F2 when the owner is not passive and the building is in operation.
- **Secret labs (claim 8).** Nothing to build: the secret type is assigned at game start and stored on the building; an ownership change neither touches it nor draws (tested).
- **Importer.** `ProduceCashStartup`, `ProduceCashAmount` and `ProduceCashDelay` are integer fields of a building type (`glasnost_convert::sim`), absent 0.
- **Seams.** The pulse that stops a building (S-0405 claim 8) is still a seam, so no test pulses a derrick; the erasure of S-0514's temporal beam will reach `power::being_erased` by itself (a warp member is the only erasure built).
- **Own AI.** See *Own AI (M6c: special abilities)*.
- **Client.** Nothing of its own: the click is the engineer's (S-0214, capture). The lane scene `tech` of `crates/app/tests/m6c_lane.rs` finds a cash-producing tech type by its rules fields, has an engineer capture it and prints the credits at the capture and at three income cycles; it writes `local-lane/m6c/tech-before.png`, `tech-captured.png` and `tech-income.png`.

## Simulation (M6c: jumpjets)

S-0502 is built in `glasnost_sim::jump` (state, queries, orders, occupancy marks in `jump.rs`; the flight update in `jump/fly.rs`); the ledger is [docs/m6-claims/S-0502.md](m6-claims/S-0502.md), the tests are `claims_s0502*.rs` (fixtures in `jump_common`) and, for the importer, `jumpjet_type_keys` and its neighbours in `crates/convert/src/sim_econ_tests.rs`. New state: `Object::jump` (`JumpState`: the flight state, whether it holds a move order, the destination point, the current and maximum speed, the target height, the wobble phase, the movement heading, the speed fraction, the ground mark, the landing reservation, the hover slot, the deck flag, the landing request of S-0517, a pending crash start) and `World::jump_slots` (the hover slot of each cell); save format version 36 (38 since step 1b: `JumpState::listed`). Revision 5 (taken in at M6c step 1b, [docs/m6-claims/step1b-m6c.md](m6-claims/step1b-m6c.md)) answers Q-0975 to Q-0981; revision 6 (taken in at step 1c, [docs/m6-claims/step1c-m6c.md](m6-claims/step1c-m6c.md)) answers Q-1010 to Q-1012. The new locomotor is `rules::Locomotor::Jump`: **the type's imported locomotor chooses it** (`JumpJet` flag or not), for infantry and vehicles.

- **Update (claims 3 and 5 to 15, F1 to F6).** `tick::object_turn` calls `jump::tick` for the locomotor. An update takes a physics step with the previous state's maximum speed and target height, then the state's outcome: speed (F1, the fraction before the ground-hold reset), wobble phase and desired height (F2), the ground reference (F3, the direct reference or the look-ahead; the quirk `jumpjet_lookahead_bridge`), the height step and ground hold (F4), the step along the heading (F5, `walk::step_point`; the off setting of `jumpjet_step_truncation` is the exact rounding of `glasnost_num::bigmath::rounded_sin_cos`) and the body facing; then the outcome of grounded (take-off), ascending, hovering (the six rows), cruising (the F6 bands, `jumpjet_turn_slowdown_one_sided`), descending (water and beach, the landing check, the deploy direction, touchdown) and crashing. A jumpjet that holds no move order and is grounded or hovering takes no update.
- **Orders (claims 16, 17, 21, 23, 24).** `infantry::destination_assigned` calls `jump::take_destination`: an order-level destination goes to `order_point` (claim 16): the landing reservation is released (unless grounded), the nearby-cell search runs with the settings of claim 23 and does not lift the unit's own occupancy (revision 6, Q-1011: an ordinary vehicle ordered to its own cell gets a nearby cell, only an entering or balloon-hover vehicle keeps the exact point; a jumpjet-type infantry unit, by the imported `JumpJet` flag and outside an ion storm (none exists), ignores a nonempty assignment to its own cell before any search or draw, `jump::own_cell_filtered`, and S-0213 claim 21's get-up skips it) (`jump::Use`: a vehicle that is not balloon-hover, hover class, flying zone, no deck mode, bridge cells excluded; infantry and balloon-hover vehicles, deck mode at a high-bridge centre, bridges allowed; the stopped or failed landing, the type's own class and zone; none has a reach region or a height condition), the exact point for a balloon-hover vehicle and an entering vehicle, the centre of the found cell for a vehicle, and for infantry the standing choice `jump::standing_choice` (`walk::choose_position` with the centre class: the vehicle-present and object-present refusals take no draw, otherwise one ranged draw over 0 to 3 picks table O's row R0 to R3, the first free side position wins, all taken is a failed adjustment; the ground record, the deck record only for a deck at or below world height 0), the re-ascent of a descending unit and the *unload*-to-*guard* replacement. A failed adjustment keeps the ordered point as the movement destination and changes nothing else; one that would retain an off-map point is refused (the preceding point, moving flag, state, speeds, target height and landing reservation stay; claim 24's safety departure). An order-level destination that becomes empty is not claim 16's direct no-point instruction (`jump::no_point`, refused while moving now): for a moving jumpjet it is claim 17's reroute (`jump::stop_with`, the stop order's `jump::stop_ordered` from `orders::apply_other`, the deploy order's `jump::destination_cleared` from `orders::apply_other` and every other clear through `take_destination`; revision 6, Q-1012: an unchanged assignment of an ordinary vehicle, an empty-to-empty clear included, is suppressed, with no reroute, no draw and the preceding point and commitment kept, `stop_ordered` and `take_destination`'s comparison with the order the locomotor last took), which assigns a replacement movement point through `give_point` and leaves the order-level destination empty; `jump::stop` is the failed landing (it also sets the order-level destination): the nearby cell, or the destruction by its health, or `quirks.jumpjet_no_cell_self_destruct` off: hovering again. The neighbour step is one ranged draw over 0 to 7 in the tick of the transition (`jump::neighbour_step`), followed by an infantry unit's own draw of the standing choice.
- **Occupancy (claims 4, 22, 26).** `JumpState::listed` is the cell list the jumpjet is in. In every physics step but hovering and cruising the old placement goes first (`fly::remove_placement`: the mark, the listing, a recompute of the cell of the position, S-0211 claim 5), the clearance is evaluated without it (`jump::clearance` reads the actual ground listings and has no self-exclusion, claim 22), and the new position is placed only when it qualifies (`fly::place_after_step`, `jump::placement_qualifies`: revision 6, Q-1010, exactly when *r* < 208 leptons or the bridge-adjusted *r*′ = 0, the 208 test reading *r*; starting a crash does not set the falling state that suppresses the subtraction; the on-deck state ends at the first rise): listed on the deck or the ground and marked (`lay_mark`: an infantry unit never takes a standing position that another unit holds), and its cell recomputed; a crash's extra drop adds its own removal and placement. Hovering and cruising make no new listing or mark, keep a listing they have in the old cell and do not recompute on a crossing (`jump::move_to` moves nothing but the position). A descending one holds its landing reservation (the same mark in the landing cell, made by the landing check; its own actual-position mark does not take the standing position from it, and `clear_mark` clears a shared position once). `occupancy::in_progress` reads *moving now* (every state except grounded and hovering), so readiness (S-0215 claim 11), the balloon-hover test of S-0310 claim 7 and the move fall-back use it. Touchdown (`settle`) and take-off (`leave_ground`) keep their own recomputes.
- **Crash (claim 18, S-0312 claims 15 and 16).** `combat::death` starts the crash of a killed crashable jumpjet above the terrain (`jump::start_crash`: passengers, three tumbling draws except for infantry, a pending start); the unit stays in play (`tick::object_turn` calls `jump::tick` for a dead crashing jumpjet), takes the extra drop *k* in its next update, enters *crashing* with target height −5, spins by claim 18's lead, and ends below 1 lepton or by crossing a deck with S-0312 claim 16's impact (`jump::fly::impact`). Magnetic lift (S-0607) is a seam: `jump::seized` is never true.
- **Elsewhere.** `combat::check` (S-0310 claim 8: the infantry speed fraction above 0.1 and the jumpjet-turn flag with *moving now*), `combat::range` (S-0307 claim 24: no detour limit for a jumpjet), `infantry` (S-0107: a moving jumpjet asks for *hover* or *fly* by its speed fraction, *stand* becomes *hover* when airborne), `econ::shroud::refresh_sight` and `cloak::machine::cell_movement_completed` at touchdown, `objects` (marks released when a jumpjet leaves the map or goes inside).
- **Importer.** `JumpjetSpeed`, `JumpjetClimb`, `JumpjetCrash`, `JumpjetHeight`, `JumpjetAccel`, `JumpjetWobbles`, `JumpjetDeviation`, `JumpjetNoWobbles`, `DeployToLand` (type), `[AudioVisual] DeployDir` (`general.economy.deploy_dir`), the six `[JumpjetControls]` values as *ignored* in the coverage table; the stock spellings `JumpJetAccel` and `JumpJetTurnRate` feed nothing; the importer warns for every type with a speed or turn rate of 0 (claim 25: the engine gives a take-off fraction and every cruise speed of 0 for a speed of 0 and an empty middle cruise band for a turn rate of 0, no quirk switch).
- **Quirks.** `jumpjet_bridge_early_cruise_height`, `jumpjet_lookahead_bridge`, `jumpjet_no_cell_self_destruct`, `jumpjet_step_truncation` and `jumpjet_turn_slowdown_one_sided` are consulted and tested in both settings.
- **Seams.** Magnetic lift (S-0607), the flying-object registry (Open questions), ion storms (out of scope), the infantry *air-death-end* timing.
- **Own AI.** See *Own AI (M6c: special abilities)*.
- **Client.** The click is S-0214's (a jumpjet is a free mover). The map view draws the unit at its world height, so the flight shows with no new drawing code. The lane scene `jumpjet` of `crates/app/tests/m6c_lane.rs` finds a non-balloon jumpjet type by its locomotor, orders it by a click to a cell across an obstacle (another reach region of the ground) and writes `local-lane/m6c/jumpjet-before.png`, `jumpjet-flight.png` and `jumpjet-landed.png`; it prints the states and heights by tick.

There are no provisional rules of the jumpjets left (Q-0975 to Q-0981 are answered by S-0502 revision 5 and Q-1010 to Q-1012 by revision 6 and Q-1030 and Q-1031 by revision 7 (step 1e: jumpjet-type infantry has no unchanged-destination suppression, `jump::clear_rejected` is the balloon-hover rejection of claim 28), and their interim rules are retired; Q-1090 is answered by S-0207 revision 32 and S-0502 revision 8 (step 1f, section *Simulation (M6c: destination refresh and the dock fallback)* below); a pending aerial crash start waking an idle jumpjet is claim 3's safety departure, an engine design rule with no quirk switch, as S-0312 revision 29 says). A landing request no longer sets the movement commitment (S-0517 revision 7): `jump::request_landing` only sets the request, so an idle uncommitted hover is not woken by it (S-0502 claim 3). The render layer of S-0102 revision 20 (claim 3 and its table) is not modelled: the map view draws mobile objects in the ground layer (`quirks.layer_change_on_events_only`, the table above), and the predicate it shares with S-0502 claim 4 is `jump::placement_qualifies`.


## Simulation (M6c: destination refresh and the dock fallback)

Step 1f (S-0207 revision 32 and S-0502 revision 8, answering Q-1090; S-0417 revisions 17 to 19; S-0402 revision 26). The module is `glasnost_sim::refresh`; the two runtime flags, `econ.refresh` (the destination-refresh state) and `econ.point_suppression` (the one-application movement-point suppression), are per-object hashed state (save format 45).

- **Assignment event.** `infantry::destination_assigned` (the hook of every `assign_destination`) calls `refresh::begin` for a vehicle after the order-level destination is stored and before any locomotor takes it. An assignment is effective when the destination changes or refresh is pending (`refresh::pending`; `jump::take_destination`, `jump::stop_ordered` and `teleport::take_destination` read it to bypass their unchanged-destination rejection). An unchanged assignment without refresh is ignored by the dock test; the driving locomotor's own re-installation of an unchanged point (which the engine always did) is unchanged. `refresh::finish` clears the refresh the assignment consumed, unless the dock fallback newly enabled it in that very assignment.
- **Dock test (S-0207 claim 27).** The type has the `Teleporter` flag (`raw.teleporter`), the vehicle has no warp-pending immobilization (S-0207 revision 34 Terms: `superweapons::warp_pending_or_leaving`, the accepted superweapon warp from its acceptance through the leaving wait until departure, read whatever the current locomotor; phasing in after a jump or a warp's departure, the arrival hold and the parasite's held countdown are not it; the same state leaves a teleporting vehicle's landing point unchanged under S-0207 claim 20 (b), `teleport::take_destination`), not seized (`jump::seized`, a seam), its contact is a building with `DockUnload`, the offered destination is a cell with no ground-layer vehicle (any house, the mover included) and the current locomotor is not teleporting (`teleport::is_teleporter`, false during temporary driving). With a suspended predecessor and no movement report the predecessor is restored (`econ::release::restore`), with no pending state. Otherwise `refresh::fallback` clears the driving locomotor's destination point (`drive::stop_point`) or a jumpjet's point (`jump::no_point`), clears the current behaviour, queues *enter* without an immediate start and sets both flags. `drive::take` and `jump::take_destination` then consume the suppression (`refresh::skip_point`) instead of applying the assignment's own nonempty point; an empty destination does not consume it.
- **Restoration (S-0417 claim 5, S-0207 claim 27 row 1).** `econ::release::restorable`: a temporary driving locomotor, not seized, no restoration lock (the lock of the new Terms entry is never set in our engine) and no driving movement report (`drive::movement_report`: no locomotor destination point, no heading point, the track's target, horizontally different from the actual position). The fall-back's restoration (`restore_locomotor`) follows it, so a teleporting vehicle that left its factory on the exit track keeps driving until its destination point is gone (a stop, or the arrival of a later move at its destination); before revision 19 it was restored at the first fall-back. The dock test inside an assignment judges the stored point only (`assigning`), because the assignment's own point is not installed yet. The restored teleporting locomotor then takes that same assignment (S-0207 revision 34 claim 27, *The restoring assignment's destination*): `refresh::begin` forgets the destination the suspended state last took, so `teleport::take_destination` fixes the landing point for the offer (replacing a pending earlier teleport) and the vehicle jumps at its next movement update; no driving point is installed first. Suppression already pending before the assignment still skips that application (`teleport::take_destination` calls `refresh::skip_point`).
- **Placement.** `objects::place_with` enables refresh for a vehicle around the placement fall-back (`refresh::placement_begin` and `placement_end`); the fall-back rows that clear the destination (`process::fall_back_vehicle`: unarmed, harvester) make the clear an effective empty-to-empty assignment while refresh is enabled, which consumes it and enables no suppression. A fresh vehicle has nothing to stop, so nothing is observable. The ordering before a visceroid's placement-only action-timing draw is not built (the engine has no such draw).
- **Harvest row 2 (S-0402 claim 10, revision 26).** `harvest::enter` offers the docking cell at every processing when pending refresh makes the repeat effective; an unchanged offer without refresh is not repeated (it was never), so ordinary harvesters re-search nothing new. For a qualified teleport-capable harvester that is not teleporting the offers keep qualifying (no once-per-entry limit): each installs the order-level cell destination and skips its point; the engine has no teleport movement for that state ("the resulting teleport/dock movement is outside this spec's scope").
- **Factory-exit lifetime (S-0417 revision 20 claim 16, S-0207 revision 34 claim 25).** Nothing new is built: the exit point is kept after the no-order track completion (no timer clears it); a rally, scatter or ore destination replaces it in the driving locomotor; the completion of that later goal or a stop clears it; a later eligible fall-back restores the teleporting locomotor. A factory-built chrono miner therefore drives its first leg to the ore and loads while still driving (the harvest queue and the start of loading restore nothing); it gets its teleporting locomotor back at a qualified docking assignment or a later eligible fall-back.
- **Request Q-1110** (answered by S-0207 revision 34 and S-0417 revision 20): its interim rules are retired.
- **Request Q-1130** (answered by S-0207 revision 36 and S-0402 revision 28, M6c step 1h; its interim rules are retired). The answers:
  - Point suppression applies to every nonempty vehicle assignment, a teleporting landing point included. `teleport::take_destination` tests `refresh::skip_point` before claim 20 (a)'s disabling refusal (phasing in, held, and now being erased by a temporal beam).
  - So in any state a pending suppression keeps the order-level destination, fixes no landing point and is consumed. `teleport::tick` keeps such a destination until a later effective assignment. There is no switch for it (S-0207 revision 37 Edge cases, answering Q-1131).
  - An empty assignment while a jump is pending cancels the jump. The landing point's reservation stays until the next accepted destination (claim 20 h).
  - A vehicle being erased or in the leaving stage keeps its destination under a pending suppression (S-0215 claim 2, S-0514 claim 17: `temporal::turn`, `superweapons::warp::turn`).
  - A harvester whose active locomotor teleports drops its destination, an adopted remembered cell included, at every seeking processing past the *full* test, and searches from where it stands (S-0402 claim 17, the seeking drop: `harvest::seeking`).

- **Request Q-1131** (answered by S-0207 revision 37, M6c step 1h). S-0207 revision 36 had proposed `quirks.teleport_suppression_keeps_destination` and left it to the lead; revision 37 decides: the original behaviour, with no switch (no ordinary or scripted play reaches the state), so the S-0215 claim 2 and S-0514 claim 17 exceptions apply unconditionally. The switch built in the meantime is reverted.

Tests: `claims_s0207_r36.rs` (step 1h: the Q-1130 table of S-0207 claim 27, claim 20 h, S-0215 claim 2's exception), `claims_s0402_r28.rs` (step 1h: the seeking drop), `claims_s0207_r34.rs` (step 1g: the immobilization predicate, the restoring assignment's destination), `claims_s0417_r20.rs` (step 1g: the factory-exit lifetime with a factory-built chrono miner), `claims_s0207_r32.rs` (placement leaves no pending state, the fallback and its separate consumption events, repeats through refresh, contact alone, every predicate, empty clear, jumpjet refresh, restoration versus fallback), `claim_10_row_2_offers_reach_the_dock_test` (`claims_s0402.rs`) and `claims_s0417_r19.rs` (`claim_5_restoration_needs_no_movement_report`, `claim_5_a_different_heading_point_is_a_report`).

## Simulation (M6c: deployable units)

S-0517 is built in `glasnost_sim::econ::inplace` (the in-place cycle, the firing refusals, the deploy-to-fire cell test and the drawing helpers), with hooks in `econ::deploy` (the order of S-0415 claim 1: rank 2, the deploy-fire shot of claim 2, and rank 5, the in-place cycle), `process` (the in-transition readiness, the *move* fallback, the deployed-firing rule of claim 16), `combat::check`, `combat::behave`, `combat::range`, `approach`, `orders` and `jump` (landing for deploy). The ledger is [docs/m6-claims/S-0517.md](m6-claims/S-0517.md); the tests are `claims_s0517.rs` and `claims_s0517_more.rs` (fixture `deploy_common`, built on `jump_common`) and, for the importer, `deployable_unit_keys` in `crates/convert/src/sim_econ_tests.rs`. New state: `DeployState::form` (`inplace::Form`: mobile, deploying, deployed, undeploying, the last two with their start tick); save format version 37.

- **The cycle (claims 3 to 8, F1, F2).** `inplace::process` takes the deploy step (mobile) or the undeploy step (deployed) at each processing of *unload*, then chooses 1 tick while the vehicle is in transition or landing for deploy and 450 ticks with *guard* started otherwise (`quirks.deploy_transition_discards_orders`). A transition completes at the first processing at or after t0 + (E - 1) d (deploy) or t0 + (E - 2) d (undeploy), never in the processing that began it; with *E* at most 1 (or 2) at t0 + 1; a zero stage delay stalls it (`quirks.deploy_stall_zero_delay`). *E* is the animation's end field, or its sprite's frame count (half of it for a shadow type) when the field is absent or -1. The presentation is an `Effect::Anim` (`reverse` for an undeploy); the sounds are `Sound::Deploy` and `Sound::Undeploy` (`quirks.deploy_sound_repeats`). A landing-first type above the ground requests landing (`jump::request_landing`; the request ends at touchdown); any other airborne type drops the order (`quirks.airborne_deploy_order_dropped`).
- **Orders and destinations (claims 9, 10, 15).** `econ::assign_destination` replaces a destination given to a deployed or changing vehicle by none, `inplace::enforce` clears one written directly at the start of the vehicle's turn, and *move* processed for such a vehicle queues *guard* with 1 tick. No rule starts a vehicle's unload or deploy cycle, and `DeployTime` is read and used for no vehicle.
- **Deploy fire (claim 2).** A deploy-fire vehicle without the in-place flag fires slot 0 at its own cell through the firing check (range test included) at each processing, then chooses 14 + u ticks (one ranged draw over 0 to 2, taken whatever the check answered).
- **Refusals and drawing (claims 11, 12).** A vehicle in transition answers *illegal* at the rank of S-0301 claim 9's group; a magnetic-beam weapon is refused against a vehicle in transition or an in-place deployer whose current behaviour is *unload* (`inplace::beam_refused`, used when S-0607 builds the beam). The map view hides the body in transition (`inplace::body_hidden`) and draws a deployed vehicle with its deployed image type (`inplace::deployed_image`, learned like any other image).
- **Deploy to fire (claims 13, 14).** The firing step queues *unload* for a deploy-to-fire vehicle without starting it (a deployed in-place one again with `quirks.deploy_to_fire_repacks`); the cell test (`inplace::deploy_test_cell`: flat, no building among the occupants, a land type with the buildable flag) feeds the *must deploy* answer, S-0215 claim 37 f2's adjusted reach and the primary-cell qualification of the approach search.
- **Deployed infantry (claim 16).** `process::deployed_firing`: a target in range of the weapon selected with no target stays; otherwise an S-0309 range search runs at once, an out-of-range target is replaced or dropped, and a unit left targetless falls back unless it is on *guard* or a jumpjet.
- **Importer.** `DeployingAnim` (an animation type), `UnloadingClass` (a vehicle type), `DeployTime` (read, no effect) and the animation keys `Start`, `End` and `Shadow` (`rules::AnimationType.start/end/shadow`). The quirks `deploy_sound_repeats`, `deploy_stall_zero_delay`, `deploy_to_fire_repacks`, `deploy_transition_discards_orders` and `airborne_deploy_order_dropped` are consulted and tested in both settings.
- **Own AI.** See *Own AI (M6c: special abilities)*.
- **Client.** The click is S-0214's (the cursor and the click of an in-place deployer, a deployed unit and the deploy-ready check already exist in `targeting`). The map view draws the deploying presentation (reversed for an undeploy), hides the body in transition and draws the deployed image type. The lane scene `deploy` of `crates/app/tests/m6c_lane.rs` finds a vehicle type that deploys in place, clicks it, and writes `local-lane/m6c/deploy-before.png`, `deploy-transition.png`, `deploy-deployed.png` and `deploy-packed.png`.

Revision 7 (taken in at step 1c) changes claim 5: a landing-first vehicle that is hovering with a movement commitment is rerouted by the deploy order's destination clear (`orders::apply_other` calls `jump::destination_cleared`, S-0502 claim 24) and keeps the replacement point, or keeps the preceding point and commitment when the clear is a suppressed unchanged assignment; an equal horizontal point lets the landing request produce descent, a different one makes it cruise first; with no commitment the request does not wake an idle hover (`jump::request_landing` sets no commitment; tests `claims_s0517_r7.rs`). There are no provisional rules of the deployable units left: Q-0982 is answered by S-0517 revision 6 claim 7 (a transition completes at t₀ + (E − 1) d + 1, an undeploy at t₀ + (E − 2) d + 1, counted with the base stage delay: `inplace::stage_delay` has no normalisation) and Q-0975's last use by S-0502 claim 23's last row (`inplace::landing_first_take_off`: the type's own class, winged counted as track, its own zone, the centre's reach region read for its deck, deck mode when on a deck, no height condition, bridges allowed).

## Rendering of aircraft attitude (M6c)

S-0105 revision 20 (answers Q-0950), claims 41 and 42, 3, 26 and 30, the importer rows. Presentation only; the simulation is read, never changed.

- **Rocket body (claim 41).** `render::voxel::rocket_body_pose` poses the body as *V · Rz(θ_body) · Ry(−p)* (`rocket_body_local`), *p* the missile's current single-precision pitch, used directly with F1's table trigonometry (no pitch facings), and the shadow as *V · Rz(θ_body)* whatever the pitch. The pitch reaches the viewer through `sim::rocket::drawing` (new, read-only: the pitch and the selected set's initial and final fractions as single bit patterns) and `Attitude::Rocket` on `Mobile` (`live::attitude`). The reuse classes are `RocketPitch` (`rocket_pitch_class`): zero is level; a pitch equal to RZ₅₃(Pᵢ × c) widened (c = 1.5707963267948966), compared without narrowing the product, is initial; else equal to RZ₂₄(RZ₅₃(P𝒇 × c)) is final; every other pitch is fresh. The image key (`ImageKey::RocketBody`) is the 32-step heading, the frame and the class. A fresh pitch is cached under the table step of its rotation: the pixels are those of a fresh draw and only the memory of the scene's images is bounded (the scene keeps every image added, `cached_part`).
- **Flying aircraft (claim 42).** `Attitude::Flying` carries the signed height (claim 40's a), the current speed fraction (`AirState::cur`), the sign of an unfinished aiming-facing turn (`Object::turret`, positive clockwise) and whether the body is crashing. `render::voxel::flight_attitude` gives the pitch α and bank β: both zero unless the height is strictly positive and the speed fraction strictly above `PitchSpeed` (double precision); α is `PitchAngle` then; β is `RollAngle` (negated for a counterclockwise turn) during an unfinished turn, a discrete state. Defaults 20 degrees, 30 degrees, 0.25 (S-0105 Import mapping; −1 keeps the default; degrees × binary64 π/180 toward zero, `build::rule_angle`); the three keys are `render` rows in the coverage table. The pose is *V · Rz(θ_flight) · Ry(α) · Rx(β)* (`flying_body_pose`) with α and β narrowed to single toward zero, the shadow the heading alone. Key: heading bucket, frame, pitch presence (only with a nonzero configured angle) and bank state (none for a zero configured angle), `ImageKey::FlyingBody`.
- **Heading sources (Terms, claims 3, 30).** A flying-locomotor aircraft's body and shadow take the **aiming facing** (`Mobile::turret`) rounded to 32 steps, a rocket's body and shadow the body facing, every other body its body facing. The shadow key of a flying body is its heading bucket with no pitch or bank class.
- **Not built.** Crashing flying bodies keep S-0312's attitude rules, which are not drawn; they are drawn level (heading only). A map-only scene (no game) draws aircraft level with the body facing. Slope tilt of aircraft is S-0106's. The reuse observations of MS-31 and MS-32 (a recorder of reuse against fresh draws) have no counterpart here: our cache cannot tell a reused image from a fresh one with the same pixels.

## Simulation (M6c: radiation fields)

S-0525 is built in `glasnost_sim::radiation`; the ledger is [docs/m6-claims/S-0525.md](m6-claims/S-0525.md), the tests are `claims_s0525*.rs` (fixture `rad_common`; the numbers are the spec's own scenarios RF1 to RF12) and, for the importer, `crates/convert/src/sim_radiation_tests.rs`. New state is `World::radiation` (`RadWorld`: the fields in creation order and the cell radiation as a sparse map of soft doubles); save format version 38.

- **Making and adding (claims 1 to 4).** `radiation::detonation(w, weapon, cell)` is called by every projectile detonation (`combat::projectile::explode`: once for a plain shot, once for an airburst's own detonation, once per cluster point; the nine airburst children are marked `Projectile::airburst_child` and leave none), by a death weapon's detonation (`projectile::detonate_weapon`) and so by the nuclear payload and its upward missile. A detonation on the centre cell of a live field tops it up (`top_up`: the new level from the floating-point field level, the removal of F5 from every cell of the square with `quirks.radiation_add_overdraw`, new duration, both timers restarted, the contribution of the new level over the old reach, the light set afresh with the old radius); any other cell makes a new field.
- **Floating point.** Cell radiation, contributions (F1), decay (F3), removal (F5), the level factor (F6) and the light products (F7) use the soft double in the order the formulas name; integers wrap; the distance is the table root of S-0204 with the height of each cell's centre point (`World::surface_at`).
- **Row 13 (claims 5, 6, 11, 14).** `radiation::maintenance` runs in `tick::step` after the storm and before the turn pass, newest field first: time left, a decay step when `t - start >= D_L`, a light step when `t - start >= D_I`, and the end at time left 0 (`quirks.radiation_residue` off takes the rest of the field's contribution). It takes no draws.
- **The hit (claims 8 to 10).** `radiation::hit_turn` runs in `tick::object_turn` right after `heal::turn` (the order of claim 11), for infantry, vehicles and aircraft on the map that are not airborne (`weapon::in_air` for aircraft, the 208-lepton height test for the rest) and not immune, on ticks that are multiples of the application delay: one `damage::apply` hit of trunc(ℓ x k_L) with the radiation-site warhead, no attacker, no house, the no-escape marker.
- **S-0215 claim 31 (immune deployers).** `process::immune_deployed` reads the field level of a field centred on the unit's cell (`radiation::centre_field_level`, claim 7) against R = trunc(W / 3) and fires the secondary weapon at the unit's own cell through the firing check, or drops the target; delays are the deployed-fire frame count or D_r + U(10, 20).
- **Light (claims 13 to 15).** `radiation::lights` lists the live fields' lights (centre cell, radius, intensity, tint after F7, with the snow-map red and blue of -128). The map view is built once with static cell lights and has no dynamic relight (no light change of any kind is drawn: storms and the dominator are the same), so the view draws a green ring of the field's reach instead (`Marker::Field`, our placeholder, `provisional`). The relight itself waits for a dynamic relight in `mapview` (S-0104 claim 12 gives its rules).
- **Importer.** The ten `[Radiation]` keys are `General` fields (`rad_*`, `rules::sim::General`) read by `convert::sim_combat` (`RadColor` as a byte triple with the value in force for unread components); warnings for a delay of 0 or less, a duration multiple of 0 or less, a missing radiation-site warhead, and weapons whose field has no light or level step.
- **Own AI.** See *Own AI (M6c: special abilities)*.
- **Client.** The lane scene `radiation` of `crates/app/tests/m6c_lane.rs` makes a field with an installed radiation weapon, stands three infantry units at the centre, beside it and outside the reach, and writes `local-lane/m6c/radiation-before.png`, `radiation-field.png` and `radiation-damage.png`.

There are no provisional rules of the radiation fields left: Q-0983 is answered by S-0215 revision 51 claim 31 (taken in at step 1c): the own-cell shot of a radiation-immune deployer is made in the processing that decides to fire, with no wait for a fire frame; the own-cell target is dropped in that processing after the shot, and the field exists in the same tick (test `the_own_cell_shot_is_made_in_the_deciding_processing`).

## Simulation (M6c: chrono weapons)

S-0514 is built in `glasnost_sim::temporal`; the ledger is [docs/m6-claims/S-0514.md](m6-claims/S-0514.md), the tests are `claims_s0514.rs` and `claims_s0514_more.rs` (fixture `temporal_common`) and, for the importer, `crates/convert/src/sim_temporal_tests.rs`. New state is `World::temporal` (`TemporalWorld`: the locks by target, the beams by holder, the beams a gunner vehicle carries); save format version 39. Revision 5 (taken in at step 1c) answers Q-0984.

- **Beams and locks (claims 1 to 4, 9, 10, 20 to 22).** A beam is a type's (primary weapon with a temporal warhead, `temporal::type_beam`) unless a gunner vehicle carries it (`hand_over` on the first passenger's entry, `hand_back` on the last one's exit, S-0308 claim 6); a holder has at most one attached (`beams`). `combat::projectile::special_of` selects the one special effect of a detonation by claim 20's table (takeover `MindControl`, bomb planting, electric charging, parasite attachment, temporal erasure, `IsLocomotor`, air-strike designation, `directrocker` against a vehicle target, bomb disarming, disguise-making, nuke-making: the first enabled row wins; takeover, locomotor replacement and direct rocking are selected only, their effects are seams; a selected row replaces ordinary damage and every lower row, built or not; radiation and the shared presentation stay), and for the erasing row `detonation_inner` skips the ordinary area damage and calls `temporal::deliver` with the firer and the shot's target: a cell or a non-combat object only lets the firer's old beam go, an object outside the ground render layer (`ground_layer`: a flying or jumpjet unit above the ground) does nothing, and for an infantry unit, vehicle, aircraft or building the old beam lets go, the side effects of claim 2 happen (spawner slots cleared; `quirks.erasure_refused_hit_side_effects`), the refusals of claim 3 apply (not warpable, protected, a vehicle in its factory's cell, a locked firer) and a lock is made: the first lock sets *R* = 10 x strength and switches a building off (`quirks.erasure_release_switches_on` at the last release), later locks join.
- **The erasure update (claims 7, 8, 11, 17, 18).** `temporal::turn`, called in `tick::object_turn` before behaviour processing: the sparkle on global ticks divisible by 24, a mobile target drops its target and destination, an open-topped leader beyond *W* cells lets go with no reduction (F4 with the table root; `quirks.erasure_distance_leader_only`), then *R* falls by the sum of the raw damages of the leader and the 51 newest joiners (`weapon::would_use` with no target); a target whose *R* is at most 0 completes. A mobile target skips the rest of its turn; a building skips `process::turn` and the delayed shot (`building_erased`).
- **Completion (claims 12, 13).** The warp-away animation, `garrison::erased` for a building, the direct experience gain of F5 for a trainable leader (`veterancy::gain` with the target's and the leader's own cost, `quirks.erasure_double_experience`), the ordinary credit and loss (`damage::credit`, hit with the leader as attacker), the destruction raises, then `objects::remove` (passengers die with no killer, slaves are freed) and the attackers fall back to rest.
- **Releases (claims 14 to 16).** `temporal::holder_turn` (a new cell), `process::fall_back` (revision 5: the release belongs to the common effects of S-0215 claim 13, at most once per actor per tick, and an aircraft's early exits skip it; the stop order, clearing a target and processing stop or sleep release nothing), `objects::remove` (`temporal::leaving`: a removed target idles its attackers, a removed attacker lets go), `victory::destroy_all` (`detach_for_destruction`, claim 15); clearing an attacker's target alone releases nothing (claim 16). The firing check answers *rearming* for a temporal weapon whose beam holds the target (claim 19).
- **Being erased.** `superweapons::being_erased` is now the union of a lock and the warp's leaving stage, so every reader (damage immunity of S-0302 claim 8, selection, power, radar, garrison entry, engineer entry, cloak) sees it.
- **Importer.** `Warpable` (unit and building types, absent: yes), `[General] WarpAway` and `ChronoSparkle1`, `[CombatDamage] OpenToppedWarpDistance` (absent: 5 cells); the four `erasure_*` quirks are consulted and tested in both settings.
- **Seams.** First-lock notices (claim 5), freeing controlled units and the beam release on mind control (S-0601, `temporal::control_changed`), the bunker redirection (S-0606), the building damage-fire sparkle positions (not imported).
- **Own AI.** See *Own AI (M6c: special abilities)*.
- **Client.** The lane scene `chrono` of `crates/app/tests/m6c_lane.rs` finds an erasing type and a plain vehicle type by their rules flags, clicks the target as a person would, and writes `local-lane/m6c/chrono-before.png`, `chrono-erasing.png` (the lock with the sparkle animation) and `chrono-erased.png`.

Provisional rules of the chrono weapons, each tied to a request (Q-0984 is answered by revision 5 and its three interim rows are retired: claim 20's table, claim 14's fall-back release, claims 21 and 22 for a firer with no beam and a boarding holder; the importer reads `MindControl` and `directrocker` as warhead flags for the selection). Q-1032 is answered by S-0614 revision 9 (step 1e): its interim row is retired, claim 24 is built with `quirks.parasite_suppressed_latch_strands_unit`. No interim rule is left.

## Simulation (M6c: linked weapons)

S-0518 is built in `glasnost_sim::linked`; the ledger is [docs/m6-claims/S-0518.md](m6-claims/S-0518.md), the tests are `claims_s0518.rs` and `claims_s0518_charge.rs` (fixture `linked_common`) and, for the importer (F4 included), `crates/convert/src/sim_linked_tests.rs`. New state is in `CombatState` (a master's support count, a supporter's pending beam, a building's charger list and overpowered flag, a unit's charging mark) and `Projectile::multiplier`; save format version 40.

- **Prism support (claims 1 to 10).** A building of the prism type (`[General] PrismType`) without the SAM flag and without installed upgrades takes `linked::master_path` in `behave::building_process` when its attack gets *can fire*: `linked::recruit` picks the nearest eligible supporter (claim 2's tests; F2 distance with the integer root; the reach is the slot-1 range) and returns, or the master's own delayed shot starts (`start_shot`, always, whatever the animation flag). The supporter's pending beam (`support_beam`, due max(d - 1, 0) ticks later) is drawn in `linked::supporter_turn` (an `Effect::SupportBeam`, drawing only) with its rearm countdown set to exactly the support rearm, and the firing check answers *rearming* meanwhile. `linked::master_shot` fires slot 0 through the check; a projectile launched with a positive count carries *M* (F1, `Projectile::multiplier`) and the count resets; the supplied area damage is ⌊*D*ₜ *M* / 256⌋ (`scaled_damage`). The resets of claim 10 are in `building_process`; the dropped-shot case is `quirks.prism_count_survives_dropped_shot`.
- **Charging (claims 11 to 15).** `linked::charge_hit` registers an infantry firer of an electric-charge detonation on the building it hit; `building_turn` (in the building's own turn) prunes chargers that no longer aim at an overpowerable building (any building with `quirks.stale_chargers_kept` off) and sets the overpowered state (F3); `econ::power::chargers` reads the list (S-0405 claim 8) and `weapon::choose_slot` rows 10 and 11 read the state and the flags. `linked::start_charging` runs in the *guard* and *area guard* processing of a charger with no target (revision 3, Q-0985: it queues *attack* without starting it, the idle action does not run, and the processing keeps its own delay: guard's rearm remainder, 0 for distributed fire with candidates, or the standard delay with its draw 0 to 2; area guard's rate delay plus a draw 1 to 5, divided by 6 under the close-range condition), and `linked::break_off` at the start of an infantry unit's *attack* processing (`quirks.vehicle_charge_no_break_off`): the range search of `SearchContext::BreakOff` takes no targeting-timer reset draw, the held building is neither tested nor cleared, a result becomes the target and clears the mark, nothing found leaves both. The tie between equally distant supporters goes by acquisition rank (`CombatState::acquired`, `World::acquisitions`: bumped at a building's placement into play and at an ownership change; save format 44). A charger that leaves play leaves every list at once (`objects::remove`).
- **Importer.** `[General]` `PrismType`, `PrismSupportMax`, `PrismSupportDelay`, `PrismSupportDuration`, the modifier through F4 (`convert::layers`: a layer with a general section rescales the held value by 100, `quirks.prism_modifier_reread_scale`, the start value 100) and the building type's `Overpowerable`; the art `DelayedFireDelay` existed.
- **Client.** The view draws a support beam as nine marks along it for its duration (`TransientKind::Beam`, our placeholder); the lane scene `prism` of `crates/app/tests/m6c_lane.rs` builds three prism buildings of the install's prism type, orders the first to attack an enemy vehicle and writes `local-lane/m6c/prism-before.png`, `prism-beams.png` and `prism-after.png`.
- **Own AI.** See *Own AI (M6c: special abilities)*.
- **Seams.** Being drained (S-0608), mind control and charge planting ranking above the charge effect (S-0601, S-0519), prism combinations with the SAM flag or upgrades (the spec's open question).

There are no provisional rules of the linked weapons left: Q-0985 is answered by S-0518 revision 3 (claims 3, 14 and 15, taken in at step 1c).


## Simulation (M6c: terror and strikes)

S-0519 is built in `glasnost_sim::bomb` (planted bombs, charges, suicide shots) and `glasnost_sim::strike` (air-strike designation); the ledger is [docs/m6-claims/S-0519.md](m6-claims/S-0519.md), the tests are `claims_s0519.rs`, `claims_s0519_more.rs` and `claims_s0519_strike.rs` (fixture `terror_common`) and, for the importer, `crates/convert/src/sim_terror_tests.rs`. New state: `CombatState::bomb` (planter, planter's house, plant tick), `CombatState::charge_planter` and `World::strike` (an ordered map of strikes by designator); save format version 41.

- **Planted bombs (claims 2 to 10).** A bomb-planting warhead's projectile calls `bomb::plant` instead of making area hits (`combat::projectile`); `combat::check` rank 9 answers illegal for a carrier or a clean disarm target; the bomb goes off in `bomb::turn`, called from the carrier's own turn after its processing in `tick::object_turn` (F1: tick above *t* + *D*), one detonation at the carrier with the bomb damage and warhead and the planter as attacker while it exists. A killed carrier (`combat::death`) sets it off once, an off-map carrier removes it silently, `objects::remove` drops it, a non-garrisonable building changing owner disarms it (`structures::ownership`), the planter leaving only blanks the planter. Visibility (F2) is `bomb::visible`: used by the engineer cursor row (S-0214 I2) and the client. The detonate order is `Order::Detonate` (S-0213 claim 11), issued in the planter's house's name unless `quirks.detonate_in_planter_name` is off.
- **Suicide (claim 11).** `combat::fire::shot` of a suicide weapon makes no projectile and calls `bomb::suicide`: one hit of the type's strength with the demolition warhead, ignoring defences; the death weapon is S-0312's.
- **Charges (claims 13 and 14).** `structures::entry::arrival` first calls `bomb::charge_arrival` for an infantry unit in *sabotage* whose type has the charges flag (or, with `quirks.charge_needs_type_flag` off, the veterancy ability): the entered-by raise, then the three-row table, the countdown *K* (F4) as the delayed-kill countdown with the planter recorded (its expiry hit's attacker, `combat::damage`), the rearm and the scatter. Revision 3 (taken in at step 1c, answers Q-0986): the rearm is the arrival delay of the second weapon (`combat::fire::arrival_delay`): no second weapon 1 tick; a sonic weapon its rate with no draw; the counter *k* (not *k* + 1, never advanced by arrival) below the burst count one draw over 3 to 5; otherwise the full delay of S-0301 claim 2 with its draw over 0 to 2 and no berserk halving. A spark-particle, fire-particle or railgun second weapon takes the bare-rate row only with a live effect of its kind on the walker; live effects are not built (S-0301 claim 5's gate is a seam), so such a weapon follows the later rows.
- **Strikes (claims 16 to 21).** `strike::designation` is called by the designation warhead's detonation instead of area hits; the readiness test (F5, with `quirks.airstrike_recharge_same_target_only`), `start` (one arrival cell, `n` visiting aircraft placed by the winged nearby search, each in *attack* at the cell of the object's reference point), `cancel` (ammunition, the newest 101 aircraft forget and queue *retreat*), `redirect` (equal ammunition only) and the end of the strike when the last aircraft leaves (`strike::leaving` from `objects::remove`). A change of the designator's target cancels in the tick it applies (revision 3, claim 19, Q-0986 answered): `strike::sweep` looks at every designator after each applied order (`orders::apply_pending`) and after each object's turn (`tick::turn_pass`), and every target change is counted (`CombatState::target_epoch`, bumped by `spawn::set_own_target` and its siblings), so a change to B and back to A still cancels; the designator's own turn and each designation shot look as before. A bomb with no bomb warhead ends silently, detaches and makes no blast (hut demolition stays, claim 22); a suicide with no demolition warhead is still delivered and the importer warns (claim 23). Marks are derived from the strike targets (`strike::marked`). `air::fall_back` no longer exempts a strike aircraft in *retreat*.
- **Importer.** The warhead flags `IvanBomb`, `BombDisarm`, the infantry flags `Ivan`, `Doggie`, the weapon flag `MigAttackCursor`, `BombSight`, the strike keys, the `[CombatDamage]` bomb and charge keys (`C4Delay` held as a decimal, F4 in `bomb::charge_countdown`); warnings for a timed delay below 6, a flicker rate of 0 and a designator with no strike aircraft type. The sound names and `LaserTargetColor` are presentation and not read.
- **Client.** Placeholder markers (`Marker::Bomb`, `Charge`, `Marked`) over carriers the viewer sees, charges counting down and marked buildings; the local lane scene `terror` (screenshots `terror-*.png` in `local-lane/m6c/`).

There are no interim rules of the terror and strike code left: Q-0986 is answered by S-0519 revision 3 (claims 13, 19, 22 and 23).


## Simulation (M6c: crates)

S-0520 is built in `glasnost_sim::crates`; the ledger is [docs/m6-claims/S-0520.md](m6-claims/S-0520.md), the tests are `claims_s0520.rs` and `claims_s0520_effects.rs` (fixture `crate_common`) and, for the importer, `crates/convert/src/sim_crate_tests.rs`. New state: `World::crates` (the crate places by priority 0 … 255 with cell, start tick, lifetime and the trigger mark, and the any-crate mark), the crate rules in `SimRules::crates`, `StartState::bases` (the bases setting kept after the game starts); save format version 42.

- **Placement and expiry (claims 1 to 5).** `crates::place_random` makes at most 1000 attempts, each with the two ranged requests of F5 and the single-cell float or track search (no draw); the first accepted cell (usable area, no overlay) takes the wooden or water overlay (`crates::finish`: `overlays::place_overlay`, the contents in the cell's overlay data, then the lifetime draw of S-0524's formula in the first free place). `place_trigger` (code 108, S-0524 claim 4: one search, no retry, `quirks.trigger_crate_respects_setting`), `place_death` (`combat::death::leave_play` for a destroyed vehicle with `CarriesCrate` or a building with `CrateBeneath`, crates on) and `place_at_start` (S-0412 claim 10's count, in `econ::setup::place_crates`) share it. `crates::update` (tick row 16, after the turn pass) expires the crates whose lifetime ran out, in place order, each replaced by one random placement; with crates off nothing expires unless `quirks.trigger_crate_expiry_while_disabled` lets trigger crates go.
- **Pickup (claims 6 to 11).** `crates::pickup(w, collector, cell)` is called when a walker (`walk::ask`), a driver or ship (`drive::step_check`) or a hover (`hover::step_check`) commits to a step into the cell, at a jumpjet's touchdown (`jump::crate_pickup`) and at a teleporter's landing cell (`teleport`); it returns whether a ground step is blocked (a unit power-up or a collector killed by the tag raise). Gates: the cell's overlay, the passive country, the crate-trigger overlay (event 49 by `triggers::crate_picked_up`, then the any-crate mark). Then the choice (`choose`: fixed contents, or R(1, total) and F1; a forced base unit), the conversions to money (`convert`), the crate's removal and replacement, the effect and the animation 200 leptons above the floor.
- **Effects (claims 12 to 25).** Money, unit (the unit-crate type, the forced unit, the harvester rule with `quirks.crate_harvester_list_prefix`, the random goodie vehicle, the nearby search, the money fallback with `quirks.crate_unit_fallback_unit_argument`), heal all (a healing hit of health - strength to every object of the house in the update order), cloak, explosion (`combat::projectile::explosion_anim_dry` picks the dry animation), napalm, darkness and reveal (`econ::shroud::crate_darkness`, `crate_reveal`), armour, firepower and speed (`quirks.crate_bonus_reaches_limbo`), the nuclear grant (`superweapons::charge::one_time_grant`), veteran (F7), gas (nine area damages, no resource reduction; a default GAS warhead is added to the table when the rules name none), resource (`econ::resource::crate_stage`).
- **Importer.** `[CrateRules]` `CrateRadius` (a value of exactly -1 is dropped), `CrateRegen`, `UnitCrateType`, `WoodCrateImg`, `WaterCrateImg`; `[CombatDamage]` `FlameDamage`; the nineteen `[Powerups]` keys into one table (`convert::sim_econ::crate_import`); `CrateGoodie`, `CrateTrigger`, `CrateBeneath`, `CrateBeneathIsMoney`; the sounds and the campaign-only keys are coverage rows (ignored).
- **Client.** A placeholder box (`Marker::Crate`) on every crate's cell (the view draws overlays from the map only); the power-up animations come through `Effect::Anim`; the local lane scene `crates` (screenshots `crate-*.png` in `local-lane/m6c/`).

Revision 3 (taken in at step 1c) answers Q-0987: the contents of every new random crate are 255 and a trigger's number slot is stored as S-0524 claim 4 says (tests `claims_s0520_r3.rs`); the awarded unit is first offered a direct placement at the crate cell centre that accepts only a clear entry outcome (`crates::clear_for`: `occupancy::vehicle_outcome` with the cell's own level), then the nearby search (the type's class, the *normal* zone, the empty record) whose result gets the same test once, then the money fallback; the stage-picture count of claim 24 is the flat run's *F* (12 for every supported image number), the highest stage 11 and an existing higher stage unchanged; the power-up table is layered (`convert::layers` records a reset event for every omitted key of a layer that holds a surviving `[Powerups]` section, `quirks.override_resets_keys`, and `convert::sim::econ::crate_import` applies it: weight 0, animation the lookup of NONE, water flag and argument kept).

Interim rules waiting for answers: none. Q-1033 and Q-1034 are answered by S-0520 revision 5 and S-0006 revision 20 (step 1e): the reset gives no animation without any lookup, and with `quirks.override_resets_keys` off an unmentioned entry keeps its built-in startup weight (`convert::sim_econ::crate_import`).

## Simulation (M7a: Psychic weapons)

S-0610 (revision 9; built at revision 4, revisions 5 to 8 taken in at M7a step 1b, [docs/m7-claims/step1b-m7a.md](m7-claims/step1b-m7a.md), revision 9 at step 1c, [docs/m7-claims/step1c-m7a.md](m7-claims/step1c-m7a.md)) is built in `glasnost_sim::psychic`; the ledger is [docs/m7-claims/S-0610.md](m7-claims/S-0610.md), the tests are `claims_s0610.rs`, `claims_s0610_r8.rs` and, for the importer, `psychic_type_flags` and `psychic_keys`. The berserk state is now a remaining count, `CombatState::berserk` (`Some(t)` while berserk, even at `t` ≤ 0), in place of the earlier end tick; save format version 46.

- **Berserk (claims 3 to 6, 8).** Damage stage 3 (S-0302 claim 10) calls `psychic::enter`: a unit that is not berserk takes T, drops its target and queues hunt; a berserk one only takes the new T; with `quirks.berserk_zero_duration` off a T ≤ 0 hit does nothing. `psychic::countdown_turn` runs in each countdown update (claim 5, revision 8, which answers Q-1180): in the behaviour part of the unit's update (`process::turn`), after the first per-tick start and before the due test, the processing, the per-tick search and every shot, so the expiry update fires no berserk shot and the behaviour it queues is first processed in the next update (S-0215 claim 11's late start may make it current at the end of the expiry update). Erasing updates (`temporal::turn`), warp-only updates (`warp::turn`, from the first pending update through arrival effects) and out-of-play units never reach it, so they keep `t`; in arrival hold it runs after the hold-end test. For a unit that is not an unlimited controller it comes after self-healing; S-0610 revision 9 claim 6 (answering Q-1166 question 2) says that order has no observable effect, so an engine may choose it. An unlimited controller's first per-tick start and countdown come earlier, before its overload update (S-0601 claim 16, below). At `t` ≤ 0 it ends the state, drops the target and queues guard for a human-controlled owner (every player house, AI houses included) or hunt (the neutral and special houses). The firing check answers illegal at rank 4 for a berserk-causing weapon against a psionics-immune object, and for a berserk firer against a berserk-friendly type (`psychic::firing_refused`); the bunker half is the S-0606 seam `bunker_refusal`. `targeting::berserk` now reads the state, so S-0214 claim 15 and rows G1, GC1, R and M apply.
- **Psychic sensor (claims 9 to 12).** Sensors are derived: the on-map buildings a house owns whose type has a psychic-detection radius and that are in operation (`psychic::sensors`). `psychic::intent_lines(w, viewer)` gives one line per enemy unit (the viewer's side of the alliance) whose target (first) or destination is within reach of a sensor (F1: the table root of Δx² + Δy² + Δz² from the sensor's reference point, ≤ 256 × R, no building size), visible or not unless `quirks.psychic_intent_ignores_visibility` is off. `psychic::range_ring` and `psychic::tint` are queries for presentation the view does not draw yet.
- **Psychic reveal (claims 13 to 15).** `superweapons::launch` calls `psychic::reveal`: `econ::shroud::psychic_reveal` (S-0409 claim 17's area reveal without claim 7's height test, radius from `[CombatDamage]` `PsychicRevealRadius`, 3 when absent, for the launching house and those S-0409 claim 2 lets share it) and `SwEvent::RevealSound` at the target cell's centre, which the audio crate plays (`PsychicRevealActivateSound`). No launch announcement, no radar event, no lasting sight.
- **Importer.** `BerserkFriendly` (any type), `HasRadialIndicator` (building), `[CombatDamage]` `PsychicRevealRadius` and `BerzerkAllowed` (read, no effect, claim 16), `[AudioVisual]` `PsychicRevealActivateSound` and `BerserkColor`; the ruleset schema was regenerated.
- **Client.** `Session::draw` draws the local player's intent lines: dashes moving with the real-time clock, a 3 × 3 square in the unit owner's radar colour at both ends (`house_colours`). The local lane scenes `berserk`, `sensor` and `reveal` (`crates/app/tests/m7a_lane.rs`, screenshots in `local-lane/m7a/`).

The late start (S-0215 claim 11, revision 57, taken in at M7a step 1b). An infantry unit or vehicle has one more ready-and-start chance in its update, `process::late_start`. It is called in `tick::object_turn` right after the locomotor step and the passenger sync. That is after the behaviour part (the drop rule and the per-tick search never see what it starts), before the depot retry, the parasite step, the vehicle firing step and turret, and the infantry sequence rules and firing. Its readiness test (`process::ready`) therefore counts this update's movement and reads the infantry sequence that earlier updates left. It makes a queued behaviour current; an infantry unit that is ready with neither a current nor a queued behaviour first falls back to rest, and the behaviour that queues becomes current there (revision 59, answering Q-1166 question 1; tests `claims_s0215_r59.rs`). A vehicle with both slots empty is left as it is. The behaviour it starts is processed in the unit's next update (claim 3b). Erasing, tunnel and warp-only updates return before it. Buildings keep their second start; aircraft keep their end-of-update start. A behaviour queued during an update (an arrival report, a refusal, a processing that queues without starting) is therefore current at the end of the update if the unit is then ready. The game hashes changed with it.

Seams: S-0606 (bunkered vehicles, claim 3), S-0613 (gas clouds), S-0611 and S-0612 (M7b; `superweapons::launch::yuri_seam` keeps the dominator and the genetic mutator), the berserk tint and range ring drawing, the ready-announcement cancellation (no kind-specific ready announcements yet). Interim rules waiting for answers: none (Q-1166 is answered by S-0215 revision 59 and S-0610 revision 9, step 1c). Tunnels are not built, so their exclusion of the countdown is a seam.

## Simulation (M7a: Mind control)
