S-0601 (revision 16; built at revision 5, revisions 6 to 10 taken in at M7a step 1c, [docs/m7-claims/step1c-m7a.md](m7-claims/step1c-m7a.md), revisions 11 and 12 at step 1d, [docs/m7-claims/step1d-m7a.md](m7-claims/step1d-m7a.md), revision 13 at step 1g, revisions 14 to 16 at step 1h, [docs/m7-claims/step1h-m7a.md](m7-claims/step1h-m7a.md): the overload's five spark systems are made, and with no game-wide spark type neither they nor their placement draws happen) is built in `glasnost_sim::mind`; the ledger is [docs/m7-claims/S-0601.md](m7-claims/S-0601.md), the tests are `claims_s0601.rs`, `claims_s0601_deploy.rs`, `claims_s0601_r9.rs` and, for the importer, `mind_control_type_and_weapon_fields`, `mind_control_game_wide_values` and `mind_control_warnings`. The state is `World::mind` (`MindWorld`: the controller records by object, the victim records with their controller and original owner, the permanently controlled set); it is saved and hashed. Save format version 47.

- **Controllers and the takeover (claims 1 to 7).** `mind::created` gives a new object a controller record when its type's rookie primary weapon has a takeover warhead (capacity: that weapon's damage; unlimited: its `InfiniteMindControl`). `mind::can_take` is the takeover test, `mind::firing_illegal` the firing check's refusal at rank 9 (`combat::check`), and the detonation path calls `mind::detonation` in place of damage: attack events 6 and then 44 for a tagged target, both caused by the firer (claim 5, revision 9, answering Q-1203; `triggers::judge` judges event 6 true in any raise of its code and event 44 when its value is the house number of the causing object's owner, S-0011 claim 8 revision 23; the event-44 raise looks the tag up again after the event-6 raise and is made on the tag the target then carries, or skipped when it has none: revision 12, answering Q-1150, makes this a departure with no switch, since the original faults there, and `quirks.removed_tag_attack_event` is gone; test `tags::claim_5_removed_tag_skips_event_44`), the test again at landing, then `mind::take` (capacity-1 replacement, original owner, owner change through `set_owner` with `Cause::MindControl`, which now requests the capture or loss announcement for a building on takeover and release, S-0509 claim 12 and S-0701 claim 39, guard reset, link timer, take-over sound).
- **The computer player's fate (claim 8).** `mind::fate` runs for a neutral or special house only (no player house is computer-controlled here): one draw on the simulation RNG over the table the marks select (claim 8's design rule, revision 9, answering Q-1202: an absent mark never selects its table, the others keep their places; the importer warns once for each absent mark when a fate table is non-empty), then hunt. The grinder fate is built with S-0603 (M7a step 3.6: `grinder::grinder_fate`, the nearest own recycling building by ρ₃) and the bio-reactor fate with S-0605 (step 3.5); a failed assignment and the team fate fall back to hunt (teams are not built).
- **Release (claims 9 to 12).** `mind::release` (sound, owner back, guard), `release_all` most recent first from death, removal, erasing hits, trigger owner changes and a controller building out of operation in its own turn (`mind::building_turn`). The quirk switches `quirks.mind_control_owner_change_keeps_victims` (`mind::owner_changed`), `quirks.dying_victim_holds_slot` (`mind::killed`) and `quirks.deploy_makes_control_permanent` (`mind::converted`) are wired; each is tested in both positions.
- **Permanent control and the original owner (claims 13 to 15).** `mind::dominate` (the S-0612 caller is not built), `mind::original_owner` (read by S-0414 claim 5's defeat), deploying through `mind::converted`. Claim 15 (revision 9, answering Q-1200): a vehicle controlled by a controller is not deploy-ready to deploy into a construction yard (`econ::deploy::deploy_ready` reads `mind::controlled_by`, so a permanently controlled one deploys, for the dominating house); the deploy command skips it (`targeting::key_command`), a queued deploy does not survive the takeover's guard reset, and after release it deploys. The refusal cursor of S-0214 table VD row VD2 is not built (`targeting::vehicle_deploy` has no deploys-into row): a seam.
- **Overload (claim 16).** `mind::overload_turn` runs in the unlimited controller's own turn (revision 9, answering Q-1201): `tick::object_turn` runs the controller's `process::head` (the first per-tick start and the berserk countdown) and then the overload update, before sinking, self-healing, radiation, the fall, the temporal step and `process::tail` (the rest of behaviour processing), movement and firing. Moving the controller's first start and countdown ahead of its self-healing is our engine's choice: S-0610 claim 6 and S-0314 claim 9 leave that order to the engine. It runs in exactly the countdown's updates: not while being erased (`temporal::erased`), not in warp-only updates, not out of play. A lethal pulse ends the update. the highlight count, F2's level, the demolition-warhead hit, the overload sound once per run, the spark and rocking draws (cosmetic effects only: no particle systems or rocking yet).
- **Presentation (claims 17 and 18).** `mind::link_lines` and `mind::ring` are queries. `Session::draw` draws the link lines in the controller house's colour while the timer runs or the controller, its victim or its carrying transport is selected, and a stand-in ellipse for the control and permanent rings (the ring animations are not drawn). The control pips of S-0111 claim 13 are not drawn yet (`mind::highlight` is ready).
- **Transports.** A vehicle transport takes neither a controlled unit nor a controller holding victims (S-0507 claim 4); a controlled unit that boards is released.
- **Importer.** `InfiniteMindControl` (weapons); `MindClearedSound`, `LeptonMindControlOffset`, `MindControlRingOffset` and `AlternateFLH0` to `AlternateFLH4` (types and art); `[AudioVisual]` take-over, release and overload sounds; `[CombatDamage]` controlled and permanently controlled animation types, the link-line frames, the overload count, damage and frames lists and the `AICapture*` tables and marks. Warnings: a takeover weapon outside the primary slot with no primary, a takeover primary with damage ≤ 0 that is not unlimited, overload lists shorter than the count list.
- **Client and lane.** The local lane scenes `mind` and `overload` (`crates/app/tests/m7a_lane.rs`, screenshots `local-lane/m7a/mind-*.png`). AI lane (map 8, seed 7, Yuri house 1): sides 0,2: Allied won at 16 266, 1 takeover, one controller built, no brain orders; sides 1,2: Soviet won at 37 607, 5 brain orders, 13 takeovers, at most 4 held at once, 9 controllers.

Seams: teams (the grinder and bio-reactor fates are built with S-0603 and S-0605 at M7a steps 3.6 and 3.5), S-0612 (domination's caller), S-0608 (`drain_link_formed`), S-0606 (bunkered vehicles), the spark particle systems and the rocking, the ring animation art, the control pips. Interim rules waiting for answers: none (Q-1200 to Q-1203 are answered by revision 9, step 1c; Q-1150 by revision 12, step 1d, which withdrew the switch `quirks.removed_tag_attack_event` that step 1c had wired). Also a seam: the deploy refusal cursor (S-0214 table VD row VD2).


## Simulation (M7a: Gattling spin-up)

S-0609 (revision 5; built at revision 3, revision 4 taken in at M7a step 1c, [docs/m7-claims/step1c-m7a.md](m7-claims/step1c-m7a.md), revision 5 at step 1d, [docs/m7-claims/step1d-m7a.md](m7-claims/step1d-m7a.md)) is built in `glasnost_sim::gattling`; the ledger is [docs/m7-claims/S-0609.md](m7-claims/S-0609.md), the tests are `claims_s0609.rs`, the audio test `spin_sound_draws` and, for the importer, `gattling_spin_settings`. The state is the spin value and gattling stage (`CombatState::spin`, `gattling_stage`), the animation counter (`CombatState::anim_frame`) and the elapsed-tick count (`Behaviours::elapsed`); all are saved and hashed. Save format version 48.

- **Spin-up and spin-down (claims 1 to 4, F1 to F3).** `gattling::spin_up` and `spin_down` apply F1 and F2 with the normal or, while the object is elite, the elite thresholds (an absent elite threshold is 0). The rise test reads the value before the update; there is no clamp. `quirks.gattling_zero_spin_down_empties` (a spin-down of amount 0 empties the value) is wired and tested in both positions.
- **Vehicles (claims 5, 6, 12).** `combat::behave` calls `gattling::vehicle_step` once per firing step with the firing check's answer for slot 0 (no target or an empty slot 0 spins down; the deploy-to-fire exit makes no update). A passenger or an object being erased takes no firing step, so its spin is frozen.
- **Buildings (claims 7 to 9, F4, F5).** `process` counts the elapsed ticks of a gattling building (`gattling::elapse`); its attack processing calls `building_attack` with the answer and multiplier c, a SAM building makes no update, and guard, sticky and area guard processings call `building_guard` (spin-down by c). `gattling::building_turn` runs in the building's own turn after its processing (`tick.rs`): the passive spin-down outside attack more than G + 5 ticks after the last shot, and the animation counter. **Buildings now import `TurretCount`** (S-0308 Import mapping, type section; `BUILDING_FIELDS`), so a gattling building is a multi-weapon type that chooses its weapon by stage.
- **Resets (claim 11).** Boarding resets value, stage and elapsed count (`transport::reset_gattling_spin`, which was a seam); each successful temporal lock, a joining one included, gives one spin-down (`temporal`). Entering an absorbing building resets too (S-0605 claim 7, built at M7a step 3.5).
- **Sound (claim 13).** The simulation reports each update as `Effect::Spin` (`SpinEvent::Up { raised, weapon }` with the slot-2s′ weapon after elite replacement, `Down`, `Left` when the object leaves the map) and marks a gattling firer's shots so they play no per-shot report. The audio crate keeps the playing mark and takes the draws on the unsynchronised stream (`Audio::spin`); an unresolved report list counts as empty. Nothing of this reaches game state.
- **Animation (claim 14).** `anim_frame` advances for a vehicle once per completed firing step with value at least 1, for a building once per tick with positive value after its processing. The original's drawing-only double advance outside attack is not reproduced. The client reads it for a building's turret frame and a vehicle's (`render::voxel::vehicle_turret_frame`).
- **Importer.** `WeaponStages`, `RateUp`, `RateDown`, `Stage1` to `Stage6` and `EliteStage1` to `EliteStage6`, read per layer while the gattling flag is set and the count is at least 2 (`sim_combat::spin_settings`); warnings for a count above 6, negative settings, a final count below 2 after an earlier layer set the thresholds (at the layer that reduced it; a count raised back to 2 or more gives no warning) and empty stage slots 2s.
- **Settings outside the domain (revision 4, answering Q-1220; revision 5, answering Q-1151).** Engine rules with no quirk switch: negative stage counts, amounts and thresholds are used as 0, with an importer warning for each; spin products, sums and the elapsed-tick count saturate at 2³¹ − 1 (`saturation_at_the_32_bit_bound`); and the stage count as the layers finally leave it governs, so a type a later layer lowered below 2 stages uses no thresholds (all 0, though the importer still writes the held rows: the cap is 0, the value never grows, the stage stays 0; `layered_reduction_below_2_uses_no_thresholds`), and the importer warns about the reducing layer (`gattling_spin_settings`).
- **Client and lane.** The local lane scene `gattling` (`crates/app/tests/m7a_lane.rs`, screenshots `local-lane/m7a/gattling-*.png`): a gattling vehicle ordered at an enemy vehicle climbs to its top stage (stage 1 after 200 ticks, stage 2 after 400) and, its target removed, falls back to 0 within 23 ticks; a gattling building beside an enemy vehicle climbs to its top stage through guard and attack. AI lane (map 8, seed 7, Yuri house 1, after merging M7a step 1b): sides 0,2: Allied won at 15 708; the Yuri house had 2 gattling types, ordered 2 gattling defences, kept 12 spins, never had a gattling at its top stage, built one controller and took nothing. Sides 1,2: Soviet won at 13 301; 2 gattling types, 1 defence ordered, 210 spins kept, 218 house-ticks at the top stage, 1 takeover. Replays and mid-game saves matched in both.

Seams: none (the absorption reset is built with S-0605 at M7a step 3.5). Interim rules waiting for answers: none (Q-1220 is answered by revision 4, Q-1151 by revision 5).

## Simulation (M7a: gas clouds)

S-0613 (revision 5 since M7a step 1e, [step1e-m7a.md](m7-claims/step1e-m7a.md); built at revision 4; S-0202 revision 29, S-0010 revision 18, S-0109 revision 29 and S-0203 revision 36, which restate its F9, its loading-time Edge case and the shared system's place, were taken in at step 1f with no behaviour change, [step1f-m7a.md](m7-claims/step1f-m7a.md)) is built in `glasnost_sim::gas`; the ledger is [docs/m7-claims/S-0613.md](m7-claims/S-0613.md), the tests are `claims_s0613.rs`, the unit tests in `gas.rs` and, for the importer, `sim_gas_tests.rs`. The state is `World::gas` (`GasWorld`: the particle systems with their particles, the shared system's object number and the virus-death animations); all of it is saved and hashed. Save format version 49.

- **Particle systems as world objects (claims 1, 4, 6, 17).** A particle system takes an object number and a place in the update order; `tick::object_turn` runs `gas::system_turn` for systems and `gas::anim_turn` for virus-death animations, after projectiles. The shared gas system (`GasCloudSys`) is the last object of the game-start step to enter play (`load::new_game`, just before the ore-twinkle setup): after the map-placed objects, the starting units, the crates and every tile or cell animation of loading, so it is appended to the update order after all of them and takes the object number after them (map objects start at 1; since step 1e, in a game with a shared system, every object created in play has a number one higher than before, and so does every particle, which flips F8's parity for them). A particle requested while loading (a μ = 0 tile or cell animation) meets no shared system: its creation draws are taken and no particle is made (Edge cases, a departure; the importer warns for a cell animation, theater tile animations are not seen by the rules import, a seam). A warhead naming a particle system creates it at the end of its detonation (`combat::area::detonate`, S-0303 claim 20) with its first particle. A system of every kind is kept and takes its turns (claim 4); since step 1h the smoke, fire, spark and railgun kinds keep their first particle and run their own turns (S-0616, section below; Q-1243 answered). After the kind's turn work, every system's lifetime goes down by 1 whatever its value (−1 goes on to −2 and below), it is marked to end when the result is exactly 0, and a marked empty system leaves play in the same turn (claim 17). A shared system that leaves play is not created again: later requests take their draws and make no particle (a departure; the importer warns when its lifetime is positive). The gate and lane cloud counters count gas-kind systems only (`gas::is_cloud`).
- **Virus death (claims 2, 3).** `combat::death::infantry_death` calls `gas::virus_death` for row 8 of S-0107 claim 17 and for the extra cases (a not-human type, or a parachuting unit hit by a kind-8 warhead). The animation is a narrow one-pass playback (`VirusAnim`) whose middle event puts its particles into the shared system; the extra cases deliver their middle event at once and one more particle. S-0109's middle event (`load::middle_event`) now delivers its particles into the shared system too.
- **Particles (claims 5 to 15, F1 to F8).** Creation with the S-0202 creation draws (E = (E_max + u) mod 65536, F9); the update pass newest first (on even ticks drift, vertical speed and deflection, then damage, stage and lifetime, claim 18), then the second pass (moves, removals, successors newest dead first). F3's deflection runs in soft single and double precision (`glasnost_num`) through the S-0106 table C4 ramp matrices and is stored as bits. Gas hits go through `combat::damage::apply` with no attacker and the system's house (a berserk warhead therefore works, S-0610's seam) and report `Effect::GasHit`. `quirks.wind_southeast_short` is wired.
- **Importer.** Particle fields `MaxDC`, `Damage`, `Warhead`, `StartFrame`, `StartStateAI`, `EndStateAI`, `StateAIAdvance`, `DeleteOnStateLimit`, `NextParticle`, `NextParticleOffset`, `WindEffect`, `Translucency`; particle-system `BehavesLike`, `HoldsWhat` (absent from a section that is read: the empty-named type, which `layers.rs` registers by reference; a section never read: none; S-0613 revision 6, step 1g), `Lifetime`; General `InfantryVirus`, `WindDirection`. Warnings for a virus animation without a particle type, a wind effect above 10, a wind direction outside 0 to 7 when a type has a wind effect, stage advances of 0 or −1, a shared gas system with a positive lifetime (claim 17), and an overlay cell animation with μ = 0 that requests particles while loading (Edge cases; only when the art lookup ran, since μ needs it).
- **Client.** Every gas particle is drawn as a green placeholder cloud (`Marker::Gas`); claim 16's art, translucency and frame-rate cutoff are not drawn yet. The live view counts gas hits and the most particles at once. Local lane figures from step 3.4, not re-run at step 1e (particles now live longer, F9): scene `gas` (`crates/app/tests/m7a_lane.rs`, screenshots `local-lane/m7a/gas-*.png`): a virus unit's kill leaves the virus animation and up to 3 clouds at once, which deal 2 hits; the install's virus warhead names no particle system. AI lane (map 8, seed 7, Yuri house 1): sides 0,2: Allied won at 16 254; the Yuri house had 1 virus unit; 3 clouds, 25 gas hits, at most 6 particles. Sides 1,2: Soviet won at 13 301 (as before); 1 virus unit, no clouds. Replays and mid-game saves matched in both.
- **Gate.** The duel's gattling top-stage weapon fires a kind-8 warhead naming a cloud system, with a virus animation and the shared system in the fixture; the run counts gas clouds and gas hits (step 3.4: seed 2 had 184 clouds and 274 hits; over seeds 1 to 16 the gas minima were 16 clouds and 26 hits). The change moved some earlier minima (old to new): units made berserk 20 to 25, intent ticks 1 982 to 1 942, slaves at work 6 312 to 6 812, stage rises 9 to 2 (seed 6), top-stage ticks 1 396 to 331, takeover orders 6 to 5, takeovers 10 to 10. A buildable virus infantry type instead lowered more of them (it takes a share of the barracks). At step 1e (revision 5: the shared system's new place, E = E_max + u, the count-down of every lifetime) only `duel-m7a.txt` moved; the goldens were regenerated. Over seeds 1 to 16 the gas minima are now 32 clouds and 141 hits (medians 182 and 717; the clouds live longer with F9). Other minima (step 3.4 to step 1e): units made berserk 25 to 19, intent ticks 1 942 to 1 776, slaves at work 6 812 to 5 537, stage rises 2 to 4, top-stage ticks 331 to 711, takeover orders 5 to 7, takeovers 10 to 8; psychic sensor orders stay 2 in every seed (the known exception).

Seams: claim 16 (drawing detail); laser-fence sections in F3's obstacle test count as inactive; the importer's loading-time warning for theater tile animations. Revisions 7 and 8 (step 1h): owner removal marks a system of every kind, and the shared system stands at S-0616's constant (2688, 2688, 0). Q-1153 and Q-1243 are answered by S-0616 (step 1h). Revision 9 (step 1j): claim 7's one successor is made for a dead particle of any kind in a gas system, the system's kind deciding (S-0616 claim 3; Q-1155 answered). Revision 10 (step 1j): that successor takes the dead particle's speed as well as its velocity, and nothing else (`gas::second_pass`). Q-1240, Q-1241, Q-1242 and Q-1244 are answered by revision 5 (step 1e).

## Simulation (M7a: particle systems)

S-0616 (revision 4, taken in at M7a step 1h, [step1h-m7a.md](m7-claims/step1h-m7a.md)) is built in `glasnost_sim::particles`, only as far as built specs need it; the ledger is [docs/m7-claims/S-0616.md](m7-claims/S-0616.md), the tests are `claims_s0616.rs`, the unit tests in `particles.rs`, the creator tests in `claims_s0302.rs`, `claims_s0601.rs` and `claims_s0614.rs` and, for the importer, `particle_system_kind_fields`, `particle_system_kind_warnings`, `default_spark_system_absent_warns` and `particle_quirks_load_in_both_positions`. The systems live with the gas systems in `World::gas`; `gas::system_turn` dispatches on the system's kind and each particle updates and moves by its own kind (claim 3). The kept state (speed, flight direction, spark velocity, fire step, emission timer, spark counter, own stage-advance value, target point, owner object) is saved and hashed. Save format version 52 (53 since step 1j, for the depot memory of S-0314 revision 25). Revision 5 (step 1i) and revision 6 (step 1j, [step1j-m7a.md](m7-claims/step1j-m7a.md)) are taken in: since revision 6 successors follow the system's kind, not the particle's (claim 3; Q-1155 answered): one successor in a gas system (S-0613 claim 7, revision 9), claim 8's two in a smoke system, none in a fire, spark or railgun system, for a dead particle of any kind (`claim_3_successors_follow_the_system_kind`). Revision 7 (step 1j): a gas system's successor takes the dead particle's speed and velocity (S-0613 claim 7, revision 10).

- **Creators built (claim 1).** A warhead's system of any kind keeps its first particle (`gas::warhead_system`). The damage smoke of S-0302 claim 13 is a smoke system owned by the damaged object at its position plus the type's damage-smoke displacement (`particles::damage_smoke`, `Object::combat.damage_smoke`); a hit above the yellow ratio marks it and the object keeps it until it leaves play (Q-1153 answered). The overload sparks of S-0601 claim 16 and the drone delivery sparks of S-0614 claim 9 are systems of the game-wide spark type (`particles::spark_system`; none without `[CombatDamage] DefaultSparkSystem`, which the importer warns about).
- **Owner removal (claim 4).** `objects::remove` calls `particles::owner_removed`: every system the object owns loses it and is marked.
- **Kinds (claims 5 to 19, F1 to F4, F8 to F13).** Smoke: update newest first, removal pass with successors (claim 8, F3), move with the smoke wind table and the deck stop (F2), emission and the timer's cut-off (claim 9, F4). Fire: update and step (F8), move, hits on the objects of the cell (F9) with no attacking house. Spark: the burst (F10) and the motion and contact with double gravity (F11). Railgun: the line (F12) once, then marked. The normalised stage-advance value (F13) is formed at creation. Every listed draw is taken, the drawn-only ones included. Single and double precision run in `glasnost_num`.
- **Quirks.** `fire_particle_no_house`, `smoke_ignores_particle_cap`, `spark_double_gravity`, `smoke_deck_extra_update` (each tested both ways) and `flame_sweep_facing` (seam: loads in both positions, changes nothing yet).

Seams: `effect_weapon_emitter` (fire, spark and railgun emitters of effect weapons, the let-go events, S-0301 claim 5's live-effect gate, the flame sweep F5, the swing F6, the insertion F7, a system's target object), `barrel_particle_system` (S-0303 claim 19 takes its draw only), `destruction_smoke`, `refinery_smoke`, `natural_smoke` (with S-0516's cloak removal and uncloak rule), `electric_bolt_sparks`, `damage_sparks`, `deactivated_sparks`, `voxel_animation_system`, the trigger actions (S-0011 codes 88 and 89), and the drawing of every kind. No object is inside a tunnel, so claim 9's tunnel test always passes. No interim rule is waiting (Q-1155 answered by revision 6, step 1j). Gate (step 1h, after merging main with S-0603, seeds 1 to 16): every minimum and median is the same as on main before the merge (berserk orders 9/19, units made berserk 27/47, sensor orders 2/2, the known exception, intent ticks 1 869/3 316, reveal orders 6/11, reveals fired 6/11, slave-miner buildings 3/5, slave ticks 1 274/7 193, gattling stage rises 6/14, top-stage ticks 448/2 373, takeover orders 3/14, takeovers 7/15, gas clouds 26/147, gas hits 39/487, absorber entry orders 6/15, infantry absorbed 5/8, grinder entry orders 4/8, units ground 4/8); the duel's seed stays 3 (12 000 ticks). The goldens were regenerated.

## Simulation (M7a: Infantry absorber)

S-0605 (revision 2 since step 1g) is built in `glasnost_sim::structures::absorb`; the ledger is [docs/m7-claims/S-0605.md](m7-claims/S-0605.md), the tests are `claims_s0605.rs`, two pairs in `pairs_m7a.rs` (a controlled enemy goes into an absorber and comes out an enemy; a released neutral unit takes the bio-reactor fate), the audio test `bio_reactor_sounds` and, for the importer, `absorber_fields`. The state is in `StructState`: a building's passengers in the passenger order, front (the most recent entrant) first (`absorbed`), a passenger's building (`absorbed_in`) the building an actor waits in line for (`waiting`) and the enter-nearest instruction for absorbing buildings (`enter_nearest`, claim 16); an entrant's contact is the ordinary contact field. All of it is saved and hashed. Save format version 51.

- **Acceptance and capacity (claims 1, 2, F1).** `absorb::answer` is the docking answer of S-0214 rows x3, x4, q2 and q3 for an absorbing building (`targeting` reads it, so the *enter* cursor and click follow it): alliance both ways, switched on, the right kind, not balloon-hover, not a controller holding a victim, then F1 (n + 1 ≤ c and size ≤ L, L compared in soft floating point). A building type carries passengers for S-0214 rows x and q when its capacity is above 0; row I13's "carries no passengers" is a capacity equal to 0, so a negative capacity meets neither (claim 1; since S-0214 revision 40, step 1h, for any building type, `targeting::building_passengers`).
- **Contact and waiting in line (claims 3 to 5, 17, F2).** `absorb::order_entered` runs when an *enter* order on an absorbing building is applied (`orders.rs`, after the order's ordinary effects), and the fate of claim 16 and the retry run the same rule (`seek_contact`): a building that holds any contact puts the actor in line with its destination cleared; otherwise it asks for contact (alliance both ways, health above 0, a free link place). Not granted, the actor does not wait and keeps the building as its destination; granted, it asks to load, and a refused loading (switched off) gives the contact up again, the destination kept. `absorb::waiting_turn` is the per-tick retry in the actor's own turn after its movement (`tick.rs`, where the depot retry is; nothing while phasing in): granted and accepted, *enter* queued with an immediate start and the building as destination; refused, contact given up, the none behaviour queued (no-op) and the destination cleared. Link places are max(NumberOfDocks, 1) (`absorb::link_places`). `absorb::process_enter` repeats the loading request at each *enter* processing (`process.rs`); a refusal gives up the contact and falls back to rest with the destination and walk kept, so the entrant walks on and never goes in (scenario A6). A waiting actor's first *enter* processing is the approach (`absorb::approach`): S-0212 claim 25's nearby search from the cell of the building's reference point with table T5's waiting absorber row (the step-off settings, shared as `path::NearbySettings::own_class`: own class with winged as track, own zone, the centre's reach region read for the deck, deck mode on a bridge deck, no height, further or overlay test, no reference cell, so position *N* mod *n*), *move* started at once, the waiting kept; with no cell found it falls back to rest in that processing and keeps waiting (S-0605 revision 5, Q-1152 answered at step 1i; `claim_4_approach_one_free_cell`, `claim_4_approach_tie_by_tick`, `claim_4_approach_no_cell`). Since step 1j (revision 6) a vehicle ordered in to a vehicle absorber always waits in line, even at an empty building: no destination and no contact at the order, then the approach and the retry (`absorb::seek_contact`, `claim_3_vehicle_always_waits_in_line`); since revision 7 claim 16's fate puts a vehicle victim in line the same way, and it sends no contact request in the release tick (`StructState::fate_tick`, `claim_3_vehicle_fate_waits_in_line`); setting up a wait forgets any remembered entry object (in our engine only S-0314 claim 23's depot is one), and an enter order on an absorbing building gives none. Without contact and not waiting the processing falls back at once. A contact ends only in claim 17's cases; `quirks.absorber_stale_contact_kept` (default on) keeps a contact held outside *enter* (off: given up in the holder's turn, the old Q-1267 rule), tested both ways.
- **Going in (claims 6 to 8).** `absorb::arrival` (`walk.rs`) for infantry at the end of a walking step in a cell of the building: one entry raise on the building's tag (S-0524 claim 12), unconditional; without contact nothing more; then the controlled entrant's release (S-0601 claim 9, the release sound before the entry sound), off the map, front passenger, power due when the extra output is above 0, the gattling reset (S-0609 claim 11, `gattling::reset`), the entry sound. `absorb::vehicle_arrival` (`drive.rs`, `hover.rs`) is claim 8's vehicle absorber: the full test on arrival, refused: destination cleared, *guard* queued, a forced scatter. `quirks.absorber_contact_no_capacity_recheck` (default on) is wired and tested both ways.
- **Inside and owners (claims 9, 10).** Passengers are off the map, keep their owner and are left out of the crate infantry count (`crates::infantry_count`); their power (S-0405 claim 1, `econ::power`) follows the building to a new owner.
- **Release (claims 11 to 13, F3).** The deploy click on an absorbing building with passengers gives *unload* (S-0214 row B5 and table M); `absorb::process_unload` lets out the front passenger per processing by S-0507 claim 10's exit search from the top-left cell, rear direction q = (facing + 4) mod 8, exit facing 8192 × e, then a move two cells out; no exit puts the passenger back in front and blocks the others; an empty processing queues *guard* and waits 1 tick. `quirks.absorber_release_second_round_skips_rear` and `quirks.absorber_release_bridge_ends_search` are wired and tested both ways. A voluntary release clears the enter-nearest instruction for a human-controlled owner only (claim 13).
- **Other ways out (claims 14, 15).** Destruction (`absorb::destroyed`, from `combat::death`, S-0312 claim 24: front first, one per foundation cell, the sale's centre placement with one draw per open cell (claim 14); no crew and killing damage that ignores defences kills them), sale (`absorb::sold`, S-0408 claim 8), erasure (`absorb::erased`, S-0514 claim 12: deleted), removal inside (`absorb::removing`: n goes down; `quirks.absorber_removal_keeps_stale_power`, default on, makes no power due so the totals stay stale until something else makes them due; off: power due; tested both ways).
- **Seams filled.** The bio-reactor fate of S-0601 claim 8 (`absorb::bio_reactor_fate`, from `mind::fate`, claim 16: the unit takes the enter-nearest instruction; candidates in the owner's absorber list, the house's holding order `econ.acquired`, each asked claim 1's test; the smallest ρ₃ to the reference point wins, ties to the later one in the list; found: *enter* queued without a start and the destination assigned, which applies claim 3; none: instruction cleared, *hunt*). The instruction repeats the search at every *guard* and *area guard* processing (`absorb::instruction_turn` from `process.rs`, *D*<sub>r</sub>, no draw; S-0215 claim 24 and claim 26 row 3; since S-0215 revision 60, step 1h, answering Q-1154, waiting in line for the building counts as already bound); `quirks.enter_nearest_survives_absorption` (default on) keeps it through an entry, so a neutral passenger let out walks back in (off: entry clears it), tested both ways; the gattling reset of S-0609 claim 11; the absorber entry raise of `mp_triggers`.
- **Importer.** Buildings `InfantryAbsorb`, `UnitAbsorb`, `Passengers`, `SizeLimit` (and the existing `NumberOfDocks`, `ExtraPower`); `[AudioVisual] EnterBioReactorSound` and `LeaveBioReactorSound`, played by the audio crate (`Sound::EnterBioReactor` and `Sound::LeaveBioReactor` reports).
- **Client and lane.** The local lane scene `absorber` (`crates/app/tests/m7a_lane.rs`, screenshots `local-lane/m7a/absorber-*.png`): three infantry clicked onto the install's infantry absorber get contact one at a time (one link place; the others wait in line), the first is inside after 95 ticks, all three after 322, the house's output rises; the deploy click empties it in 31 ticks and the output goes back. The passenger pips (S-0111 claim 12) and the building's passenger animation (S-0108 claim 17) are not drawn. AI lane (map 8, seed 7, Yuri house 1): sides 0,2: Allied won at 20 112; the Yuri house had 3 absorbing buildings, gave 4 entry orders, 1 infantry went in. Sides 1,2: Soviet won at 13 305; 2 absorbing buildings, 1 entry order, none went in. Replays and mid-game saves matched in both.
- **Gate.** Alpha's power plant in the M7a duel is BIOP, an infantry absorber (power 50, capacity 5, size limit 1, extra power 40; the fixture's POWR is Beta's only); the run counts entry orders and infantry that went in, and saves and loads at the first tick a passenger is inside. Seed 2: 34 entry orders, 10 absorbed. Minimum / median over seeds 1 to 16 (after merging main at 75350df): berserk orders 6/13, units made berserk 22/29, sensor orders 2/2 (the known exception), intent ticks 1 912/3 740, reveal orders 7/12, reveals fired 7/12, slave-miner buildings 5/8, slave ticks 2 765/8 338, gattling stage rises 4/13, top-stage ticks 868/2 279, takeover orders 7/17, takeovers 11/16, gas clouds 36/110, gas hits 144/404, absorber entry orders 12/20, infantry absorbed 3/7. Every scenario's golden moved from tick 1 (new saved state); the goldens were regenerated. At step 1g (S-0605 revision 2; save format 51, AI game format 20 with the stalled-entrant re-send) the minimum / median over seeds 1 to 16: berserk orders 6/17, units made berserk 20/28, sensor orders 2/2 (the known exception), intent ticks 1 770/3 224, reveal orders 7/12, reveals fired 7/12, slave-miner buildings 6/8, slave ticks 286/6 016, gattling stage rises 4/13, top-stage ticks 461/2 035, takeover orders 3/13, takeovers 10/16, gas clouds 14/100, gas hits 15/404, absorber entry orders 10/18, infantry absorbed 4/8. Without the re-send the infantry-absorbed minimum was 2 (seeds 11, 13 and 14: a contact kept outside *enter*, claim 17). The goldens were regenerated.

Seams: the passenger pips and animation (drawing); teams are still a seam of S-0601 claim 8 (the grinder fate is built with S-0603). Q-1260 to Q-1267 are answered by revision 2 (step 1g). Q-1154 is answered by S-0215 revision 60 (step 1h). Q-1152 is answered by S-0605 revision 5 (step 1i). Revisions 6 and 7 at step 1j. Q-1159 is open: claim 3's example of a vehicle not ready to change, one still driving from an earlier order, conflicts with S-0215 claim 11, under which a moving actor whose queued behaviour is *enter* is ready. Q-1159 interim: S-0215 claim 11 decides, so a driving vehicle makes *enter* current and takes the approach step. Save format 53 since step 1j.

## Simulation (M7a: Grinder)

S-0603 (revision 2) is built in `glasnost_sim::structures::grinder`; the ledger is [docs/m7-claims/S-0603.md](m7-claims/S-0603.md), the tests are `claims_s0603.rs`, two pairs in `pairs_m7a.rs` (a controlled enemy is ground for its controller's house; a released neutral unit takes the grinder fate), the audio test `grinder_consumption_sounds` and, for the importer, `grinder_fields`. No state of its own: an entrant is a unit in *eaten* with the building as its destination, so save format 50 is unchanged.

- **Order and answer (claims 1, 2).** `grinder::answer` is the docking answer of S-0214 table V rows n1 and n2 and row I26 (`targeting` reads it, so the *recycle* cursor and click follow it): one-way alliance, not under construction or being sold, a link place (F2), the naval match, not a balloon, not a loaded vehicle or a controller with a victim, switched on. A building that also absorbs answers by S-0605 claim 1.
- **Approach and consumption (claims 3 to 7).** No contact and no link place; `grinder::process_eaten` is S-0215 claim 28's table for *eaten*. Infantry is consumed at the end of a walking step in a foundation cell (`walk.rs`), a vehicle at the cell-arrival update of the first foundation cell (`drive.rs`, `hover.rs`; occupancy rows VP6 and VP7). The consumption tick: the entry raise, `Effect::Consumed`, the payments, the parasite's host-consumed rule, the entrant's destroyed-by-anything raise, the silent removal of passengers (two levels) and `objects::remove`. `quirks.grinder_no_arrival_recheck` is wired and tested both ways.
- **Payments (claims 8 to 11).** Each consumed object pays the building's owner `sell::refund` of its type with its own owner's factors (a controlled entrant with the controlling house's); health and a harvester's load play no part. No kill, no loss, no `Killed` effect.
- **Interruptions (claim 15).** A new order, a destroyed or sold building and an owner change end the trip (`objects::remove` clears destinations). A path search that fails more than 1 cell from the building clears the destination and queues *guard* (S-0603 revision 4 claims 3 and 15, S-0212 claim 23 row 2; `claim_3_failed_approach_gives_up`).
- **Seams filled.** The grinder fate of S-0601 claim 8 (`mind::grinder_fate`: the nearest own recycling building by ρ₃, ties to the latest placed, *eaten* queued); the recycling consumption of S-0524 claim 12's consuming seam with its passenger removal.
- **Seam left.** Claim 12's *active-1* / *special-1* swap (display only).
- **Own AI.** `glasnost_ai::grinder` (AI game format 21, `Uses.grinds`; 20 on this branch until step 1g took 20): the house builds one grinder, the cheapest it can place, with twice its cost in hand (`Brain::build_grinder`). It sends idle controlled enemy vehicles (and controlled enemy harvesters, which do no work for it; controlled infantry go to an absorber with room first), badly damaged own units (at most 30 % strength; vehicles only without a repair depot) and, while credits are below 1 000 and no absorber has room, own infantry at home beyond four. Every order is the *recycle* click; a unit more than 12 cells away is first clicked to a cell 4 cells off the grinder. The base defence leaves units bound for a grinder alone. All numbers are `nocite` tuning. Tests: `crates/deterministic/ai/tests/grinder.rs` (10). Finding (Q-1280, answered by S-0212 revision 49 at step 1i): a unit sent from afar gave up the order on the next tick when buildings stood across its route. This is not a grinder matter. `path::compute` follows S-0212 claims 13 and 13a: on open synthetic ground a straight line of seven 1 × 1 buildings, 20 cells away, can defeat a vehicle's or an infantry unit's move to a cell just behind it, and the recycle order to a grinder behind it. Two cell searches fail; the retries close level-0 and level-1 connections but none at level 2, because there the free part borders the next sector; then the new coarse search fails at level 1, and claim 13's table fails the computation. S-0212's short-line edge case confirms this as the original's behaviour with `quirks.corridor_retry_no_fallback` on (the default); off, one more cell search without a corridor follows and the line is passed at every alignment. In the synthetic geometry of `run_s_shifts_with_the_switch_on`, lines of three and five pass at all eight shifts, the 7-line fails at shifts 0 and 1 and the 9-line at shift 0. The failed unit gives up (S-0603 claim 3, S-0212 claim 23 row 2): *guard* queued, and in our engine it starts at once (Q-1157 interim). Because a person's direct click still fails at the default, the AI keeps its two steps.
- **Client and lane.** The local lane scene `grinder` (`crates/app/tests/m7a_lane.rs`, screenshots `local-lane/m7a/grinder-*.png`): three infantry, an own vehicle and an enemy vehicle under control are clicked in (cursor *recycle* for all five). They are consumed after 98, 99, 104, 116 and 163 ticks, and the house is paid. AI lane (map 8, seed 7, Yuri house 1; counts in `local-lane/m7a/s0603/`): sides 0,2: Allied won at 20 112; sides 1,2: Soviet won at 13 305. After merging step 1g (seed 7): map 8 sides 0,2: Allied won at 16 779; sides 1,2: Soviet won at 13 305; map 2 sides 0,2: Allied won at 18 913; sides 1,2: Yuri won at 29 979. All four: 0 grinder entries, 0 units ground, no recycling building. (Before the merge, map 2 sides 1,2 built one at about 16 500 and ground 1 unit.) Why, traced with a temporary probe of `Brain::build_grinder`: the stock recycling building is offered (`side-audit 2 8 7` orders it at 6 915 in the sandbox), and from 4 200 every condition holds but the money. Until about 4 600 to 5 100 the building line is busy with plan buildings while the grinder is affordable. After that, credits stay below twice its cost whenever the line is free (map 2 sides 1,2: from 6 900 to the end of the game). On map 8 sides 0,2, by the time money came back the house had lost a prerequisite building. So credits and the plan's use of the line decide it. No rule of ours stops a build a person would make, and the AI was not changed for it. Outputs: `ai-sides-0-2.txt`, `ai-sides-1-2.txt`, `ai-sides-0-2-map2.txt`, `ai-sides-1-2-map2.txt` (money values removed). Replays and mid-game saves matched in both.
- **Gate.** The M7a duel's Alpha may build GRND (2 × 2, cost 400, no power; Alpha only), and starts with four HULKs north of its start (an unarmed vehicle of the fixture that no house can build), placed at health 51 of 256, which a person would recycle. The run counts grinder entry orders and units ground (`Effect::Consumed`), and saves and loads at the first tick a unit is bound and the first consumption. Seed 3 (`M7A_SEED`, unchanged from step 1g): 4 entry orders, 4 ground, every other counter at least 3 except sensor orders 2. Minimum / median over seeds 1 to 16, measured after merging step 1g:
  - berserk orders 9/19, units made berserk 27/47;
  - sensor orders 2/2, intent ticks 1 869/3 316;
  - reveal orders 6/11, reveals fired 6/11;
  - slave-miner buildings 3/5, slave ticks 1 274/7 193;
  - gattling stage rises 6/14, top-stage ticks 448/2 373;
  - takeover orders 3/14, takeovers 7/15;
  - gas clouds 26/147, gas hits 39/487;
  - absorber entry orders 6/15, infantry absorbed 5/8;
  - grinder entry orders 4/8, units ground 4/8.

  Without the hulks the grinder counters missed the margin (before step 1g: minimum 0, median 3): after the grinder stands (tick 6 200 to 7 400) Alpha seldom has an idle controlled vehicle, nothing at or below 30 % health, and no surplus infantry at home. Letting Beta build GRND as well was tried and dropped. With the hulks, 8 of the 16 games end before 12 000 ticks (Alpha wins seeds 1, 2, 7, 8, 9, 12, 14 and 15); seed 3 plays the full run. The AI duels' goldens moved with the AI game format and were regenerated after the merge. At step 1i (S-0212 revision 49 with `corridor_retry_no_fallback`, S-0605 revision 5's approach, S-0506 revision 10's flagged slot) the goldens were regenerated and no golden file changed: no gate game reaches the changed rows, and every figure above (seed 3 and the sweep) is the same.

## Own AI (M5b)

`glasnost_ai::game::Game` runs every game with AI houses: the world plus one `brain::Brain` per AI house of the setup (`HouseSetup.ai` and the player role, glossary *AI house*). In each tick, after the person's orders, every AI house that is not defeated or removed thinks and its orders go through `orders::issue` like a person's; then `tick::step` runs. The brains are saved with the game (`Game::save`, `Game::load`) and hashed with the world (`Game::state_hash`), and a replay holds only the setup and the person's orders: `Game::run_replay` runs the AI again. The AI draws from no random stream.

The brain plays in two layers (PLAN §4.7 stage 1 and enough of stage 2):

- **The plan** (`scripted::Script`, M5a's scripted builder), every `THINK_PERIOD` ticks: deploy the MCV, build power, a refinery and factories, keep two harvesters, build the army round-robin and attack in waves (see `scripted.rs`). It rebuilds lost power, refineries, factories and harvesters, since it asks for each role whenever the house has none (fewer harvesters than the plan's).
- **Selling out** (part of the plan's think): a house whose harvesters' summed loads and credits show no income for `BROKE_PATIENCE` ticks (a rise within `SALE_WINDOW` ticks of its own sale counts as the refund) and that cannot afford anything it could build sells its costliest sellable building (`sell::sellable`, S-0408 claim 3), one per patience period, keeping one factory per kind while it has army units, and from then on builds no buildings, harvesters or construction yard, only army units. Income ends the mode. A house with nothing left to fight with thus sells out, so a game between two broke houses ends. The loads of its slaves on the map count with the harvesters', so a house whose slaves are delivering is not broke.
- **The reactions**, every tick (no reaction delay): place a finished building at once; defend the base (every army unit within 30 cells of home that is not already fighting one attacks the nearest seen enemy within 12 cells of a house building); rebuild a lost construction yard (build the cheapest buildable unit that deploys into one, queued behind what the line holds); and move an idle deploying unit that could not deploy where it stands to the nearest spiral cell where the deploy's cell test (S-0415 claim 3, the answer a person's deploy cursor gives) passes.

- **New units (M6a).** Aircraft are army of their own kind in the round-robin; a type the house cannot build yet makes the tech climb build its blocker, so a pad for an aircraft and a naval yard for a ship (ordered only when the placement spiral has a cell for it, which for a yard means water). An idle aircraft with ammunition attacks the nearest seen enemy it can hit and flies back to rearm by itself (S-0501); aircraft are kept out of the land waves. A launcher that spawns rockets (S-0506) is army like any armed vehicle. A unit whose goal lies outside its reach region (`Script::reachable`, S-0212 claim 4: a ship for a goal on land) goes to the nearest cell of its own region instead, as a person sends ships to the water off an enemy base. Defence and attacks pick only targets one of the unit's weapons can hit (`scripted::can_attack`: `weapon::would_use` gives a weapon and the firing check answers neither *illegal* nor *impossible*), so ground units do not chase hovering aircraft.

What it knows is what a person in its seat knows: its own objects, its production lines and sidebar offer, enemy objects on cells its house has explored (`econ::shroud::explored`; fog of war is off in a skirmish, S-0409 claim 19, so a person sees what stands there), the map's start waypoints (lobby knowledge), and the answers of order-legality checks a person's cursor gives (`placement::place_legal`, `placement::cell_test`). Its placement spacing (`Script::free`) counts only its own objects and those on explored cells; it keeps a gap of two cells between buildings, and `place_cell` narrows the gap to one cell and then none when the base is too cramped for any cell with the wider gap (M6c step 2d). A unit resting in guard that holds a target out of its weapon's range counts as idle (M6c step 2a, `stale_guard_target`). A refinery is a building with the refinery flag or a member of the refinery group (S-0407 Import mapping, `PROC`); a role the house cannot build a type for yet does not hold up the next one, and the army waits for harvesters only while a harvester type is buildable, or a vehicle factory is while the house has none. Refused orders are not an error: the next think tries again. Every number in `brain.rs` and `scripted.rs` is tuning of our own opponent (`nocite`), not game behaviour. Types are found by role from the rules among those the house can build, so every faction plays with its own types. A slave miner (S-0402 claim 14) plays the harvester role and its deployed building counts towards the harvesters kept; it is built without a refinery, and a resting one whose slaves are home is sent to harvest with the nearest explored resource cell as its destination, the order a person gives it (no other order starts a player house's slave miner); it then finds its site and deploys by S-0402 claim 22. A slave with a master is never army, even when its type is armed. Only a vehicle that deploys into a construction yard is deployed as the MCV or kept as the lost yard's replacement.

Tests: `crates/deterministic/ai/tests/ai.rs` on the synthetic skirmish (`fixture`): the build-up, a rebuilt power plant, base defence, and shroud honesty (two worlds that differ only by an enemy unit on a cell the house has not explored give identical orders over a think period) in debug; a broke house selling a building (and a house with income not selling), the rebuilt harvesters, the rebuilt construction yard, a slave-miner side earning income from its slaves (and rebuilding a lost slave miner) and two whole games played to one winner with each house's loop checked in release (`cargo test --release -p glasnost-ai -- --include-ignored`). The determinism gate adds three AI duels (`duel`, `duel-diagonal`, `duel-naval`: the AI against itself on `fixture::map`, `fixture::map_diagonal` and the water map `fixture::map_naval`, hashed with `Game::state_hash`, saved and loaded halfway and at the first tick with a rocket in flight and with an aircraft in the air; the loaded game must hash alike and the run goes on from it, and a full run of the straight duel must have flown both; the diagonal game ends before a launcher is sent, and since S-0507 revision 4 the naval game no longer reaches a launcher shot within its run). The fixture (M6a) adds a pad, an armed plane, a V3-style launcher with its set-A rocket type (not scored, S-0414 claim 4), a naval yard and a gunboat, its own tile catalogue (`fixture::catalogue`, water tile `fixture::WATER_TILE`) and the release tests `flies_aircraft_from_pads` and `builds_a_navy_on_a_water_map` in `tests/skirmish.rs`. M6b adds HOVR, a gun hovercraft (hovering locomotor, amphibious zone, needs NAVY and LAB), as army of the vehicle factory, and the debug test `a_hover_crosses_the_sea_to_a_land_goal`.

Transports and hovers in the local lane (M6c step 2c). The ferry (`Brain::ferry`) is built and works; the AI-lane zero readings of the M6b lane on maps 3 and 5 were short games. At the start of M6c (seed 7, 120 000 ticks) map 2 had 40 loads and 5 launches, map 3 ended at tick 13 129 and map 5 at tick 11 721, both with 0 loads: the first ferry needs the harvesters kept, idle infantry at home, a type the tech tree allows and its build time, which a rush by tick 12 000 does not leave. On map 3 the run never crosses water (`diagnosis` of a 6 000, 12 000 and 18 000 tick run: infantry reaches the enemy base by its zone, no ferry type buildable before tick 18 000), so no crossing was due. With the final code (seed 7, all seven maps end with a winner; counts in `local-lane/m6c/ai-final-maps.txt`) the loads are 69 on map 0 (5 of them onto a hovering transport), 48 on map 2, 1 on map 3 (onto a hovering transport), 32 on map 5, 40 on maps 6 and 7 and 48 on map 8, and hovering or amphibious units were seen on water on maps 0 (4 units), 3 (1) and 5 (1). The lane now counts the loads, the loads onto a hovering transport and the hovering or amphibious units seen on water (`Tally`, the `hover or amphibious on water` feature); tests `counts_the_loads_onto_a_hovering_transport` and `counts_a_launch_by_kind` (`crates/tools/tests/tally.rs`); the ferry itself is tested by `ferries_infantry_across_a_strait`. The brain's diagnosis names the ferry state (idle infantry at home, whether the run crosses water, ferries owned, ferry types buildable). Not done in this step: a transport for vehicles or for a naval landing (the AI carries infantry only).

Maps 6 and 7 of the local lane (M6c step 2d). In the M6b lane both reached no winner in 120 000 ticks (AI against AI, seed 7). Diagnosis (`GLASNOST_AI_QUICK=1 glasnost-headless ai 24000 7 6 7`, the lane prints a counts-only `diagnosis` for a game that does not end): the maps are not islands and no house was out of resources. On map 6 both houses held a yard, a power plant, a refinery, a barracks and one harvester, 29 000 credits and 389 resource cells worth 105 225 on the map; on map 7 one house stood so and the other held only its yard with 10 000 credits and no power plant. Neither had a war factory: of the 624 cells of the placement spiral, 156 to 164 were legal for it, 10 to 28 passed the cell test (their start sits in a small clearing), and none kept the two-cell gap to the buildings already standing (`Script::free`). So the second harvester, which needs the war factory, never came, and the army waits for harvesters, so no unit was built and the houses did not meet. Class: a defect in our AI (a fixed gap in a cramped base), not a stalemate. Fix: `Script::place_cell` narrows the gap to one cell, then to none, when no cell keeps it (the diagnosis names the cell counts per stage). Result with seed 7: map 6 over at tick 23 804 (house 1 won), map 7 over at tick 31 784 (house 1 won), 24 and 16 transport loads, 3 superweapon launches each. Test `builds_every_building_in_a_cramped_base` (`ai.rs`, the clearing of `fixture::map_cramped`). Open: a house whose factory has no cell even without a gap still waits for its harvester before any army (the army rule `economy_first`); not reached on these maps. Cost per tick (release, final code, no replay): map 7 6.0 ms, maps 0, 3 and 5 2.8 to 3.0 ms, map 2 2.1 ms, maps 6 and 8 0.4 to 0.6 ms; the M6b lane read 1.0 ms on map 0 and 1.3 ms on map 7 with one house idle there, but the games differ in length and army, and the share of the three placement passes in `place_cell` (called for each giving building type every think and in `water_goal`) was not measured.

Whole-game AI tests (M6c step 2a). The release games `builds_a_navy_on_a_water_map` (`crates/deterministic/ai/tests/skirmish.rs`, seed 6) and `builds_and_fires_a_superweapon` (`ai.rs`, seed 5) run one fixed seed again; `naval_seed_sweep` and `superweapon_seed_sweep` (`GLASNOST_SWEEP=<from>-<to>`, `--ignored --nocapture`) print the pass count of a seed range and the claims that fail. Cause, found by running the same seeds at 1783c23^, 1783c23 and the tip: the S-0207 claim 26 goal cleanup (`drive::goal_cleanup`) follows its spec and is not a defect; it only shifted which seed wins the first clash. The chain, from the first diverging tick of the scripted naval game (seed 5, the replay's orders equal up to tick 8145): claim 26 keeps the order-level destination of a driving vehicle for one locomotion update after its last track ends, S-0402 claim 3 makes a seeking harvester with a destination wait for its next processing, so a harvester's decisions come later (about ten ticks in the traced case), harvest and income timing move, the Build orders move by tens of ticks, and with them the winner of the first clash. Whether the two specs compose that way was asked in Q-0900, which S-0402 revision 25 claim 3b (with S-0207 revision 30 claim 26 and S-0215 revision 49 claim 2) answers as coded: the seeking test reads the order-level destination, a due processing in the tick after the last track still sees it and stays seeking with the standard delay, and loading starts at the first processing from the tick after that (test `claim_3b_loading_starts_after_the_destination_is_removed`). In 20 of 32 seeds one house loses its construction yard to the first wave (near tick 10 000, the yard at a few health points) both before and after that commit (20 of 32 at 1783c23^, 22 at 1783c23), and the loser never reaches its naval yard or silo; the claim that both houses build a navy held in 12 of 32 seeds before and 9 after, by luck of the seed. Two AI gaps found on the way. (1) An armed unit resting in guard keeps an acquired target that is out of range and does not pursue it (S-0215 claim 24), so `scripted::idle` never saw it as free: a home guard stood still while the wave destroyed the base (the firing check answered out of range for most of the guard-with-target unit-ticks). `idle` now treats a resting unit whose target it cannot reach as idle (`stale_guard_target`), as a person would give it an order. (2) The army round-robin shared one counter over three kinds of army, which skipped some types every round (the hovercraft was rarely built); it is one counter per kind now (`next_army`, game save format 2). Pass counts over seeds 1 to 32. The naval game under the original claim set (a navy and a ship, a hovercraft, a ship crossed, fired and one sank, yards on water): 7 before, 8 after. Under the new claim set (the hovercraft claim moved out): 29 before and 29 after (the three that fail, 5, 7 and 17, send no ship out before the game ends); so the 29 comes from the moved claim, not from a fix. One silo built and one superweapon fired (the old test wanted a silo of each house too; its count was not taken): 21 before, 26 after the idle-guard fix, 29 with the later superweapon changes (step 2b). The hovercraft claim moved out of the whole naval game (its lab comes after the naval yard and most games end first) into `builds_a_hovercraft_once_a_naval_yard_and_a_lab_stand`; the claims that both houses build a navy or a silo are dropped as seed luck. The gate's straight duel no longer flies a rocket and the diagonal duel no longer launches (`crates/sim-gate/src/lib.rs`). A stock-map AI duel still plays to a winner in every case run (local lane).

Lane findings of M6c step 5 (AI, fixed). (1) **Infantry left at the shore.** After step 1f's stronger economy, map 5 ran out of ore and reached no winner: army infantry that could not walk to the attack goal stood at the nearest shore, out of the ferry's reach (`Brain::ferry` takes idle infantry within twice `FERRY_HOME` of home). Now an idle infantry unit that does not fly and whose reach region does not hold the goal (`Script::needs_ferry`) walks back to within `FERRY_GATHER` cells of home and waits there for a ferry (test `infantry_that_cannot_walk_to_the_goal_waits_at_home`; `a_wave_sends_its_jumpjets_across` checks that the wave gives such a walker no destination). (2) **A barracks without a rally point.** The lane's unit-class count (`glasnost-headless ai`, line *unit types by class*, and for each never-built type its kind, class, weapon, AI role and first offered tick) showed one early infantry type of the Allied side never built in 9 of 14 games, although the army builder ordered it. Its release was refused: with no rally point a released infantry unit gets no destination (S-0417 claim 10, S-0404 claim 13) and rests at the barracks while still in contact with it, so when the next item was done no contact place was free (S-0417 claim 14), the release was busy and the item was cancelled with its refund and not retried (S-0404 claim 7). The cancelled item was whatever the round robin had next; this type came after a unit that stayed every time. A person sets a rally point, and now the AI does too: `Script::rally_points` clicks (`targeting::click`, S-0213 claim 20 (a), the click's own nearby-cell search of S-0212 table T5) on a cell `RALLY_AHEAD` cells beyond each barracks without one, toward the attack goal (tests `sets_a_rally_point_on_its_barracks`, `a_released_infantry_unit_frees_its_barracks`; both fail without it). With the rally point the infantry fights from the start, so the games are shorter and some house-local tests now remove the armies while they wait (`builds_a_hovercraft_once_a_naval_yard_and_a_lab_stand`, `builds_one_building_of_every_kind`). (3) **A ferry-wait goal that flew away.** The ferry-wait test of (1) and `Brain::ferry` aimed at `attack_goal`, the nearest seen enemy of any kind; on map 8 that was an aircraft or a unit across the water, so the walkers stayed home for good and the game reached no winner. Both now aim at `Script::base_goal`: the seen enemy building nearest home, else the next enemy start position. (4) **Army types never reached.** The army builder took one type per kind in turn; on the Soviet side the vehicle list grows with the tech tree and one line builds them all, so in a short game the later types were never reached, and on a water map a naval yard's pick moved the same counter as the war factory's, so the war factory skipped the hovercraft every round (the AI test above failed once the games were shorter). Now, of the types whose line is free, the builder takes the one the house has fewest of on the map, then the one it has built fewest of (S-0407 claim 10's ever-built count), then the next in turn. The lane's same-side pairing (`GLASNOST_AI_SIDES=1,1`: the first two open countries of side 1) showed that the winning Soviet house builds every class, jumpjet units included; the Soviet house that loses to an Allied house falls before its last vehicle types come up (Allied against Soviet the Allied house wins every game run; the AI plays both the same way, so this reads as balance of the data under our AI, not as an exclusion). (5) **A loaded ferry that never left.** On map 5 a loaded hover transport stood 5 to 11 cells from home, just outside `FERRY_HOME`, so `Brain::ferry` took it for a ferry that had arrived and clicked it to unload; the infantry were then within the gathering radius and were boarded again, and the game reached no winner in 120 000 ticks. A loaded ferry within the gathering radius (twice `FERRY_HOME`) that is not at home now leaves for the drop ring near the goal (test `a_loaded_ferry_near_home_leaves_for_the_goal`, which fails without it), and a ferry that has stood in its unload for more than `UNLOAD_STUCK` ticks with passengers still aboard (a passenger with no exit blocks the rest, S-0507 claim 10) is moved to land nearby, where the unload click is made again (test `a_ferry_stuck_unloading_is_moved_on`). Map 5 then ended with a winner at tick 95 700. (A loaded ferry near home with no drop cell to go to falls back to the earlier rule and lets its passengers out.) (6) **A wave sent to the shore by a ship.** On map 3 the wave goal was the seen enemy nearest home, of any kind; for most of the game that was an object off the house's own shore. About a hundred infantry stood at the shore by it, arrived and so idle, and every wave sent them there again: the game reached no winner in 120 000 ticks although one house was down to four buildings. Now each unit of a wave takes the seen enemy nearest home that lies in its own reach region (S-0212 claim 4; any for a unit that flies, and a ship keeps the old rule, as it fires from the water off an enemy base) and that it can attack (`can_attack`), else the next enemy start cell as before (test `a_wave_goes_for_an_enemy_it_can_reach`, which fails without it). Map 3 then ended at tick 33 915 and map 5 at 48 382 (quick runs).

Lane findings of M7a step 2 (seed 7, quick runs: `GLASNOST_AI_QUICK=1`, no replay or save check; outputs under the git-ignored `local-lane/m7a/`). (a) **The ferry fallback** of M6c step 5 (5) now has a test: `a_loaded_ferry_with_no_drop_cell_lets_its_passengers_out` (ai.rs) fills every land cell across the strait, and the loaded ferry near home is told to unload; without the fallback the brain gives it no order. (b) **The M6c duel's margins** come from its setup alone (start units, a second tech and spy building per side, the HULKs, a second jumpjet type); see *Margins* under *Determinism gate*. (c) **Charges and disarms at 0.** The runner's new line *occasions (S-0518, S-0519)* counts per house the buildable charger, overpowerable (and of them the prism type), disarmer and bomb-detector types, and the house-ticks with an occasion. Over the 16 games (Allied against Allied on maps 0, 2, 3, 5, 6, 7 and 8, Allied against Soviet on the same maps, Soviet against Soviet on maps 2 and 8): the 21 Allied houses could build 0 charger types and 0 overpowerable types; each of the 11 Soviet houses could build one charger type and one overpowerable type that is not the prism type, had chargers on the map for 3 601 to 17 924 house-ticks and an overpowerable building for 0, because the linked module ordered only the prism type. A person who builds chargers builds the building they charge, so the module now orders it (see *Linked weapons*). In the re-run with the fix 2 of the 11 Soviet houses built one (12 charge orders); the others never held twice its price with the building line free (one game sampled every 300 ticks: 75 of 78 samples short of the credits, the line busy in 52), and chargers stood idle within 12 cells of the building in 3 and 87 house-ticks, the rest of the time they were with the army. Disarms: every house could build one disarmer type and one detector type; 5 own objects carried a bomb over the 16 games (9 in the re-run), and in no house-tick did the carrier's house see one of them (`bomb::visible`), so no person in the seat had an occasion to disarm; no change. (d) **Map 8, Allied against Soviet: the hover class never built.** Nothing excludes it: in the 5 668 ticks from its first offer to the end the Allied war factory's line was free for a new pick only twice, because each item it built waited for money (8 army vehicles in the whole game, 1 200 to 4 000 ticks each; three times an item's progress stood still for 300 ticks or more; credits 92 at the end). At the first pick the hover type and another vehicle type were tied at 0 on the map and 0 built and the round robin took the other; at the second (tick 15 240) it took the hover type, 29 ticks before the game ended. No change. (e) **An order flood in the Soviet against Yuri game** (map 0): 1253 bomb, 340 suicide and 263 air-strike orders from 6 units, fixed in the memory (see *Orders a unit did not take*); 119, 1 and 10 after. Re-run of the 16 pairings with (c) and (e), every game over with a winner: Allied against Allied map 0 house 1 at 10 978, 2 house 0 at 63 719, 3 house 0 at 41 804, 5 house 1 at 32 646, 6 house 0 at 50 604, 7 house 0 at 28 646, 8 house 0 at 44 242; Allied against Soviet house 0 on every map, at 23 630 (0), 20 168 (2), 24 607 (3), 15 583 (5), 16 217 (6), 17 272 (7), 15 269 (8); Soviet against Soviet map 2 house 0 at 86 860, map 8 house 0 at 29 107; Soviet against Yuri map 0 house 1 (Yuri) at 83 108. Unit classes (line *unit types by class*): the winning house built at least one type of every class it could build in 12 of the 16 games (both Soviet against Soviet winners built 2 of 2 jumpjet types). The four others: Allied against Allied map 0 (the shortest game: hover 0 of 1, teleporter 0 of 1) and Allied against Soviet maps 0 (hover 0/1, ship 0/4, teleporter 0/1), 5 (hover 0/1) and 8 (hover 0/1, as in (d)). The losing Soviet houses of the Allied against Soviet games built no jumpjet type (0 of 2), as at M6c step 5.

Yuri side (M7a step 4; seed 7; outputs under the git-ignored `local-lane/m7a/yuri4/`). (a) **Audit.** `glasnost-headless side-audit [side] [map index] [seed]` (`glasnost_tools::side_audit`) lists every type whose owner list names the side's first open country with its kind, plan role, flags and weapon and warhead flags, and plays a sandbox game (1 000 000 credits, an idle opponent, each factory given a rally point) that orders each offered type once, places finished buildings on the plan's spiral and releases finished units; for a type never offered it names the unmet prerequisites and the country and stolen-tech conditions. Map 0, side 2: 79 types (53 buildings, 16 infantry, 10 vehicles, no aircraft), 78 within the house tech level. 31 are offered, and all 31 are built and placed or released (0 without a cell, 0 left on the line). Not offered: 1 above the tech level, 3 that need stolen technology (S-0407 claim 6; infiltration is S-0510), 4 that the country's forbidden list refuses, 2 that only other countries may build, and the rest need buildings of another side (a captured yard). Every offered type is covered by an approved spec: the base, power, factories, naval yard, wall and radar by S-0404/S-0407 (the radar's psychic reveal and sensor by S-0610), the slave miner and its building by S-0402, the cloning building by S-0404 claim 14, the force shield by S-0512, the hover transport by S-0507, the ship by the naval and spawn specs (S-0503, S-0506), the deployers by S-0517, the jumpjet vehicle by S-0502, the berserk vehicle by S-0610, and the rest by the batch-6 specs that are approved but not built yet: mind control (S-0601, being built in parallel), grinder (S-0603), infantry absorber (S-0605, the power plant), vehicle bunker (S-0606), magnetic lift (S-0607), drain (S-0608), gattling (S-0609), mutation (S-0611), the dominator (S-0612), gas clouds (S-0613) and special hits (S-0615). Those types import, are offered and are built today; what is missing is their ability, a seam of the step that builds each spec. No type lacks a spec, so no request. (b) **The jumpjet vehicle.** No rule excludes it: map 2 against the Allied side (a quick run before (c)), it was picked at the first free pick of the war factory after its offer (tick 11 221; offered at 6 601) and cancelled with a refund at 45 of 54 (no AI code cancels; most likely a prerequisite was lost); on map 8 against the Soviet side it was picked at 13 741 and was still waiting for money when the game ended. The house's credits sat near 0 from about tick 6 600 and each vehicle of its kind took about 4 000 ticks while it waited for money. (`GLASNOST_AI_LINES=h` prints house h's production line changes: started, ended, and every 300 ticks progress, waiting and credits.) In the full re-run below the Yuri house built it in each of the four games in which it was offered (against the Allied side on maps 0 and 8 the game ended before it was). (c) **Fixed: a refinery ordered while every slave miner was moving.** When all of a house's slave miners had packed up to move on (S-0402 claims 22 and 24), the plan saw no refinery and ordered the side's refinery building, which is the slave miner's costly deployed form; on map 2 such an order (tick 12 721) sat at progress 2 with no money and held the building line until 13 261. A slave miner acts as its slaves' refinery whether it is a vehicle or deployed (S-0402 claim 14), and a person does not buy a refinery while theirs drives to the next field, so `Script::has_refinery` now counts a slave miner of the house (test `a_moving_slave_miner_stands_for_the_refinery`, ai.rs: with a driving slave miner the plan orders no refinery; with none it does). Looked at, no change: the slaves are not counted as harvesters (only the miner, and its deployed building); the plan's refinery-less harvester rule already lets a slave-miner side build its miner; the absorbing power plant is S-0605's; no side's AI builds defences (a general gap, not the Yuri side's). (d) **Lane, full mode** (`ai-sides 120000 7 0 2 8`, replay and mid-game save checks on; both true in all six games). Allied (house 0) against Yuri (house 1): house 0 won on map 0 at 13 998, on map 2 at 21 228 and on map 8 at 18 333. Soviet (house 0) against Yuri (house 1): house 1 won on map 0 at 41 519 and on map 2 at 51 707; house 0 won on map 8 at 16 915. The runner's per-role counts are distinct objects a house ever held, so every deploy and undeploy of a slave miner adds a refinery and a harvester (the Yuri house on map 0 against the Soviet side: 49 and 48 over 41 519 ticks); the counts do not tell relocations from purchases. Unit classes (line *unit types by class*), the Yuri house: every class it could build had a built type in five of the six games (jumpjet 1/1 in the four where it was offered; ship 1/1 and transport 1/1 on map 0 against the Soviet side); against the Soviet side on map 8 (the shortest of its games) one armed and one deployer type were not built. The Allied and Soviet houses: against Yuri the Allied side left 5 types unbuilt on map 0 (a 14 000-tick game) and the hover type on map 8; the Soviet side left an armed, a jumpjet, three ship and a transport type unbuilt on map 0, both jumpjet types on map 2 and one jumpjet type on map 8 (it lost two of three games). Psychic weapons in these games: 0 to 6 units gone berserk, 4 553 to 32 843 house-ticks with a hostile-intent line, 0 to 13 psychic reveals per game. Not traced: on map 2 against the Soviet side the Yuri slaves spent 293 517 slave-ticks in *guard* while returning (about six slaves for the whole game), against 3 592 on the same map against the Allied side; S-0402's relocation is the simulation's and was left alone.

## Own AI (M6c: special abilities)

`glasnost_ai::special` (one module of its own, hooked into `Brain::think` once per think period) uses the special abilities the way a person in the house's seat could: it clicks (`targeting::cursor` and `targeting::click`, so the cursor rules of S-0214 decide what a click means) on objects of cells its house has explored (`econ::shroud::explored`) and issues the resulting orders through `orders::issue`. It reads no hidden state and draws no random value. Its counters (`Uses`: garrison, capture and infiltration orders) are kept with the brain, saved and hashed with the game (`ai::game::GAME_FORMAT_VERSION` 2), and the headless runner's AI games print them (`special abilities (orders): …; garrison occupants inside at the end: …` in `local-lane/ai-duel.txt`). Tests: `crates/deterministic/ai/tests/special.rs`.

- **Garrisons (S-0508).** Every idle infantry unit of an occupier type that the house can select is sent to the nearest building it may garrison (S-0508 claim 1, the cursor of row I8) on an explored cell within 24 cells, civilian garrison buildings before the house's own, with a free place (occupants plus units already on their way, the orders of the same think included). Numbers are tuning of our own opponent (`nocite`). It keeps at most six occupants in garrisons, and looks for buildings near its home.
- **Engineers (S-0509).** Every idle engineer is sent to the nearest seen enemy building whose cursor shows capture or damaging entry (S-0214 rows I6 and I7: the enemy's capturable buildings), else to the nearest own building whose cursor shows repair, never to a building another engineer of the house is already bound for. A house with no engineer (on the map, off it or in its line) and no engineer queued that sees a capturable enemy building orders the cheapest engineer type its sidebar offers, once, when it holds at least twice its price in credits and is not selling out.
- **Tech buildings (S-0515).** `glasnost_ai::tech_buildings` ranks the engineer's targets: a tech building held by a passive house that pays (an acquisition bonus or an income delay) comes before one that only serves (among captures; repair stays last), then the nearest; a house with no engineer orders one also when it sees such a building (the earlier rule looked only at buildings of player houses). `Uses::tech` counts the orders to tech buildings (a subset of `engineers`; AI game format 3) and the runner prints it as "tech buildings". Tests: `sends_an_engineer_to_a_paying_tech_building_first`, `takes_a_serving_tech_building_and_only_an_explored_one`, `orders_an_engineer_for_a_tech_building`.
- **Jumpjets (S-0502).** `glasnost_ai::jumpjets` (a new file; `Special::jumpjets` is called from `Special::think`): every idle armed jumpjet unit is sent against the nearest seen enemy that a walking unit of the house's cannot reach (another reach region of the ground, `across_obstacle`) and that it may attack (`scripted::can_attack`), with the order a person gives; a wave's jumpjets go to the goal itself (`Script::reachable` returns the goal for a unit that flies); a house with no jumpjet unit (on the map or in its line) that sees such an enemy would order the cheapest armed jumpjet type its sidebar offers, once, under the credit reserve (held back at M6c step 2e under the interim rule of Q-1070, orders again since step 1f: see *A jumpjet unit and its factory*). `Uses::jumps` counts the orders (AI game format 4) and the runner prints it as "jumpjets". Tests: `crates/deterministic/ai/tests/jumpjets.rs` on the strait map (`sends_a_jumpjet_over_the_strait`, `flies_only_at_what_it_has_explored`, `a_wave_sends_its_jumpjets_across`, `builds_a_jumpjet_unit_and_its_factory_stays_free`).
- **Deployable units (S-0517).** `glasnost_ai::deployers` (a new file; `Special::deployers` is called from `Special::think`): a standing in-place vehicle, deploy-fire vehicle or firing infantry unit (no destination, resting or attacking, with or without a target) that has a seen enemy it can attack within 9 cells is sent the deploy order a person gives (`Order::Deploy`); a deployed in-place vehicle with no such enemy within 13 cells is sent the same order again to pack up, so that it joins the next wave (`scripted::idle` leaves a deployed or changing vehicle out of the waves). Every order is counted by `Uses::deploys`; the runner prints it as "deploys". It reads only what the house sees and draws no random value.
- **Radiation (S-0525).** `glasnost_ai::radiation` (a new file; `Special::radiation` is called from `Special::think`): every idle infantry unit or vehicle that radiation hurts (not immune, on the map) and that stands on a cell its house has explored where the cell radiation is at least 1 is sent to the nearest cell within 8 cells that is usable, explored, clean and in its own reach region, with a *move* order. A radiation-immune deployer is deployed by the deploy rules near a seen enemy and then fires its radiation weapon at its own cell (S-0215 claim 31). `Uses::radiation` counts the orders (AI game format 7); the runner prints it as "radiation exits". Tests: `crates/deterministic/ai/tests/radiation.rs` (`leaves_a_field`, `stays_when_it_is_safe`, `reads_only_explored_cells`).
- **Chrono weapons (S-0514).** `glasnost_ai::chrono` (a new file; `Special::chrono` is called from `Special::think`): every idle unit with a temporal beam that holds none and is selectable is sent the attack order against the costliest enemy its house sees (explored cells) that it may attack and that can be erased (an erasable type, not protected, in the ground layer), the nearest on equal cost. `Uses::erasures` counts the orders (AI game format 8); the runner prints it as "erasures". Tests: `crates/deterministic/ai/tests/chrono.rs`.
- **Linked weapons (S-0518).** `glasnost_ai::linked` (a new file; `Special::linked` is called from `Special::think`): a house that can build the prism type and holds at least twice its price orders one while it has fewer than three (on the map or queued), the finished ones being placed by the ordinary place reaction near the base (so within the support reach of each other, which the spiral keeps small); every idle charger (infantry or vehicle with an electric-charge secondary) is sent the attack order at the nearest own overpowerable building within 12 cells that has fewer than three chargers. `Uses::prisms` and `Uses::charges` count the orders (AI game format 9); the runner prints them as "prisms" and "charges".
