Since M7a step 2c a house that has a charger and no overpowerable building (on the map or queued) orders the cheapest overpowerable building type other than the prism type that its sidebar offers, under the same rules as the prism building (a free building line, not selling out, twice its price in hand; one such building wanted); `Uses::overpowerables` counts these orders (AI game format 15) and the runner prints "overpowerable buildings". Tests: `crates/deterministic/ai/tests/linked.rs` (`a_house_with_a_charger_orders_an_overpowerable_building` fails without the step 2c rule).
- **Terror and strikes (S-0519).** `glasnost_ai::terror` (a new file; `Special::terror` is called from `Special::think`): every idle selectable unit is given the order a person would give by its role: a designator (strike size above 0) attacks the costliest seen enemy building it may attack; a bomb planter (primary weapon with a bomb-planting warhead) the costliest seen enemy it may attack (a bombed carrier is illegal by the firing check, so it is passed over); a disarmer an own object that carries a bomb the house sees (`bomb::visible`); a suicide unit the nearest seen enemy it may attack; infantry with explosive charges the nearest seen enemy building the cursor offers sabotage on. `Uses::bombs`, `disarms`, `suicides`, `sabotages` and `strikes` count the orders (AI game format 10); the runner prints them. Tests: `crates/deterministic/ai/tests/terror.rs`.
- **Crates (S-0520).** `glasnost_ai::crates` (a new file; `Special::crates` is called from `Special::think`): every crate on a cell the house has explored (crate places whose cell holds a crate overlay) that no own unit already runs to is given to the fastest idle selectable ground unit within 30 cells (infantry or vehicle, not a harvester or an engineer; the nearest on equal speed), with a move order to the crate's cell; one unit per crate per think. `Uses::crates` counts the orders (AI game format 11); the runner prints "crates". Tests: `crates/deterministic/ai/tests/crates.rs`.
- **Orders a unit did not take (PLAN §4.7; AI game format 13, 14 since M7a step 2e).** `glasnost_ai::memory::Memory` (kept in `Special`, saved and hashed with the brain): every behaviour order a special-ability module gives is noted with the cell the unit stood on; at the next think a unit that is idle again on that very cell did not take the order (no path out of a pocket of buildings, a target it cannot attack), and the pair (unit, goal, cell) is blocked for 4500 ticks. Every module (garrison, engineers, spies, jumpjets, chrono, linked, terror, radiation, crates) asks `Memory::ok` when it picks a goal, so the unit is sent at the next best goal, or none, instead of the same click every think. A unit that moved, entered something, died or fired (its rearm tick changed) leaves no memory. A unit that is still busy on the same cell without having fired is judged again at the next think, for at most 4500 ticks, and a new order for the unit replaces its note (M7a step 2e; until then a busy unit's note was dropped at once). Found by the lane at step 2e: in the Soviet against Yuri game on map 0 (seed 7) the Soviet house gave 1253 bomb, 340 suicide and 263 air-strike orders; with a print of every such order, 2 planters took all 1253 bomb orders (one unit at one goal 499 times), 1 suicide unit all 340 (111 at one goal) and 3 designators all 263 (132 at one goal). Each held its attack order through the next think on the cell it stood on (busy, so the note was dropped) and then fell back to guarding there, idle, and was ordered again. With the change the same game gives 119 bomb (at most 4 orders for one unit and goal), 1 suicide and 10 air-strike orders; it now ends at tick 83 108 with the Yuri house the winner (before: 56 956, the Soviet house). Crates also skip a unit whose reach region does not hold the crate. Found by the lane: a starting engineer boxed in by buildings (the path search returned no steps, `Capture` lasted one tick) was ordered at the same tech building every think, 1250 times in map 2's 21 000 ticks, 5540 times in map 5. Tests: `does_not_reorder_an_engineer_that_did_not_take_the_order`, `an_engineer_that_gave_up_a_building_goes_for_another`, `an_engineer_on_its_way_is_not_given_up` (special.rs), `does_not_resend_a_unit_that_did_not_take_the_order`, `does_not_resend_a_unit_that_dropped_the_order_where_it_stood` (fails without the step 2e change), `a_unit_on_its_way_to_a_crate_is_not_given_up` (crates.rs).
- **A jumpjet unit and its factory (Q-1070 answered, S-0417 revision 19).** The interim hold of M6c step 2e is gone. S-0417 claim 15 gives a released jumpjet infantry unit or vehicle (type marked `JumpJet` or `BalloonHover`, in contact with a vehicle factory or a building with a barracks door kind) a clear report in its first ascending update whose adjusted height lies in trunc(*h*t / *q*) < *h* < *h*t (*q* = 2 for a balloon-hover vehicle, 4 otherwise); contact and tether end together (`econ::release::ascent_report`, called from `jump::fly::ascending_outcome`, so the factory closes by table S1 or the infantry place is free, whatever the unit does next). The design fallback (the update in which *h* reaches or passes *h*t reports too) has no quirk switch. The saved guard spot becomes the destination, otherwise the order-level destination is discarded (`jump::discard_order_destination`: the movement point and commitment stay) and a forced scatter request follows. The own AI therefore builds and uses jumpjet units like a person: `scripted::jams_factory` is removed, the army builder and the jumpjet module's order take the type again. Test: `builds_a_jumpjet_unit_and_its_factory_stays_free` (the barracks of that test has a door kind; the link must end while the unit is ascending); sim tests in `claims_s0417_r19.rs`. The lane counts of jumpjet units built and used are in step 5 of the M6c step ledger.
- **Psychic weapons (S-0610, M7a; AI game format 16 at merge).** `glasnost_ai::psychic` (a new file; `Special::berserk` is called from `Special::think`): every idle unit with a berserk weapon (a weapon slot with a berserk-causing warhead), or one resting in guard on a target it took by itself, is sent the attack order against the seen enemy unit within 12 cells that has the most seen enemy units within 3 cells of it (then the nearest), that it may attack (`scripted::can_attack`, so psionics-immune targets are passed over) and that is not berserk already. Once the harvesters are kept, `Brain::build_sensor` orders the cheapest psychic sensor type the house can build and place when it has none (replacing a tech-climb building of that think, like a superweapon building). `fire_superweapons` fires a ready psychic reveal at the enemy start cell nearest home that the house does not see, then at the unexplored cell (on a 4-cell lattice) nearest an enemy start, and keeps it when nothing is unexplored. `Brain::think` drops every order to a berserk unit (S-0214 claim 15). The deployed psychic pulse is the deployers' (firing infantry, S-0517 claim 16). `Uses::berserks`, `sensors` and `reveals` count the orders; the lane prints them and, per game, the units gone berserk and the house-ticks with an intent line (`skirmish_lane::Tally`). Numbers are tuning of our own.
- **Mind control (S-0601, M7a; AI game format 17).** `glasnost_ai::mind` (a new file; `Special::mind_control` is called from `Special::think` after the berserk weapons): every idle or guarding controller of the house that is selectable, not berserk, not at capacity (claim 2) and, if unlimited, safe to take one more (`mind::safe_more`: the overload level for N + 1 victims does no damage, claim 16) is sent with the attack order at the costliest seen enemy within 10 cells that the takeover test accepts (claim 3) and its firing check allows, then the nearest; no two controllers at the same enemy in one think. The brain drops any other attack order at an object to an unlimited controller that may not safely take more. Controlled objects belong to the house and join the waves like its own; controller types are built as army types. `Uses::takeovers` counts the orders; the skirmish lane prints it and a `mind control:` line (takeovers, most objects held at once and controllers per house). Tests `crates/deterministic/ai/tests/mind.rs` and the tally test `counts_takeovers_and_controllers`.
- **Gattling weapons (S-0609, M7a; AI game format 18).** `glasnost_ai::gattling` (a new file). (1) **Kept spin:** a gattling unit firing at an enemy object (`gattling::engaged`: its firing check gives can fire, rearming, not facing or still turning, the spin-up answers of claim 5) is given no attack order at another object by any part of the brain (the base defence used to re-target such units at an intruder, which would make them drive and spin down); `Uses::spins_kept` counts the dropped orders. (2) **Defences:** the brain orders the cheapest buildable armed gattling building type, up to two (`DEFENCES_WANTED`), once the house keeps its harvesters, holds twice the price, can place it and gave no other building or placement order in that think (a tech-climb building aside, which it replaces; never instead of a superweapon building or a psychic sensor); `Uses::gattling_defences` counts the orders. (3) **Armed by slots:** a gattling type whose weapons are only its stage slots (no primary weapon) is now armed for the army role, the jumpjet search and the ferry test (`scripted::armed`), so the production round-robin builds gattling units and the waves use them. The skirmish lane prints `gattling defences` and `spins kept` per house and a `gattling:` line (house-ticks with a gattling at its top stage and the gattling types each house had). Tests `crates/deterministic/ai/tests/gattling.rs` and the tally test `counts_gattling_types_and_top_stage_ticks`.
- **Gas clouds (S-0613, M7a; AI game format 18, unchanged).** No new decision: a gas weapon is an ordinary weapon, and a virus infantry type is an armed army type the brain builds and sends like any other. The AI lane counts per house its virus units, and in all gas hits, clouds and the most particles at once (`skirmish_lane::Tally`).
- **Absorbing buildings (S-0605, M7a; AI game format 20 since step 1g).** `glasnost_ai::absorber` (a new file; `Special::absorbers` is called from `Special::think` after mind control). The absorber is built by the plan's power role (a power plant in the stock data). Every idle, selectable, controlled enemy infantry unit is sent in (its controller is then free for another victim; its power goes to the house); own idle infantry at home (within 16 cells) are sent while the house is short of power, or beyond the 4 it keeps, cheapest first; engineers, spies, mind controllers and units already waiting in line or in contact are kept out. At most 3 per think, each to the nearest own infantry absorber with room (capacity less passengers and entrants on their way), by the *enter* click. Since step 1g (S-0605 revision 2, claim 17: a contact outlives *enter*) a unit of the house's own that holds an absorber's contact with neither *enter* current nor queued gets the *enter* click on that building again before the others, once per cell it stands on (`absorber::stalled`, the order memory's `Memory::pending`); the new order ends the stale contact (S-0213 table T3 (b)). `Uses::absorbs` counts the orders; the AI lane prints them, the infantry that went in, releases beside a standing building, the most passengers at once and the absorbing buildings per house (`skirmish_lane::Tally`, test `counts_absorber_entries_and_releases`). Tests: `crates/deterministic/ai/tests/absorber.rs`.
- **Lane diagnostics.** `GLASNOST_AI_DUMP=t1,t2,...` prints every object of the player houses (type, cell, behaviour, destination, target, power state and production items) at those ticks, and `GLASNOST_AI_WATCH=id,from,to` prints one object every tick in a range (with `GLASNOST_AI_QUICK=1` to skip the replay and save checks).

- **Spies (S-0510).** Every idle spy is sent to the nearest seen enemy building with an infiltration effect, ranked by what a person wants (stolen technology, a factory flag it lacks, money, power, a superweapon, radar), never one another spy of the house is bound for. A house with no spy and none in its line that sees such a building orders the cheapest spy type its sidebar offers, once, under the same credit reserve as engineers.

## Map triggers (M5b)

`glasnost_sim::triggers` runs the map triggers of S-0011 in a skirmish; the converter
(`crates/convert/src/map/triggers.rs`) resolves the map's `[Triggers]`, `[Events]`, `[Actions]`,
`[Tags]`, `[CellTags]` and `[VariableNames]` into registration-indexed definitions
(`glasnost_map::triggers`), which the world keeps, so a save carries them. Tags are created at the
cell tags, at pre-placed objects and at the end of loading (with their draws, claim 34), polled in
row 1 of the tick, raised by their owner house every eighth tick, and deleted at the end of the
tick when a one-time tag fired. Covered events: any event, elapsed time, random delay, game time,
variable set and clear, type exists and does not exist, and the code-matched evaluation with its
latches for raises with a code. Covered actions: enable and disable, set and clear variable, the
mission timer (`TriggerState::mission`), reveal around a waypoint, and the presentation-only
actions as `Effect::Trigger` (the audio crate plays sound and sound-at-waypoint by name).
Interim rules and gaps:

- Carrier raises: `triggers::cell_entered` (S-0011 claim 18; called at the arrival of S-0207 and
  S-0208 claim 22) and `triggers::destruction_raises` (S-0011 claim 19 with S-0312 claim 29's
  groups; called in the killing hit before death debris). The bridge-destroyed raise waits for
  bridge destruction.
- Sound at a waypoint plays the sound once at the cell's centre; giving a building or terrain object
  on the cell the sound as its ambient sound (claim 32) waits for ambient sounds in the audio crate.
  Text messages, speech and radar events reach the presentation as effects but are not shown yet.
- An owner country is matched against the countries' type names only; our rules data has no country
  name field yet.
- Type exists and does not exist look through buildings, vehicles, infantry and aircraft in that
  order and take the last exact match (claim 21 leaves the order open).
- Not implemented yet (recognised, no effect, a note): change owner, destroy attached objects,
  explosion at a waypoint, make a house cheer, play an animation and the drop-zone animation
  (claims 24, 27 to 29 and 36), and the hand-over actions of claim 30 (teams, crates and
  preferred targets). The lightning storm and the other multiplayer actions are S-0524's (see
  *Simulation (M6b: multiplayer triggers)*). The stock skirmish maps in use need only elapsed time and
  sound at a waypoint.

## Audio (M5b)

`glasnost-audio` turns what the game reports into sound, following S-0701 (sound selection, revision 3). It never feeds back into the simulation, and nothing in it is simulation state. The claim 29 test checks that a game's state hash is the same with and without audio.

- **Samples (`bank`).** `SoundBank` maps names (case-insensitive) to samples and decodes each one on first use (`clip`), caching the result. A name that does not decode is remembered as a failure. Sources: IDX/BAG banks (`add_idx`), AUD and plain PCM WAVE files (`add_file`, with WAVE read by `glasnost_fmt::pcm::Pcm::from_wav`), and decoded PCM (`add_pcm`). `bank::discover(&Vfs)` builds the one sample bank of S-0701 claim 34 from `AUDIO.IDX` and `AUDIO.BAG`, each found through the game's file lookup (S-0005), so either may be an archive member or a loose file. It returns a `DiscoverReport` (index found, index without a readable data file, sample count). Sound entries play only bank samples.
- **Sound entries (`catalog`).** `SoundCatalog::from_ini` reads the sound database (S-0701 claims 1 to 3): samples, control and placement words (the last of `shroud` and `unshroud` applies), levels (F1, and with `quirks.full_volume_16383` off an entry of 100 or more is at full), priority, limit, range, delay, pitch shift and attack/decay. The install reads the sound and speech databases (S-0005 Import mapping) into `Install::sounds` and `Install::speech`. The client builds the catalogs at each game start, under that game's quirk switches.
- **Announcement entries (`speech`).** `SpeechCatalog::from_ini` reads the speech database (claim 26). `Announcer` keeps one line playing at a time and queues the others by priority (claim 27). Each announcement requests a fixed entry (`Announcement::entry`, S-0701 Import mapping), and the sample is chosen by the local house's voice set. Announcement samples are separate files, the eight-character name plus `.wav` (`speech::file_name`, claim 34), never bank samples. The client's start job reads the local voice set's files (`SpeechCatalog::files`) through the file lookup and hands them to `Audio::set_voices`. Announcements skip the master level and the sound volume option (claim 35); our engine has no options screen, so the voice option stays at full.
- **Names by role (`names`).** `SoundNames::from_ruleset` reads the sound keys the specs name from the imported rules: the per-type keys (S-0701 Import mapping, S-0415, S-0107, S-0210, S-0207, S-0214), the weapon reports, the animations' start sounds (S-0109 claim 13) and the game-wide keys (S-0408, S-0410 claim 3, S-0416 claim 4, S-0701). Lists follow S-0003 claim 12 and `quirks.list_items_untrimmed`.
- **Mixer (`mixer`).** `Mixer` is integer only and mixes interleaved stereo at the output rate. It provides:
  - placement on screen in half-pixels (F2), levels and truncation (F3), and attenuation and balance (F4);
  - 16 channels and a pool of 300 instances, of which 200 may follow an object; the per-entry limit (claim 8) and the victim choice (claim 9);
  - the per-instance draws in their order: pitch, shift, pre-delay, attack/body/decay, the startup draw, the shuffle (claims 10 to 14);
  - loops and the dormant restart (claim 15);
  - a separate announcement voice;
  - `render`, which tests call offline with no device.
- **Outputs (`output`, `web`, `pipe`).** `Output::pump` is called once per frame and pushes enough mixed audio to stay about 0.1 s ahead.
  - **wasm32:** `web::WebOutput` schedules 1024-frame AudioBuffers on an AudioContext. The context starts suspended and is resumed by the first pointer, key or touch gesture. `Audio::resume` can also be called from an input handler.
  - **Native:** the `native-output` feature (off by default) pipes 16-bit stereo to `pw-cat`, `pacat` or `aplay`. This is a stopgap: an in-process device crate (cpal) could not be fetched offline when M5b was written, and the default build needs no system audio headers.
  - **Otherwise:** `NullOutput`, which discards voices, is used and its state names the reason.
- **Events (`Audio`).** The game client owns one `Audio` (`Audio::platform()` picks the output). At each game start it calls `set_quirks`, `set_catalog`, `set_speech`, `set_bank`, `set_voices`, `set_names` and `start(world, local_house)`. Then it calls:
  - after each tick, `tick(world, effects, unsync)`. This plays:
    - the hit, shot, death and animation effects (claims 20 to 22), and the game-wide building damage and death sounds for building types with none of their own (claim 37);
    - the response slots (claim 16);
    - the movement, turret, unit-ready, building and promotion sounds (claim 23), and the promotion announcement (claim 36);
    - the low-funds, low-power, base-attack and unit-lost announcements (claim 28, F7).
  - after input:
    - `selected` for the first object each input selects (claim 17);
    - `ordered` for a click that gave orders (claim 18, F6). The repair vehicle (`FV`) repairing plays the repair response; an attack with a secondary weapon by a non-elite object plays its secondary-weapon response;
    - `key` for the guard, stop and scatter commands (claim 19);
    - `cue(UiCue)` for the interface sounds (claim 24, S-0410 claim 3, S-0416 claim 4) and the building placement sound. Under claim 24 the start, queue, resume, hold and cancel clicks play the build click with their announcement (start and resume request the training entry for infantry and the building entry for any other kind; a queued copy on an already active line requests no announcement). A busy production line gives only the unable-to-comply announcement, and a refused placement click only the cannot-deploy-here announcement. A page request already at the strip's end plays the refusal sound, an accepted page move the tab sound, and nothing plays while the icons fit (S-0411 claim 18).
  - every frame, `set_view` (the view's origin and size, with S-0101's projection) and `frame(now_ms, unsync)`, which runs the audio passes and pumps the output.

  Game-side choices and the mixer's per-instance draws all take raw values from the client's one unsynchronised stream (S-0202 claim 11, S-0701 claim 30). `frame` and `service` lend that stream to the mixer for the pass and take it back. `now_ms` is host time; S-0701 revision 14 claim 30 (answering Q-0671) specifies the deterministic logical schedule that replaces it, which is not built yet (row below).
- **Not modelled yet.**
  - Claim 22's animation stop sound: the simulation reports no animation end.
  - Revision 15's Import mapping for `Report`, `StartSound` and `StopSound` per import layer (an unknown name keeps an earlier layer's reference, and the `Report` alias is read only while no start reference is held) is not built: `SoundNames` reads the merged section, where the last layer's value wins and an unknown name resolves to no entry at play time. The 127-character cut is the common text limit.
  - Claim 25's lightning sounds: there is no weather storm.
  - Enslaved and deactivated objects do not exist yet, so the normal response lists always apply (claim 17).
  - Sounds the simulation does not report yet: crushing (S-0210), chrono departure and arrival (S-0207), entering and leaving water, idle comments and undeploying (S-0107).
  - Claim 24's refusals that need a signal from the simulation: a production request refused as it is applied (full queue or build limit, S-0404), and a place order that fails as it is applied. The route-mode refusal (S-0213 claim 16) is absent too, because the client has no route mode yet.
  - Claim 35's voice volume option: there is no options screen, so it is at full.
  - Claim 36's defeat, victory and player-defeated announcements, and the play-speech trigger action as an interrupt. Alliance announcements are not raised either; S-0414 revision 12 (claims 5 and 13) leaves their selection and entry bindings unspecified, apart from the break announcement's condition and the post-defeat muting. Structure-sold announcements are not raised either.
- **Viewer.** The asset viewer's `Player` (`preview.rs`) is unchanged and still plays previews natively through a command-line player.

S-0701 answers these earlier interim rules:

- Q-0260, what a sound entry means;
- Q-0262, mixing;
- Q-0264, the weapon report, death and damage sounds;
- Q-0265, the responses of their own kind;
- Q-0266, the sidebar sounds;
- Q-0261, which announcement plays for which event;
- Q-0263, which files hold the samples (claim 34), except for which copy of a name listed twice wins;
- Q-0332, the building damage and death sounds (claim 37);
- Q-0325, the announcement entries, files and level (claim 36, Import mapping, claims 34 and 35);
- Q-0338, the repair-response vehicle (Import mapping, revision 3);
- Q-0326, the primary, elite, undeploy and death response keys (Import mapping, revision 5);
- Q-0328, the responder and selection order (claim 18);
- Q-0330, the initial game-wide sound references and speak delay (claim 38);
- Q-0334, the delay and pitch-shift token forms (claim 2);
- Q-0335, selecting another house's object (claim 17);
- Q-0336, key-command sounds (claim 19);
- Q-0337, the turret rotation sound's end (claim 23);
- Q-0340, the training and building announcements (claim 28).

Provisional rules of the audio, each tied to a request:

| Where | Interim rule | Request |
|---|---|---|
| `bank::SoundBank::add_idx` | when the index lists a name twice, the first copy wins | Q-0263 |
| `mixer` | a delayed instance plays the immediate mode's partition, keeps one body sample across loops, takes no startup draw and has no waits | Q-0329 |
| `mixer` | the crowded master level reaches its target at once (S-0701 F3 gives the settled target only; the transition ramp is outside its coverage) | Q-0333 |
| `Audio::frame`, `Mixer::service` | audio passes still run on host time (`now_ms`, a pass when more than 33 ms have passed) and samples complete when the device finishes them, so the per-instance draws' place in the unsynchronised stream can vary with host timing. S-0701 revision 14 claim 30 answers Q-0671 with the engine's schedule, which is not built yet: logical audio time advancing by *P(s)* (8 ms at speed 0, 16s ms otherwise) at each cycle end with a pass when *C* − *L* > 33 ms; logical sample durations max(1, ⌈100000 × *N* / (*f* × *p*)⌉) ms completing at the first pass at or past the deadline; waits expiring at the first pass past *C₀* + *d*; game-side choices before the pass's per-instance choices, in request-number order. The scenario stream and game rules are unaffected (claim 29) | none (S-0701 claim 30, not built) |

S-0701 claims 21 and 32 answer Q-0327: a weapon report plays entry *r* mod *n* of the weapon's list, *r* the firer's retained report value (`Object::report_value`), carried on `Effect::Shot::report`, with no draw. Its row is retired. Q-0329 stays partial (claim 2 and E3 defer the delayed sequence), Q-0333's ramp is outside F3, and S-0005 claim 10a does not say which copy of a name listed twice in the one index wins (Q-0263), so those rows stay.

## Game client (M5b)

A person plays a skirmish against our AI from the main menu to the end screen, natively (`cargo run -p glasnost-platform-native -- --game --game-dir DIR`) and on the web (`?mode=game`, docs/web.md). The interface is our own code, `glasnost-ui` (no egui), drawn by `glasnost_render::batch2d` within the WebGL2 core.

- **Screens (`glasnost_ui::shell`).** Main menu, then skirmish setup (S-0412), then the game, then the end screen (S-0414), then back to the menu. The setup offers every multiplayer map with at least two start positions (S-0009 claim 23, `install::start_count`). Maps are known by their position in the list and, in the game, by a content hash (`session::content_hash`), never by a stock name. Lobby colours are the colour slots of S-0103 claim 10.
- **Modules (`crates/app/src/game`).**
  - `install` reads what the menus need from the mounted install: the base rules (setup values, S-0412 Import mapping), the maps and their start counts, the sound names, the sound and speech databases and the sample bank.
  - `play` is the running game without drawing: selection, tools (select, place, sell, repair, power), sidebar clicks (`econ::sidebar::click`), status messages and the end screen. Every order goes through `glasnost_ai::game::Game::issue`, so it is in the replay.
  - `session` is a game on a loaded map: camera (S-0101 limits), the live scene, radar, gestures, and the HUD's draw list.
  - `paint` turns draw lists into batch quads, and draws them on the CPU for tests.
  - `client` holds the screens, jobs, pacing, sound and input, without a window.
  - `window` runs winit and wgpu. The battlefield is drawn on the GPU (`map_gpu::MapRenderer` into a target the batch shows through `Batch2d::set_image_view`); if that fails, it falls back to the CPU compose.
- **Pacing.** `mapview::live::Pacer` gives one tick per drawn frame at most, measured from each cycle's start (S-0201 claims 2 to 4). Nothing ticks while the options menu is open (claim 8) or the window is out of focus (claim 9). The speed comes from the setup and the options menu.
- **Sound.** `Audio` is created at the first game start and resumed on the first gesture. At each game start the client sets it up from the install's sound and speech databases, bank and names, under the game's quirk switches (S-0701). After each tick it calls `tick`. After input it calls `selected`, `ordered`, `key` and `cue`, with what `play` collected in `selected`, `acks`, `keys` and `cues`. Each frame it calls `set_view` and `frame`. Game-side choices use the unsynchronised stream seeded with the game's seed (S-0202 claim 8).
- **Input.** Clicks go through `glasnost_sim::targeting` (S-0214). A left release selects or orders (claim 26), a left drag is the band box, a right release clears the selection or ends a sidebar or placement mode (claims 26 and 28), and a right drag past F8 (8 pixels on either axis, `band::RightDrag`) scrolls the view (S-0101 claims 16 and 17) and is released with no effect (claim 27). The key commands are S-0213 table T2. Box selection, scrolling and key bindings follow those claims; the client's remaining interim rules are in the table below.
- **Superweapons (S-0512, S-0513, S-0410).**
  - `glasnost_ui::hud::entries` puts the house's granted superweapons first in the second tab (`hud::SW_TAB`, S-0004 claim 11). They sort by charge time, with ties per `quirks.sidebar_tie_newest_first`; display names are not read, so they compare equal.
  - A superweapon icon is the type's sidebar image cut to 24 bytes plus `.shp`, with `XXICON.SHP` when that file is missing (S-0007 claim 31). It shows the F3 charge stage as the build-clock veil, "On hold" while suspended, "Ready", and "Charging" or "Active" for drain-mode types (S-0512 claim 16). A ready superweapon in its flash window flashes its tab (claim 17).
  - A left click on an icon that offers targeting arms it (`play::Tool::Superweapon`). The tool is dropped when the record stops offering targeting. A source pick of the local house (`SwEvent::SourceMarker`) arms the warp type by `quirks.superweapon_kind_as_list_position` (S-0513 claim 33).
  - With the tool armed, a left click on the battlefield issues `Order::FireSuperweapon` at the cell and returns to selecting (S-0513 claims 1 and 2). A force shield is offered only over an own or allied building; elsewhere a click sends no order and the aim stays. A non-empty unit selection takes precedence, and a right click cancels. A radar click fires the kinds of S-0410 claim 12 table C1 (`play::Play::radar_aims`, `click_radar`).
  - Effect animations come from the simulation's `Effect::Anim` and are played like any other animation. The teleport source marker takes its creation draws in the simulation on every pick (S-0513 claim 33, Q-0741 part 1); only its picture and sound are the launching viewer's. `mapview::live::Live` plays the `chrono_placement` animation at a `SwEvent::SourceMarker` of the viewer's house, replacing an earlier one, and removes it at `SwEvent::MarkerEnd` (S-0513 claims 33 and 34).
  - Not built: the aim cursor images (S-0214 claim 31 table CS keys them by aim action, and the action words are still interim, Q-0731) and the protection appearance (S-0513 claim 29's brightness phases and F9, S-0102 claim 34's colour masks). The egui map viewer (`mapview::sidebar`) has its own sidebar model without superweapons.
- **Tests.**
  - `game::play` tests: select, move, deploy, build and place, box selection, superweapon arming and firing (`s0512_claim_16_*`, `s0513_claim_1_*`, `s0513_claim_33_*`), and surrender to an end screen, all on the synthetic skirmish.
  - `game::client` tests: menu, setup and the start job on a synthetic install.
  - `game::paint` tests and `crates/render/tests/offscreen_batch2d.rs`: the batch's pixels against the CPU reference, image views included.
  - The local lane: `GLASNOST_GAME_DIR=<install> cargo test -p glasnost-app --test ui_lane -- --ignored --nocapture` drives the client from the menu through a game (deploy, a few hundred ticks, a building ready for placement, surrender) to the end screen. It writes `menu.png`, `setup.png`, `game.png`, `placement.png` and `end.png` to `local-lane/ui/` (git-ignored; never commit them). With a GPU adapter it also draws the game frame through the window's path (`game::GpuDraw`) offscreen, to `game-gpu.png`, and compares it with the CPU frame. It writes the first icon's palette indices as grey levels to `icon-indices.png`, which separates the icon palette (Q-0275) from the decoding. `GLASNOST_UI_MAP=n` picks the n-th offered map and `GLASNOST_UI_TICKS=n` sets the ticks before `game.png`.

Interim rules of the game client, each tied to a request:

| Where | Interim rule | Request |
|---|---|---|
| `session::read_icons`, `register_art` | an icon is frame 0 of its file, index 0 transparent, its canvas centred in the slot and clipped; a darkened icon gets a translucent black veil; the alternate icon is unused (the palette part is answered, see below; frame selection, placement and the darkening mask are S-0411 claim 16's and not yet brought over) | Q-0275 |
| `glasnost_ui::hud::Layout` | our own sidebar layout: the radar, credits and power bar (the meter's three parts follow S-0405 claim 19 and F4), sell, repair and power buttons, four tabs, a grid of icon slots with scroll buttons, and the options button; the build clock is a veil of stage/55; "Ready" and "On hold" labels | Q-0276 |
| `session::update_cursor` | battlefield and sidebar-mode cursors follow S-0214 claims 21 and 22 and the edge-scroll cursor S-0213 claim 24 (`game::cursor`); the parts not yet brought over keep the system pointer: the radar, building-placement hover and route-point editing | Q-0277 |
| `session::update_cursor` | while a superweapon is armed the system pointer stays: S-0214 claim 31's table CS keys the aim images by aim action, and which words name those actions is open | Q-0731 |
| `install::offered_countries` | S-0412 claim 15 applies (indices 0 to 9, the listing flag); the part still open: the resolved display-name condition is not tested, because country display names are not read yet | Q-0281 |

Q-0742 is answered by S-0214 revision 37 (claim 31, table CS: the aim cursor images), S-0512 revision 6 (claim 16: the original's clock overlay and label positions are reference facts; our build-clock veil and label places stay our design under S-0411 claim 8), S-0513 revision 9 (claim 29: the protection brightness envelope and its draw) and S-0102 revision 17 (claim 34 and F9: the protection colour masks). The sidebar part of its row is retired. The cursor part waits for Q-0731's action words. The appearance is not built. Answered requests of the game client: Q-0570 by S-0101 revision 11, claim 8 and F5 (`session::outside_local_area`, `render::projection::local_area_rect`: on each oversized axis, every battlefield pixel beyond that axis's interval of the local-area rectangle is black, over the battlefield image and under the overlays; the bands of both axes form a union and a non-oversized axis is not clipped); Q-0530 by S-0014 revision 3, claims 4 and 6, and S-0213 revision 25, claim 25 (Vertical metrics) and Scenario O21 d (`glasnost_fmt::fnt::Font::parse` rejects the whole font for another identifying sequence, an incomplete header, mapping or record payload, any selector beyond the record count, a record span below 1 + *HB*, any stored width beyond 8*B* or an absent capital-X mapping, checking every selector and record whether used or not; a zero-width mapped X is accepted; `game::status_font::read` then reports one diagnostic naming the file and the reason and status text is omitted, with no partial use and no substitute font; `status_font::draw` keeps the line origins a fixed 19 pixels apart, puts each glyph's row 0 at its line origin and clips each line to its first min(*H*, *P*) rows, so a zero pitch paints nothing, a pitch below *H* cuts the bottom without the original's extra row at *P*, and tall masks may overlap the next line without re-spacing it; the pitch changes no splitting, capacity or lifetime); Q-0501 by S-0014 and S-0213 claim 25 (`glasnost_fmt::fnt`: the `fonT` header, the 65536 16-bit selectors and the one-bit records; `game::status_font`: `GAME.FNT` resolved through the ordinary precedence (S-0005 claim 18a), each 16-bit text unit selecting its glyph without low-byte reduction, a zero selector drawing the generated complement of the mapped capital X with X's width; `glasnost_ui::status`: lines kept as 16-bit units, the zero unit ending the text, carriage returns and line feeds with no advance and no glyph, other control characters drawn with their glyphs; `play::Play::say` measures with the font's widths and `status_font::draw` draws the masks 3 pixels in from the battlefield's top left with 19 pixels between line origins, zero bits leaving the battlefield unchanged and zero-width glyphs painting nothing; without a usable font the session logs a missing-asset diagnostic and status text is omitted. Our own messages, "Game started." and "Nothing to do there.", use our text colour as foreground: no route-refusal notice, whose foreground is the local player's colour definition, is shown yet); Q-0393 by S-0213 claim 25, Line splitting and font (`glasnost_ui::status::split`, `play::Play::status_view`, `session::resize`: the budget is the view width minus 22 at game start and minus 16 after a size change, which clears the lines; each character advances its glyph width plus one pixel, tabs go to 64-pixel stops, at most 111 UTF-16 units per line, word breaks after the last character of value 32 or less, explicit breaks, continuation control characters skipped, an empty text one blank line; every line has its own place and lifetime; an unfittable remainder is left out with a logged warning); Q-0392 by S-0214 claims 24 and 25 and F7 (`session::box_candidates`, `band::admit`, `band::mobile_point_in`, `band::building_point_in`, `live::Live::drawn_mobiles`: the in-play buildings on the map whose type is not invisible in the game with their point in the view, 0 to *W* and 0 to *H* inclusive, in creation order, then the drawn mobile objects with their point from −32 to *W* + 32 and −32 to *H* + 32, in drawing order, cut at 500; every mobile object is drawn in the ground layer, the upper layers not being modelled (S-0102 claim 7), and transient drawing displacements are ignored (S-0214 claim 19)); Q-0391 by S-0213 claim 23 and S-0201 claim 14 (`client::key`, `scroll::arrow_event_accepted`, `orders::blocked`: a view-arrow press or release made while orders are blocked is discarded under `quirks.blocked_arrow_events`, so an accepted arrow keeps scrolling and a newly pressed one adds nothing until a fresh press after the block; the window drops auto-repeated key-downs, so holding a key is never a new press); Q-0281 in part by S-0412 claim 15 (`install::offered_countries`, `glasnost_ui::setup`: the offered countries by index and listing flag, the map's rows with hidden computer slots beyond them, no team and four teams); Q-0276 in part by S-0405 claim 19 and F4 (`power::meter_base`, `power::meter`, `hud::power_meter`: the power bar's three parts; the rest of the layout keeps the interim row); Q-0339 by S-0411 claim 18 (`play::Play::scroll_icons`: a page move, the end sound at the end, no effect when the icons fit, one silent row per wheel step); Q-0285 by S-0414 claims 19 to 23 (`econ::stats`: the result screen's own built total, kills, losses and running score, the displayed score fixed once after play stops with one scenario-stream draw per undefeated listed house, and `Play::clock`'s active 16-ms clock for the duration), Q-0283 by S-0411 claim 11 (`glasnost_ui::hud::tab_lit`: a tab flash keeps the tick that started it, with the initial extra period and `quirks.tab_flash_initial_extra_period`; the lit look is our own design), Q-0279 by S-0101 claims 12 to 15 (`game::scroll`: edge scrolling at the outermost client pixel with F9's direction, momentum and magnitude table scaled by the imported `ScrollMultiplier`, 21 px per held arrow, both once per unfrozen cycle; the scroll index is the default 3, since our engine has no settings file yet; an order block turns edge scrolling off, S-0201 claim 14, and no timed freeze is modelled), and Q-0280 by S-0103 claims 11 and 12 (`install::slot_swatches`: the fixed slot colours after the 16-bit step).

Known limits:

- Icons are drawn through the icon palette of S-0103 claim 13 (`palette::icon_palette_565`: `CAMEO.PAL` through claim 1's conversion, the middle shade row, the 16-bit step; black with an importer warning when the file is missing, our engine's departure). The file is read with each game's files, not once at program start, so a side's archives that hold a different copy would change it between games (claim 13's session lifetime is not modelled).
- With no radar building, the radar box says "No radar" (S-0410).
- The seed of a game comes from the host clock when Start is clicked.
- Natively, the install is read when the window opens and the game is loaded synchronously after the frame that shows "Loading". The window does not respond while it loads (a few seconds in a debug build).

### Lead visual review (M6c, row 4)

The debug pixel trace answers "what is drawn here". `Scene::items_at` lists every item with a non-transparent pixel at a world pixel; `Live::trace_point` and `Session::trace_at` print, for each, its scene place, group, paint, image size, the object (kind, type, owner, cell, rocket phase; for a map-placed object its table entry) and the draw path (object body or part, object shadow, effect or projectile placeholder, static draw). The lanes use it: `GLASNOST_TRACE="x,y;x,y"` and `GLASNOST_TRACE_BOX="x0,y0,x1,y1,step"` (screen points of each saved shot) in `m6a_lane`, `GLASNOST_BLANK="item,item"` there for a second shot without those items' images (`<name>-blank.png`), and `GLASNOST_MAP_TRACE_BOX` and `GLASNOST_MAP_BLANK` in `map_lane`. `GLASNOST_LANE_OUT=<folder>` names the folder under `local-lane/` for `m6a_lane` and `anim_lane`; `GLASNOST_RADAR_TYPE=<type,type>` makes `m6a_lane` shoot those building types (a single name: `yuri-radar.png`). The shots below are in `local-lane/m6c/` (git-ignored).

| Item | What it was | Fix | New shot |
|---|---|---|---|

The launcher's own voxel body holds a loaded rocket, and the viewer always drew the main body: the alternative-body rule of S-0105 claim 12 (the type's no-spawn alternative body while none of its spawned aircraft is docked) was not drawn. | `Mobile::alternative` from `spawn::any_docked` (new, read-only: a slot that is docked or reloading, S-0105 Terms); `vehicle_voxels` draws the alternative body for a type with the option and an alternative body file, keyed apart in the cache, the shadow still the main body's (claim 29). Tests `alternative_body_replaces_the_main_body_without_a_docked_spawn` (app) and `any_docked_follows_the_slots` (sim). | `m6c/v3-rocket-in-flight-2.png` (and `-1`, `-3`), `m6c/v3-rocket-anims.png` |

## Tests

```sh
cargo test --workspace          # unit tests, render tests, dependency rule
cargo xtask check               # the checks above
```

`crates/render/tests/offscreen_quad.rs` renders the palette quad on a real or software GPU and compares every pixel with the CPU palette lookup. Without an adapter it skips, unless `GLASNOST_REQUIRE_GPU=1` is set (CI sets it and installs lavapipe). `WGPU_BACKEND=gl` runs it on the native GL backend. `crates/render/tests/webgl2_shaders.rs` translates every shader to GLSL ES 3.00, so constructs WebGL2 can't express fail without a browser.

The browser lane is described in [web.md](web.md).

## CI

`.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs these jobs:

- rustfmt;
- clippy, native and wasm32;
- tests, with lavapipe and `GLASNOST_REQUIRE_GPU=1`;
- `cargo xtask check`;
- cargo-deny licence check against `deny.toml` (GPL-3.0-or-later compatible licences only);
- the determinism gate (`cargo xtask gate`: native release and wasm32 under node);
- the release web build;
- the browser lanes:
  - WebGL2 on Chromium, Firefox and WebKit (WebKit non-blocking until it has run green);
  - optional WebGPU on Chromium.

CI uses synthetic data only.
